# Roadmap: analytic toric geometry, Part II: arithmetic toroidal compactifications

**Summary.** This roadmap extends [AnalyticToricGeometry](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/tree/main/AnalyticToricGeometry) from its finite complex toric anchor to arithmetic toroidal geometry. Its targets include relative coefficient charts, mixed boundary data, characteristic-zero gluing and descent, compatible refinements and Hecke maps, polarized degeneration, integral PEL models and Hilbert/modular specializations.

The full mathematical specification is here; [Suggested.lean](../suggested/ShimuraCompactifications.lean) records representative signatures on existing carriers.

## Scope and ownership

L0–L5 of AnalyticToricGeometry supply the common lattice/cone, finite complex chart, finite analytic gluing, boundary and toric-map work. C0 changes coefficients, descends torsor algebras and adds arithmetic fan systems. C2 and C3 add controlled arithmetic quotients and descent. C4 imports local Raynaud theory from R11.3 and owns its relative cusp extension. The nonarchimedean Binda–Kato–Vezzani Part II imports the single C0 arbitrary-ring scheme result and starts its new work at formal, adic and perfectoid geometry.

ShimuraCompactifications and AutomorphicBundles are mutually compatible suppliers. Generic algebraic/formal foundations, abelian schemes, PEL, Hilbert, Néron and finite-flat group inputs come from their existing owners. C4 supplies the semiabelian boundary Hasse comparison used by T0. R07.2 remains the generic Hasse/LF/Hodge–Tate owner. The AG2.4 cohomology theorem consumes C5’s good-boundary geometry and is not an upstream input.

The construction separates early mixed-group input in C1 from its later cusp labels, and early C5 toroidal input from B5 constant terms and later C5 minimal models. The local R11.3 input must precede its relative C4 extension; the precise missing interface is stated below.

## Conventions

- R is a commutative coefficient ring, possibly nonreduced or zero, unless a target explicitly assumes a normal domain. R[P] means the existing additive monoid algebra, with finite coefficient support and monomials eₚ.
- A face submonoid F⊆P satisfies a+b∈F iff a∈F and b∈F. Face coefficient restriction becomes multiplicative because of this condition. It does not reduce R.
- The closed stratum ideal generated by off-face monomials differs from the union-of-boundary ideal. In a regular chart the latter is (x₁⋯xᵣ); the intersection of specified branches is cut out by the selected coordinate ideal.
- Use the existing closed rational cones and their relative interiors when comparing Lan’s open-cone notation. Completeness concerns support in the rational closure of the positivity cone. Local finiteness is on compact subsets of the open positivity domain, not at the origin.
- Arithmetic cone collections have finite orbit count under the effective integral action; they need not contain finitely many cones. Keep the ineffective stabilizer kernel separate.
- Normal crossings is a local coordinate property. Global simple/no-self-intersection statements impose the extra face-compatibility condition. Non-neat stack and coarse-space conclusions are stated separately.
- Formal completion, algebraic étale gluing and analytic/adic realization use their actual supplier carriers and comparison maps. Neither arbitrary tensor/completion interchange nor generic-to-special normality transfer is implicit.
- For a Hilbert cusp, f*=f⁻¹d⁻¹ and X=cbb′. Period u, base parameter q, the trace pairing, the cyclotomic phase and the full unit/cusp action retain their specified carriers.
- Good-prime ordinary PEL geometry, higher-level finite normalization and projective normalized blow-up are distinct constructions. Hilbert discriminant-prime geometry uses H2 and the direct C6 gluing/minimal targets.
- A test in this reader is a planned verification target. Elaboration of an example with a proof placeholder checks its statement, not its mathematical proof. Source statements and proof discussions are paraphrases, with theorem/section/page locators.

Read C1’s group and effective-action input before C0’s arithmetic fan compatibility; then use the chart, quotient and degeneration chains appropriate to the target. The C6 model chain is C0/C4 → formal gluing/H2 → toroidal properness → Hodge generation → section algebra/minimal contraction → boundary functions and cusp ideals. It does not pass through a good-prime C5 theorem at a ramified prime.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c0"></a>
## Layer C0: Admissible fans and relative torus embeddings

The starting point is the existing finite complex toric roadmap. Keep its lattice, cone, dual monoid and finite-fan vocabulary. The new coefficient algebra is uniform in a commutative ring R, and its relative version uses the character-line grading of an actual torus torsor. Scheme-theoretic strata retain every coefficient nilpotent. Arithmetic fans may have infinitely many cones; their finiteness lies in cone orbits and local finiteness on the open positivity domain. Neither finiteness condition is the finite-cone field of the existing Fan.

The layer ends with compatible refinements, relative fan morphisms and integral chart comparisons. Completion is a separate product-adic comparison, imported through formal-scheme infrastructure. A changed-lattice map with its pullback fan is finite; a subsequent subdivision can be proper without being finite.

**Milestones.**

- [Relative torus embedding](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-relative-torus-embedding)
- [Toric stratum](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-relative-stratum-quotient)
- [Admissible cone decomposition](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-arithmetic-admissible-fan)
- [Smooth projective refinement](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-smooth-projective-refinement)
- [Integral toric chart](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-arbitrary-ring-toric-charts)

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-face-projection"></a>
### C0.1 The integral face projection

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/face-projection -->

Construct the R-algebra homomorphism pi_(R,F): R[P] to R[F] that restricts a finite coefficient family to F. On the monomial r e_p it is r e_(p in F) when p belongs to F and zero otherwise. Its underlying additive map is the pinned coefficient restriction comapDomain along the inclusion F to P. In particular this is not an augmentation, reduction of R, or an arbitrary linear map renamed a stratum restriction. (Sources: [LAN-2021](#lan-2021) 6.1.2.1–6.1.2.6, especially the homogeneous quotient in Lemma 6.1.2.6, printed pp. 443–445.)

R is a commutative ring, including the zero ring; P is an additive commutative monoid.

F is an existing additive submonoid of P with the explicit face condition: for all a,b in P, a+b belongs to F if and only if both a and b belong to F. No replacement face or monoid carrier is introduced.

Use target name `AddMonoidAlgebra.faceProjection` in `TauCeti/Algebra/MonoidAlgebra/FaceProjection`, namespace `AddMonoidAlgebra`.

Reuse the existing additive monoid-algebra carrier and its coefficient restriction along the injective subtype map. The additive operation is already in the pinned library.

For multiplication, reduce by finite bilinearity to e_a e_b=e_(a+b). The face condition says exactly that the product survives precisely when both factors survive. The surviving subtype sum is the original sum. This proves multiplicativity without cancellation, reducedness or a domain hypothesis.

Zero belongs to F, so e_0 maps to e_0 and every scalar is preserved. Package these laws as an AlgHom. Equivalently apply the pinned AddMonoidAlgebra.lift to the monomial-or-zero multiplicative map; the lift universal property proves agreement with coefficient restriction.

This supplies [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/relative-stratum-quotient](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-relative-stratum-quotient): Realizes the local restriction from a torus embedding to its closed orbit without reducing the base.

This supplies [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/relative-boundary-coordinates](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-relative-boundary-coordinates): Kills the selected coordinate directions in the actual scheme-theoretic boundary ideal.

Expose the following API under the same hypotheses:

- `AddMonoidAlgebra.faceProjection_eq_comapDomain`: For every f in R[P], pi_(R,F)(f) equals comapDomain along the injective inclusion F to P applied to f.
- `AddMonoidAlgebra.faceProjection_single_mem`: For p in F and r in R, pi_(R,F)(r e_p)=r e_(p in F).
- `AddMonoidAlgebra.faceProjection_single_not_mem`: For p outside F and r in R, pi_(R,F)(r e_p)=0.
- `AddMonoidAlgebra.faceProjection_coeff`: For f in R[P] and m in F, the m-coefficient of pi_(R,F)(f) is the underlying m-coefficient of f; promoted to face-projection-coeff.
- `AddMonoidAlgebra.faceProjection_comp_inclusion`: The composite R[F] to R[P] to R[F] is the identity; promoted to face-projection-section.
- `AddMonoidAlgebra.faceProjection_coefficient_change`: For every ring homomorphism R to S, coefficient change commutes with pi; promoted to face-projection-coefficient-change.

**Checks.**

- `AddMonoidAlgebra.faceProjection_zero_test`: The zero element of R[P] maps to zero in R[F].
- `AddMonoidAlgebra.faceProjection_positive_test`: For R=Z/4, P=N and F={0}, pi(e_1)=0.
- `AddMonoidAlgebra.faceProjection_nilpotent_test`: For R=Z/4, P=N and F={0}, the zero-degree coefficient of pi(2 e_0) is 2, hence nonzero. The nonzero nilpotent scalar is retained.
- `AddMonoidAlgebra.faceProjection_laurent_test`: For R=Z, P=Z and F=P, the coefficient in degree -1 of pi(e_(-1)) is 1. Invertible character directions are not discarded.
- For R nonzero and P=N, F={0}, the positive-degree monomial maps to zero but the constant one does not.
- The face assumption cannot be dropped: restriction to the even submonoid of N kills e_1 but retains e_2, although e_1 squared is e_2.

*Needs:* `mathlib:MonoidAlgebra.comapDomain`; `mathlib:AddMonoidAlgebra.lift`; `mathlib:AddMonoidAlgebra.lift_single`.

Attribution: This is the general coefficient-algebra proof behind the source toric-stratum quotient, written in existing monoid-algebra vocabulary. The extension to an arbitrary face submonoid is proved here, not attributed to a separately numbered general theorem in Lan.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-face-projection-coeff"></a>
### C0.2 Coefficients of the face projection

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/face-projection-coeff -->

For f in R[P] and m in F, coeff_m(pi_(R,F)(f))=coeff_m(f). This is the named coefficient API used by the kernel and base-change arguments. (Sources: [LAN-2021](#lan-2021) 6.1.2.6, homogeneous components of the quotient, printed pp. 443–445.)

R is a commutative ring, including the zero ring; P is an additive commutative monoid.

F is an existing additive submonoid of P with the explicit face condition: for all a,b in P, a+b belongs to F if and only if both a and b belong to F. No replacement face or monoid carrier is introduced.

Use target name `AddMonoidAlgebra.faceProjection_coeff` in `TauCeti/Algebra/MonoidAlgebra/FaceProjection`, namespace `AddMonoidAlgebra`.

Evaluate the coefficient restriction used in face-projection at the subtype element m. Injectivity of the subtype inclusion makes this precisely the coefficient at its underlying element. No summation of distinct degrees occurs.

**Checks.**

- The formula also holds when R is zero or when the coefficient is nilpotent.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/face-projection](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-face-projection); `mathlib:MonoidAlgebra.comapDomain`.

Attribution: Makes the degreewise content of the source quotient explicit.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-face-projection-section"></a>
### C0.3 A section of the face projection

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/face-projection-section -->

Let i_F:R[F] to R[P] be the existing monoid-algebra map induced by the subtype inclusion. Then pi_(R,F) composed with i_F is the identity R-algebra homomorphism. In particular pi_(R,F) is surjective. (Sources: [LAN-2021](#lan-2021) 6.1.2.6, quotient algebra on the orthogonal characters, printed pp. 443–445.)

R is a commutative ring, including the zero ring; P is an additive commutative monoid.

F is an existing additive submonoid of P with the explicit face condition: for all a,b in P, a+b belongs to F if and only if both a and b belong to F. No replacement face or monoid carrier is introduced.

Use target name `AddMonoidAlgebra.faceProjection_comp_inclusion` in `TauCeti/Algebra/MonoidAlgebra/FaceProjection`, namespace `AddMonoidAlgebra`.

Use the existing mapDomainAlgHom for the inclusion. On a coefficient monomial r e_m, the inclusion gives the same monomial with its underlying P-degree.

Apply face-projection-coeff and coefficient extensionality. This proves the identity on arbitrary finite sums; it supplies an actual right inverse, not only an existence assertion. Surjectivity is the immediate elementwise consequence.

**Checks.**

- An arbitrary finite F-supported polynomial, not just the unit, is recovered.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/face-projection-coeff](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-face-projection-coeff); `mathlib:MonoidAlgebra.mapDomainAlgHom`.

Attribution: The retained homogeneous summands provide the explicit section in a trivialized chart.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-face-projection-kernel"></a>
### C0.4 The monomial ideal of the face complement

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/face-projection-kernel -->

The kernel of pi_(R,F) is the ideal J_F generated by e_p for p outside F. Equivalently, a polynomial lies in J_F exactly when all its F-coefficients vanish. The statement is about the actual Ideal.span in R[P], not the radical of this ideal. (Sources: [LAN-2021](#lan-2021) 6.1.2.6, displayed homogeneous ideal, printed pp. 443–445.)

R is a commutative ring, including the zero ring; P is an additive commutative monoid.

F is an existing additive submonoid of P with the explicit face condition: for all a,b in P, a+b belongs to F if and only if both a and b belong to F. No replacement face or monoid carrier is introduced.

Use target name `AddMonoidAlgebra.ker_faceProjection` in `TauCeti/Algebra/MonoidAlgebra/FaceProjection`, namespace `AddMonoidAlgebra`.

The coefficient formula identifies the kernel with the polynomials whose retained coefficients vanish.

Each generator e_p outside F is killed. Therefore its ideal span lies in the kernel.

Conversely expand an element of the kernel as its finite monomial sum. Every nonzero term has degree outside F and is a scalar multiple of one of the indicated generators. Thus it belongs to that ideal span. This proof also establishes the coefficient characterization.

**Checks.**

- Over Z/4, J_{0} in (Z/4)[N] is the positive-degree ideal; it does not contain 2 e_0.
- For F=P, the generating set is empty and the kernel is the zero ideal.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/face-projection-coeff](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-face-projection-coeff).

Attribution: Provides a coefficient-level proof and retains nilpotent coefficients under arbitrary base change.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-face-quotient"></a>
### C0.5 The integral stratum quotient algebra

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/face-quotient -->

Construct the canonical R-algebra equivalence R[P]/J_F with R[F], where J_F is the ideal span of the off-face monomials from face-projection-kernel. It takes the class of f to pi_(R,F)(f). Use the existing ideal-quotient carrier and the pinned first isomorphism theorem. (Sources: [LAN-2021](#lan-2021) 6.1.2.6, quotient defining the closed stratum, printed pp. 443–445.)

R is a commutative ring, including the zero ring; P is an additive commutative monoid.

F is an existing additive submonoid of P with the explicit face condition: for all a,b in P, a+b belongs to F if and only if both a and b belong to F. No replacement face or monoid carrier is introduced.

Use target name `AddMonoidAlgebra.faceQuotientEquiv` in `TauCeti/Algebra/MonoidAlgebra/FaceProjection`, namespace `AddMonoidAlgebra`.

Apply Ideal.quotientKerAlgEquivOfRightInverse using the explicit inclusion section from face-projection-section.

Transport the quotient along face-projection-kernel to the specified monomial ideal J_F. The resulting equivalence has its quotient-map formula fixed, so it is not an unspecified isomorphism of rings.

This supplies [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/relative-stratum-quotient](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-relative-stratum-quotient): Identifies the affine-local coordinate algebra of the relative closed orbit.

This supplies [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/relative-boundary-coordinates](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-relative-boundary-coordinates): Computes scheme-theoretic boundary intersections after local trivialization.

Expose the following API under the same hypotheses:

- `AddMonoidAlgebra.faceQuotientEquiv_mk`: The equivalence evaluated at the quotient class of f is pi_(R,F)(f).
- `AddMonoidAlgebra.faceQuotientEquiv_symm_single`: The inverse sends r e_m for m in F to the quotient class of r e_m in R[P].
- `AddMonoidAlgebra.faceQuotientEquiv_coeff`: The m-coefficient of the image of the class of f equals coeff_m(f), for m in F.
- `AddMonoidAlgebra.faceQuotient_mk_eq_iff`: The quotient classes of f and g are equal exactly when their coefficients agree in every F-degree.

**Checks.**

- `AddMonoidAlgebra.faceQuotient_zero_test`: The class of zero maps to zero.
- `AddMonoidAlgebra.faceQuotient_positive_test`: For R=Z/4, P=N and F={0}, the class of e_1 maps to zero.
- `AddMonoidAlgebra.faceQuotient_nilpotent_test`: For R=Z/4, P=N and F={0}, the image of the class of 2 e_0 has zero-degree coefficient 2, not zero.
- `AddMonoidAlgebra.faceQuotient_laurent_test`: For R=Z, P=Z and F=P, the image of the class of e_(-1) has degree -1 coefficient 1.
- The equivalence preserves the R-algebra structure, including nonzero nilpotents in R.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/face-projection-section](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-face-projection-section); [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/face-projection-kernel](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-face-projection-kernel); `mathlib:Ideal.quotientKerAlgEquivOfRightInverse`.

Attribution: Identifies the specified monomial quotient, using the existing generic first isomorphism theorem instead of replanning it.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-face-projection-coefficient-change"></a>
### C0.6 Coefficient change commutes with face restriction

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/face-projection-coefficient-change -->

For every unital ring homomorphism phi:R to S, the two ring homomorphisms R[P] to S[F] obtained by projecting then changing coefficients, or changing coefficients then projecting, are equal. There is no flatness, injectivity or surjectivity hypothesis on phi. (Sources: [LAN-2021](#lan-2021) 6.1.2.1–6.1.2.6, homogeneous algebra construction, printed pp. 443–445.)

R is a commutative ring, including the zero ring; P is an additive commutative monoid.

F is an existing additive submonoid of P with the explicit face condition: for all a,b in P, a+b belongs to F if and only if both a and b belong to F. No replacement face or monoid carrier is introduced.

S is any commutative ring and phi:R to S is a ring homomorphism.

Use target name `AddMonoidAlgebra.faceProjection_coefficient_change` in `TauCeti/Algebra/MonoidAlgebra/FaceProjection`, namespace `AddMonoidAlgebra`.

The pinned monoid-algebra coefficient map sends r e_p to phi(r) e_p.

Compare the two composites on each coefficient monomial. Membership of p in F is unchanged by phi, and phi(0)=0. Finite-sum or algebra-homomorphism extensionality gives equality.

**Checks.**

- Apply to Z/4 to Z/2 and to identity and composite coefficient maps.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/face-projection-coeff](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-face-projection-coeff); `mathlib:MonoidAlgebra.mapRingHom`.

Attribution: The arbitrary-ring naturality is a direct verification on the source homogeneous construction. It does not assert any tensor/inverse-limit interchange.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-face-kernel-coefficient-change"></a>
### C0.7 The face ideal commutes with coefficient change

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/face-kernel-coefficient-change -->

For phi:R to S, extension of the ideal ker(pi_(R,F)) along the existing coefficient map R[P] to S[P] is ker(pi_(S,F)). Consequently the specified stratum quotient has its expected coefficient-base-change comparison. This is extension of this monomial ideal, not a claim that arbitrary kernel formation commutes with tensoring. (Sources: [LAN-2021](#lan-2021) 6.1.2.6, monomial description of the stratum ideal, printed pp. 443–445.)

R is a commutative ring, including the zero ring; P is an additive commutative monoid.

F is an existing additive submonoid of P with the explicit face condition: for all a,b in P, a+b belongs to F if and only if both a and b belong to F. No replacement face or monoid carrier is introduced.

S and phi are as in face-projection-coefficient-change.

Use target name `AddMonoidAlgebra.ker_faceProjection_map` in `TauCeti/Algebra/MonoidAlgebra/FaceProjection`, namespace `AddMonoidAlgebra`.

Rewrite both kernels using face-projection-kernel.

The image of each generating monomial e_p is the same e_p since phi(1)=1. Extension of an ideal generated by a set is generated by its image. This proves the equality even if phi is nonflat.

For the quotient comparison, apply face-quotient on both bases and use the naturality square. The ordinary monoid-algebra coefficient extension is computed from its free monomial basis; no completion is involved.

**Checks.**

- The nonflat map Z/4 to Z/2 passes the ideal-generator test.
- Keep the distinction from a general ideal kernel: the argument uses the fixed monomial generating set and the explicit split coefficient basis.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/face-projection-kernel](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-face-projection-kernel); [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/face-projection-coefficient-change](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-face-projection-coefficient-change); [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/face-quotient](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-face-quotient); `mathlib:MonoidAlgebra.mapRingHom`.

Attribution: This proves the needed coefficient-change property of the displayed ideal, rather than claiming it for all kernel or completion functors.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-relative-torus-embedding"></a>
### C0.8 Relative torus embeddings from the actual torsor algebra

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/relative-torus-embedding -->

Construct T(sigma)=Spec_Z(A_sigma), where A_sigma is the homogeneous O_Z-subalgebra direct-summing L_m over m in P_sigma inside the actual torsor algebra. Its multiplication and unit are inherited from O_T. The construction is functorial under base change and is equivariant for the given split torus. It extends the unchanged finite-complex toric chart, not its lattice or cone carrier. (Sources: [LAN-2021](#lan-2021) 6.1.2.1, Remark 6.1.2.2, Definition 6.1.2.3, printed pp. 443–444; [FARB-KISIN-WOLFSON-2024](#farb-kisin-wolfson-2024) 3.2.1; Proposition 3.2.7 proof, printed pp. 24–25.)

Z is a scheme, or an algebraic space treated on its actual etale atlas. H is a split torus with finite free character lattice M; T to Z is a right H-torsor in the fppf topology.

Use the common toric lattice/cone vocabulary and its dual additive monoids. For a rational polyhedral salient closed cone sigma in the real cocharacter space, P_sigma consists of the integral characters nonnegative on sigma.

Let L_m be the weight-m subsheaf of the actual pushforward of O_T: right translation by h acts on a local function by multiplication by m(h). Its unit and multiplication maps L_m tensor L_n to L_(m+n) are those of O_T, not arbitrary rigidifications.

Lan's cone convention is relatively open; its nonnegative character monoid agrees with that of our closed cone. Strict positivity, when comparing the source ideal notation, is tested on the relative interior, not on the origin. The zero-cone case is treated separately.

Use target name `TauCeti.Toric.Relative.torusEmbedding` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Toric/Algebraic/Relative`, namespace `TauCeti.Toric.Relative`.

Import the actual graded torsor algebra and its coherent multiplication from the generic torsor/descent owner SF.1. In particular identify L_0 with O_Z and prove that character-line multiplication is an isomorphism; do not select incompatible trivializations.

Nonnegativity is closed under addition and includes zero, so the indicated homogeneous direct sum is a quasi-coherent subalgebra. Use SF.0 relative Spec for this algebra, not a new scheme or gluing carrier.

Trivialize a basis of character lines on a cover of Z. The multiplication-compatible trivialization identifies A_sigma there with O_Z[P_sigma]. Two trivializations differ by character units and give the actual descent cocycle.

Pullback commutes with direct sums and the individual character lines. The cocycle respects multiplication, so relative Spec base change and the torus action descend. No C4 object is a prerequisite: this is the generic construction that C4 instantiates.

This supplies [ShimuraCompactifications:C4](#shimuracompactifications-c4): Supplies the relative torus embeddings used in actual degeneration charts.

This supplies [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/relative-regular-coordinates](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-relative-regular-coordinates): Provides the scheme whose ordinary integral coordinates are identified.

This supplies [ShimuraCompactifications:C5/roadmap/neat-boundary-intersection-smooth](#shimuracompactifications-c5-roadmap-neat-boundary-intersection-smooth): Its coordinate form is pulled to the ordinary good algebraic models.

This supplies `PAPER-FARB-KISIN-WOLFSON-24/089`: Specializes the same generic torus-torsor/character-line supplier over an abelian variety; no second torsor definition.

Expose the following API under the same hypotheses:

- `TauCeti.Toric.Relative.embedding_trivialization`: A multiplication-compatible trivialization of the torsor identifies T(sigma) with Spec of the existing monoid algebra over that open of Z.
- `TauCeti.Toric.Relative.embedding_baseChange`: For Zprime to Z, pullback of T(sigma) is canonically the embedding of the pulled-back torsor, compatibly with identity and composition.
- `TauCeti.Toric.Relative.embedding_torusAction`: The given H-action extends to T(sigma), and the structural morphism to Z is invariant.
- `TauCeti.Toric.Relative.embedding_zeroCone`: For the zero cone, P_sigma=M and T(sigma) is canonically the original torsor T.
- `TauCeti.Toric.Relative.embedding_changeTrivialization`: If local torsor sections satisfy t_beta=t_alpha g_alpha_beta, the weight-m coordinate functions satisfy q_beta,m=m(g_alpha_beta)^(-1) q_alpha,m. These transitions preserve multiplication, boundary ideals and all face maps.
- `TauCeti.Toric.Relative.embedding_homEquiv`: For a Z-scheme Y, maps Y→T(sigma) over Z correspond to maps from the pulled-back graded character algebra A_sigma to O_Y; evaluation on homogeneous characters respects its inherited multiplication.

**Checks.**

- `TauCeti.Toric.Relative.embedding_rankOne_test`: For a trivial G_m-torsor and its positive ray, the embedding is A1_Z with its usual G_m open.
- `TauCeti.Toric.Relative.embedding_zeroCone_test`: The zero-cone embedding is the given torsor, even if that torsor is nontrivial.
- `TauCeti.Toric.Relative.embedding_lineDual_test`: For rank one with weight-one function line L, the positive-ray embedding is Spec_Z Sym(L), the total space of L dual under the convention V(E)=Spec Sym(E dual). Do not replace it by the total space of L.
- `TauCeti.Toric.Relative.embedding_nilpotentBase_test`: For the trivial rank-one torsor over Z/4, the positive-ray coordinate ring is (Z/4)[q], and restriction to q=0 retains the nonzero nilpotent scalar 2.
- On a trivial torsor over Spec R, the construction is the actual affine spectrum of R[P_sigma].
- A nontrivial torsor need not admit a global monomial function of every degree.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-relative-face-open"></a>
### C0.9 Ordinary face charts are relative open subspaces

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/relative-face-open -->

For a face tau of sigma, construct the canonical equivariant open immersion T(tau) to T(sigma). On a multiplication-compatible trivialization it is the monomial localization R[P_sigma] to R[P_tau]; these immersions obey identity and composition and have the expected common-face intersections. This is an ordinary scheme statement, not a morphism between completions at different strata. (Sources: [LAN-2021](#lan-2021) Lemma 6.1.2.4 and Theorem 6.1.2.8(2), printed pp. 443–445.)

Z is a scheme, or an algebraic space treated on its actual etale atlas. H is a split torus with finite free character lattice M; T to Z is a right H-torsor in the fppf topology.

Use the common toric lattice/cone vocabulary and its dual additive monoids. For a rational polyhedral salient closed cone sigma in the real cocharacter space, P_sigma consists of the integral characters nonnegative on sigma.

Let L_m be the weight-m subsheaf of the actual pushforward of O_T: right translation by h acts on a local function by multiplication by m(h). Its unit and multiplication maps L_m tensor L_n to L_(m+n) are those of O_T, not arbitrary rigidifications.

Use the common integral supporting-character and face-localization theorem from the toric anchor: choose m in P_sigma exposing tau, so P_tau=P_sigma+N(-m).

Lan's cone convention is relatively open; its nonnegative character monoid agrees with that of our closed cone. Strict positivity, when comparing the source ideal notation, is tested on the relative interior, not on the origin. The zero-cone case is treated separately.

Use target name `TauCeti.Toric.Relative.faceOpenImmersion` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Toric/Algebraic/Relative`, namespace `TauCeti.Toric.Relative`.

On a trivialized chart, apply the anchor supporting-character/monoid identity and the generic monoid-algebra localization universal property. A character algebra map extends exactly when the exposed monomial is invertible.

Relative Spec gives a principal open on each trivializing chart. On overlaps the monomial is multiplied by a unit, so the opens and their structural maps agree and descend.

Identity, successive localization and overlap formulas follow by the same character maps, hence descend. Do not infer a map between completions along two distinct stratum ideals from this open immersion.

**Checks.**

- For the quadrant and its first-coordinate ray face, the face chart inverts the second coordinate.
- For sigma the zero cone, the identity face gives the identity torsor map.
- For nontrivial character lines the open is defined locally by a monomial, not by a falsely chosen global q.

### C0.10 Integral regular coordinates on a relative torus chart

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/relative-regular-coordinates -->

On monomials it is the character-exponent map from the existing dual-monoid identification P_sigma with N^r times Z^(n-r). The associated scheme isomorphism is over the actual local base. Use the existing anchor lattice/regular-cone theorem to obtain the additive equivalence of dual monoids. The choice of basis is a hypothesis of this coordinate comparison, not the definition of the global embedding.

Apply the pinned monoid-algebra equivalence induced by that degree equivalence. This operation is already general in the coefficient ring; no new generic algebra congruence is planned.

Identify the product monoid algebra as the stated polynomial/Laurent polynomial algebra by its monomial basis and generic free-algebra interfaces. Transport the actual relative algebra trivialization, then relative Spec.

A change of basis or torsor trivialization is the induced character monomial map and units. Compose these maps using existing functoriality; coordinates never canonize one basis.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-relative-stratum-quotient"></a>
### C0.11 The scheme-theoretic relative toric stratum

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/relative-stratum-quotient -->

For a split torus chart T(sigma), the homogeneous quotient by the sum of L_m over m in P_sigma outside F_sigma=M intersect sigma-perp is canonically Spec_Z of the direct sum of L_m over F_sigma. It is the induced torsor for the split quotient torus with character lattice F_sigma. Its ideal and the quotient construction commute with arbitrary base change. This is the relative scheme-theoretic stratum; identify it with a reduced complement only under the needed reducedness hypotheses. (Sources: [LAN-2021](#lan-2021) Lemma 6.1.2.6 and Definition 6.1.2.7, printed pp. 443–445.)

Z is a scheme, or an algebraic space treated on its actual etale atlas. H is a split torus with finite free character lattice M; T to Z is a right H-torsor in the fppf topology.

Use the common toric lattice/cone vocabulary and its dual additive monoids. For a rational polyhedral salient closed cone sigma in the real cocharacter space, P_sigma consists of the integral characters nonnegative on sigma.

Let L_m be the weight-m subsheaf of the actual pushforward of O_T: right translation by h acts on a local function by multiplication by m(h). Its unit and multiplication maps L_m tensor L_n to L_(m+n) are those of O_T, not arbitrary rigidifications.

Lan's cone convention is relatively open; its nonnegative character monoid agrees with that of our closed cone. Strict positivity, when comparing the source ideal notation, is tested on the relative interior, not on the origin. The zero-cone case is treated separately.

Use target name `TauCeti.Toric.Relative.stratumQuotientIso` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Toric/Algebraic/Relative`, namespace `TauCeti.Toric.Relative`.

Nonnegative real character evaluations show that a+b vanishes on sigma precisely when both a and b do. Thus F_sigma is a face submonoid, and in this case is a saturated subgroup of M.

On a torsor trivialization apply face-projection, face-projection-kernel and face-quotient. The quotient ideal is the off-face homogeneous ideal, not its radical.

Character-unit transitions preserve the retained summands and the projection. Descend the ideal, quotient and isomorphism through the actual torsor cocycle.

The retained character algebra with its inherited multiplication is the pushout torsor for the quotient torus. Obtain this generic torsor/graded-algebra identification from SF.1, rather than defining a second torsor.

Apply face-kernel-coefficient-change and relative Spec base change locally, then descend the comparison. Neither reducedness nor completion commutes with arbitrary base change by this argument.

**Checks.**

- The full positive ray has closed stratum Z; the zero cone has stratum the whole torsor.
- Over Z/4 the full-ray stratum has ring Z/4, not Z/2.
- For a rank-one cone in a rank-two lattice the stratum retains a rank-one torus direction.

### C0.12 Coordinate ideals of relative boundary intersections

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/relative-boundary-coordinates -->

In the regular coordinates of relative-regular-coordinates, the scheme-theoretic intersection of the coordinate boundary components indexed by J has ideal (x_j : j in J) and coordinate algebra R[x_i : i outside J, y_1^(+-1),...,y_(n-r)^(+-1)]. Its exact boundary open is the principal open obtained by inverting the product of x_i for i outside J. These identifications are compatible with base change and with the character-unit transition maps. (Sources: [LAN-2021](#lan-2021) 6.1.2.6–6.1.2.8 and the ordinary charts used in 6.3.2.5, printed pp. 443–445 and 503.)

Z is a scheme, or an algebraic space treated on its actual etale atlas. H is a split torus with finite free character lattice M; T to Z is a right H-torsor in the fppf topology.

Use the common toric lattice/cone vocabulary and its dual additive monoids. For a rational polyhedral salient closed cone sigma in the real cocharacter space, P_sigma consists of the integral characters nonnegative on sigma.

Let L_m be the weight-m subsheaf of the actual pushforward of O_T: right translation by h acts on a local function by multiplication by m(h). Its unit and multiplication maps L_m tensor L_n to L_(m+n) are those of O_T, not arbitrary rigidifications.

Use the supplied regular cone basis and actual torsor trivialization, and J a subset of the r boundary-coordinate indices.

Lan's cone convention is relatively open; its nonnegative character monoid agrees with that of our closed cone. Strict positivity, when comparing the source ideal notation, is tested on the relative interior, not on the origin. The zero-cone case is treated separately.

Use target name `TauCeti.Toric.Relative.boundaryCoordinateIso` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Toric/Algebraic/Relative`, namespace `TauCeti.Toric.Relative`.

The degrees whose J coordinates are zero form a face submonoid. The complement monomial ideal is exactly generated by the x_j with j in J: every off-face monomial contains such a variable, and every multiple of such a variable is off-face.

Apply face-quotient to that face and the regular monoid coordinates. This computes the scheme-theoretic intersection with its actual base coefficients.

Removing the other boundary components is precisely localization at the product of the remaining x_i. Multiplication by this monomial is injective by shifting the exponent basis, over every coefficient ring and after every base change. Thus this open is universally schematically dense; ordinary topological density follows as well.

Polynomial and Laurent polynomial algebras are smooth over R. The quotient algebra therefore gives smoothness of each intersection over the local base. Descend these particular coordinate descriptions and consequences through SF.0/SF.1. Smoothness over the arithmetic base additionally requires smoothness of the cusp base.

Transport each ideal and open using the actual character-unit changes. Do not apply the conclusion to identified global boundary branches without C5 neat branch separation and label-preserving ordinary charts.

**Checks.**

- For a quadrant, J empty, one coordinate and both coordinates give the plane, a line and the base, respectively.
- When no remaining boundary coordinate is present, the product is 1 and the exact open is the whole intersection.
- The coordinate quotient over Z/4 retains 2; passing to a radical ideal would fail the test.
- For G_m over F_2, density is a scheme-theoretic statement, not a claim about the number of rational points.

### C0.13 Arithmetic-admissible cone systems

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/arithmetic-admissible-fan -->

Extend the common fan incidence data by a possibly infinite set of rational polyhedral salient cones, an integral arithmetic action and finitely many cone orbits. For every cusp the support lies in the rational closure C* of its positivity cone; completeness means support=C*. Require local finiteness on compact subsets of the OPEN positivity domain, closure under faces, intersections as common faces, stabilizer invariance, and compatibility under the actual rational boundary restriction and level actions. The finite specialization is the pinned Fan; finite orbit count is never substituted for a finite set of cones. (Sources: [PINK-1990](#pink-1990) 6.4–6.6, printed pp. 96–99.)

The lattice and PointedCone, IsToricCone and IsFaceOf predicates are the pinned common carriers. Supply integral linear actions preserving the lattice and C*.

The arithmetic quotient action is its effective image on the lattice. Global cusp compatibility includes the finite double-coset indexing, not just one fan at one cusp.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C0.arithmetic_admissible_fan` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C0`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Keep the shared incidence fields of Fan, replacing only finite_cones by the arithmetic action/orbit-finiteness package. Assemble cusp-indexed cones with Pink 6.4 equivariance under rational conjugation, boundary restriction and right level action.

Express support and local finiteness in the positivity domain. The origin lies in every cone and cannot have an ambient locally finite neighbourhood for an infinite arithmetic fan.

Restriction to a boundary component preserves incidence and equivariance; separately check completeness and orbit finiteness, which Pink 6.6 does not grant automatically.

This supplies [ShimuraCompactifications:C3](#shimuracompactifications-c3): Supplies compatible refinement data rather than a fixed Hecke-stable fan.

Expose the following API under the same hypotheses:

- `ArithmeticFan.ext`: Two systems with the same cusp lattices, actions and cone sets agree; proof witnesses do not create a second cone carrier.
- `ArithmeticFan.toFiniteFan`: If the cone set is finite, forgetting the action/support data gives the pinned Fan with exactly that cone set.
- `ArithmeticFan.restrict`: Boundary and level restriction preserve incidence and equivariance; completeness and orbit finiteness are transported only under the established source hypotheses.
- `ArithmeticFan.support`: Completeness is the equality of union of cones with the specified rational closure C*, not all ambient vectors.

**Checks.**

- `ArithmeticFan.zero_test`: For the rank-zero lattice C*={0}, the complete system has exactly the zero cone.
- `ArithmeticFan.finite_test`: For a finite quadrant fan and trivial action, toFiniteFan has exactly its original cones.
- `ArithmeticFan.origin_test`: A genuinely infinite fan with common vertex 0 fails ambient local finiteness at 0 and remains allowed when locally finite on C.
- `ArithmeticFan.support_test`: The single positive ray fan in R has support R>=0 and is not a complete fan with prescribed support R.
- An infinite arithmetic fan can have finitely many orbits; the API must not coerce it to a finite Fan.
- Restriction never asserts completeness without a hypothesis.

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/smooth-projective-refinement -->

Every complete admissible system at neat level has a complete smooth projective admissible refinement, compatible with any specified finite family of fan maps. Resolve singular cones by primitive integral subdivisions, retaining orbit and boundary compatibility; barycentrically refine when the separate no-self-identification property is requested.

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/arbitrary-ring-toric-charts -->

For the shared lattice and dual monoid P_sigma, construct U_sigma,R=Spec R[P_sigma] for every commutative ring R, by base change of Spec Z[P_sigma]. Glue a FINITE fan using the ordinary face localizations to obtain X_Sigma,R, with its split torus action and stratum ideals. - `Toric.affineChart_ray_Z4_test`: The positive ray over Z/4 has coordinate ring (Z/4)[q], and its closed stratum has coordinate ring Z/4.
- `Toric.affineChart_zero_test`: The zero cone yields Spec R[M], the split torus rather than affine n-space.
- `Toric.affineChart_valuation_test`: For an arbitrary valuation ring V, U_sigma,V is the base change of U_sigma,Z and the same face-open maps apply.
- `Toric.affineChart_nilpotent_test`: Replacing R by its reduction would kill the nonzero scalar 2 over Z/4 and is rejected.
- Over Z/4, U_ray has ring (Z/4)[q] and its q=0 stratum retains 2.
- Finite complex specialization compares to the original anchor through the same monomials.

*Needs:* [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-0-the-toric-compatible-algebraic-supplier](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-0-the-toric-compatible-algebraic-supplier); [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/face-projection-coefficient-change](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-face-projection-coefficient-change); [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/face-kernel-coefficient-change](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-face-kernel-coefficient-change); `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.0`; `tauceti:TauCeti.SplitTorus.groupScheme`; `mathlib:AlgebraicGeometry.Spec`; `mathlib:AlgebraicGeometry.Spec.map`.

Attribution: Relative monoid charts specialize to the trivial torsor; universal coefficient extension is proved by the monomial basis.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-relative-fan-properness"></a>
### C0.17 Relative fan maps and support properness

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/relative-fan-properness -->

An integral lattice map and a compatible equivariant map of split torus torsors with FINITE fans induce the relative toric map over the same base scheme. The inverse-image support criterion implies properness over every base. Conversely, for a NONEMPTY source fan over a NONEMPTY base, properness implies that criterion by testing a geometric fibre. Over the empty base every map is proper and support is not detected. For arithmetic quotients separately require finite-type separated quotient charts and compatible arithmetic support; orbit finiteness alone never makes the infinite unquotiented toric space proper. A nonempty fan contains the zero cone; an empty source fan instead gives a proper empty morphism without detecting the inverse-image support condition. (Sources: [PINK-1990](#pink-1990) 6.25 and 6.27, printed pp. 112–115.)

Use finite fans and an actual morphism of torsors equivariant for the torus homomorphism. The base morphism in the relative assertion is the identity; composing with another proper base morphism preserves properness.

The arithmetic variant uses the actual separated finite-type quotient model from C2 or C5 and complete compatible cusp systems.

The necessary direction requires both a nonempty base and a nonempty source fan. The sufficient direction permits empty bases and empty source fans whenever its support criterion holds.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C0.relative_fan_properness` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C0`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Locally trivialize the torsors and prove the integral finite-fan support criterion by valuation extension of character monomials; the same lattice inequalities hold over arbitrary coefficients. For necessity choose a geometric point of the nonempty base and use the nonempty source fan; explicitly exclude the empty-source counterexample.

Glue the local morphisms through torus transition units. Properness descends fpqc locally on the base.

For the arithmetic assertion apply the criterion on the finite quotient-chart presentations and separately prove the quotient is finite type and separated. Compare over C to L5.

**Checks.**

- A complete P1 fan gives a proper relative P1; a single positive ray gives A1, which is not proper.
- A subdivision with unchanged support gives a proper map.
- Over a nonempty base the empty-source map is proper, but its empty support is not the inverse image of a nonempty target fan support containing zero. Necessity excludes this case; sufficiency still allows the empty base.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/arbitrary-ring-toric-charts](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-arbitrary-ring-toric-charts); [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/relative-torus-embedding](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-relative-torus-embedding); [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-5-toric-maps-and-properness](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-5-toric-maps-and-properness); `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.0`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.1`.

Attribution: Arithmetic compatibility and compactness supply the additional quotient hypotheses.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-regular-boundary-union"></a>
### C0.18 The integral toric boundary union

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/regular-boundary-union -->

In a regular split chart A_R=R[x_1,...,x_r,y_1^±1,...,y_s^±1], the scheme-theoretic union of the r toric boundary divisors has ideal (t), t=x_1...x_r. Its quotient has R-basis the monomials x^a y^b for which at least one a_i is zero. Thus the boundary is flat over R, its geometric fibres are reduced, and formation of this quotient commutes with every coefficient homomorphism. The intersection of selected components indexed by J has instead ideal (x_j : j in J). (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Section 2, printed pp. 529–530; [LAN-2021](#lan-2021) Theorem 6.1.2.8(5), printed p. 445.)

R is any commutative ring; r,s are finite nonnegative integers. The coordinates arise from a basis extending the primitive regular cone generators, after a genuine torsor trivialization.

For r=0 the boundary is empty: t=1, A_R/(t)=0. Geometric reducedness is a statement about fibres over fields; it does not make the total boundary reduced over a nonreduced R.

Use target name `regular_boundary_union` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Compactification/C0`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Use the free coefficient basis of the existing monoid algebra. Multiplication by t shifts the first r exponent coordinates by one and is injective over every R.

The intersection of the principal component ideals consists exactly of monomials divisible by every x_i, hence equals (t). The quotient keeps precisely the complementary coefficient basis.

The quotient is R-free, and the same monomial presentation after coefficient change proves the asserted base change. Over a field the squarefree monomial ideal (t) is radical, by its intersection of the prime coordinate ideals.

Descend the ideal and flatness along the actual etale torsor trivializations; retain the separate intersection ideal.

**Checks.**

- For r=2 the union quotient is R[x_1,x_2]/(x_1x_2), while the intersection quotient is R; they must differ.
- Over Z/4, the boundary quotient retains the nonzero nilpotent constant 2.
- For r=0 the boundary quotient is zero, and for r=1 it is the Laurent algebra R[y^±1].

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/relative-regular-coordinates](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-relative-regular-coordinates); [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/relative-boundary-coordinates](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-relative-boundary-coordinates); `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.0`.

Attribution: The regular chart and product boundary parameter give this coefficient-basis comparison. Flatness and fibre reducedness are deductions here, not a separately numbered theorem in the paper.

Attribution: The relative Cartier boundary in regular charts is the geometric setting of the comparison.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-completed-regular-chart-support"></a>
### C0.19 Completed regular toric chart coefficients

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/completed-regular-chart-support -->

For r>0 and t=x_1...x_r in A_R, identify the t-adic completion lim_h A_R/(t^h) with coefficient families c_(a,b) indexed by N^r × Z^s such that for every positive h only finitely many nonzero coefficients have a outside (h,...,h)+N^r. Multiplication is the compatible finite convolution in every quotient. Inverting a fixed monomial before completion shifts the exponent lattice and the support bound by that monomial; this describes boundary-meromorphic sections with a fixed finite pole divisor. The comparison is natural at the quotient levels for every R→S, and with the formal completion functor under its stated completion/base-change hypotheses. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Section 2, printed pp. 529–530.)

R is any commutative ring, r>0 and s≥0 are finite. The same regular coordinates and their actual boundary union ideal are used.

The coefficient family condition includes finite support in the Laurent directions outside every diagonal tail. It is not the unrestricted multivariable power-series ring.

A localization shift describes a fixed localization or finite pole order; arbitrary infinite localizations and tensor products are not asserted to commute with completion.

Use target name `completed_regular_chart_support` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Compactification/C0`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Use the monomial quotient basis of A_R/(t^h): the surviving indices are those with at least one a_i<h, and each element has finite support there.

A compatible family of quotient coefficients determines a unique coefficient at every index. Conversely the stated finiteness condition gives an element in each quotient and makes their transition maps compatible.

Define multiplication through the quotient rings; verify compatibility and the coefficient formula wherever a fixed quotient coefficient is computed. A monomial localization translates the supported exponents and the finite pole bound.

Apply the imported formal completion and sheafification interfaces, with their exact adic base-change conditions. Use quotient-level coefficient comparisons for arbitrary R→S rather than asserting an ordinary tensor-product formula for the completed ring.

**Checks.**

- For r=1,s=0 the completion is R[[x]].
- For r=2 the series sum_(n≥0) x_1^n x_2^n is admitted, whereas sum_(n≥0) x_1^n is excluded since it remains infinite modulo (x_1x_2).
- For r=1,s=1 an infinite Laurent sum with x-degree zero is excluded, but sum_(n≥0) x^n y^n is admitted.
- A nonzero nilpotent constant remains nonzero; localization by t permits a finite negative diagonal shift.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/regular-boundary-union](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-regular-boundary-union); `AdicSpacesPartII:F0`; [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/arbitrary-ring-toric-charts](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-arbitrary-ring-toric-charts).

Attribution: The product-adic chart coefficients and finite support outside every diagonal tail underlie the completed-chart comparison and the finite localization shift.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-integral-changed-lattice-comparison"></a>
### C0.20 Finite toric maps for changed lattices

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/integral-changed-lattice-comparison -->

For an injective finite-index cocharacter lattice map i:N′→N and a finite rational fan Σ in N_R, the pulled-back fan in N′_R yields a finite toric morphism X(i^*Σ)_R→X(Σ)_R. On each corresponding chart it is the map R[σ^∨∩M]→R[(i_R^−1σ)^∨∩M′] induced by the dual inclusion M→M′. Over a normal coefficient domain these normal source charts identify the normalization of the target charts in the finite torus function-field extension; the identification glues. Further subdivisions give proper refinement maps, which need not be finite. Arithmetic cusp systems use this statement chartwise, with quotient descent checked separately. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Remark 7.3, printed p. 543; [LAN-2017](#lan-2017) Theorem 6.1(3)–(4), printed pp. 22–23; Construction 4.5, printed p. 11.)

R is any commutative ring for the finite morphism statement. N,N′ are finite free Z-modules and the fan is finite; the real lattice map is an isomorphism.

The normalization identification additionally assumes R is a normal domain and uses the saturated monoid charts inside their actual common torus function fields. No normalization assertion is made for arbitrary nonreduced coefficients.

For a global arithmetic quotient, exact cusp relations and stabilizer descent must be verified. This local theorem does not imply that every change of level extends to unrelated fans.

Use target name `integral_changed_lattice_comparison` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Compactification/C0`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Import the anchor lattice/fan morphism vocabulary and the C0 uniform coefficient charts. The contravariant dual inclusion identifies the corresponding character monoids.

A common positive integer kills M′/M, so a positive multiple of every new character belongs to the old monoid. Its monomial satisfies a monic equation over the old chart. Finite generation of rational saturated monoids makes this integral algebra finite.

For normal R the saturated chart algebra is normal, and its fraction field is that of the source torus. Apply the normalization universal property and the exact face localizations to glue.

Separate a pullback fan from a subdivision; use the refinement properness theorem for the latter and the actual arithmetic relation for quotient descent.

**Checks.**

- In rank one, i is multiplication by e>0 and the coordinate map is q↦(q′)^e, finite of rank e over every R.
- Blowing up the origin in the two-dimensional regular cone is a proper fan refinement with an exceptional curve, hence not finite.
- Regularity must be checked in N′, and a nilpotent coefficient ring does not acquire a normality claim.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/arbitrary-ring-toric-charts](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-arbitrary-ring-toric-charts); [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/relative-face-open](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-relative-face-open); [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-5-toric-maps-and-properness](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-5-toric-maps-and-properness); `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.0`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.1`.

Attribution: The level changes the cusp lattice; normalizing in the higher-level torus field need not preserve regularity of cones.

Attribution: The normalized chart comparison motivates the integral pullback-fan instance; its global arithmetic descent is a separate obligation.

### Examples

- Take R=Z/4, P=N, F={0}. The stratum sends e₁ to zero and sends 2e₀ to the nonzero nilpotent 2. Replacing the quotient by its reduction fails this test.
- For A=R[x,y], the boundary union is A/(xy), with basis 1,x,x²,…,y,y²,… over R. The closed intersection is A/(x,y), and these are different targets.
- In the (xy)-adic completion, a family may have infinitely many diagonal terms xⁿyⁿ. The family ∑ₙxⁿ fails the finite-support condition already modulo xy. The completion is consequently not an unrestricted two-variable formal-series ring.
- For the inclusion 2Z→Z of cocharacter lattices and the positive ray, the chart map is R[z]→R[w], z↦w². It is finite even over Z/4. Its normalization interpretation needs normal-domain hypotheses.

### Dependencies

Alongside the local target chain, this layer consumes `AdicSpacesPartII:F0`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.0`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.1`; `ShimuraVarieties:V2/rational-boundary`; `mathlib:AddMonoidAlgebra.lift`; `mathlib:AddMonoidAlgebra.lift_single`; `mathlib:AlgebraicGeometry.Spec`; `mathlib:AlgebraicGeometry.Spec.map`; `mathlib:Ideal.quotientKerAlgEquivOfRightInverse`; `mathlib:MonoidAlgebra.comapDomain`; `mathlib:MonoidAlgebra.domCongr`; `mathlib:MonoidAlgebra.mapDomainAlgHom`; `mathlib:MonoidAlgebra.mapRingHom`; `tauceti:TauCeti.SplitTorus.groupScheme`; `tauceti:TauCeti.Toric.Fan.ext`; [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-0-the-toric-compatible-algebraic-supplier](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-0-the-toric-compatible-algebraic-supplier); [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-5-toric-maps-and-properness](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-5-toric-maps-and-properness); [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/ReductiveGroups#layer-7-structure-theory](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/ReductiveGroups/README.md#layer-7-structure-theory).

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c1"></a>
## Layer C1: Rational boundary and mixed data

Import the rational boundary component from V2 and enrich it by its mixed group, the central weight-minus-two subgroup, quotient, homogeneous cover and Hodge structure. Preserve the finite cover of the h-map image. The weight filtration is on Lie P₁: its three nontrivial levels are Lie U₁, Lie W₁ and Lie P₁. The quotient V₁=W₁/U₁ is not interchangeable with W₁.

The pure quotient and torus/abelian torsors describe the boundary without assuming a universal abelian scheme for an arbitrary pure datum. Early mixed-group and effective-action exports precede C0 arithmetic fan compatibility; later cusp labels and incidence consume that compatibility. This ordering explains the proposed module split without changing any stage id.

**Milestones.**

- [Mixed Shimura boundary datum](#shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-mixed-boundary-datum)
- [Boundary mixed Hodge structure](#shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-boundary-mixed-hodge-structure)
- [Boundary torus torsor](#shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-boundary-torsor-tower)
- [Cusp label](#shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-cusp-label)
- [Cusp stabilizer](#shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-arithmetic-stabilizer)

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-mixed-boundary-datum"></a>
### C1.1 Mixed Shimura boundary datum

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C1/roadmap/mixed-boundary-datum -->

Enrich V2's existing rational boundary component by Pink's admissible parabolic Q, the associated connected group P1, its unipotent radical W1, distinguished central weight-minus-two subgroup U1, V1=W1/U1 and pure quotient G1=P1/W1. The boundary domain X1 is the specified homogeneous finite-cover space with h1:S_C→(P1)_C; retain its finite fibers, real descent modulo U1, central weight cocharacter, Cartan-involution/no-compact-Q-factor and center conditions. Ad on Lie P1 has only weights 0,-1,-2 with W_-2=Lie U1, W_-1=Lie W1 and W_0=Lie P1. U1 and W1 are distinct inputs. (Sources: [PINK-1990](#pink-1990) 2.1; 4.7–4.12, printed pp. 29–30 and 59–63.)

Fix a pure Shimura datum (G,X), compact open K and its actual arithmetic component quotients from V0. No universal abelian scheme is assumed for this datum.

V2 supplies the rational boundary component; D4 supplies the ambient pure Shimura datum and its algebraic morphisms; the parabolic/Lie structure is imported from ReductiveGroups Layer 7. The homogeneous finite cover X1 is not replaced by the image of h1.

Mixed Hodge structures, filtrations and strictness are the pinned Hodge carriers and HodgeStructures L2, not new generic definitions.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C1.mixed_boundary_datum` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C1`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Apply Pink 4.7–4.12 boundary homomorphism construction to the existing rational boundary/parabolic datum, and obtain P1,W1,U1 by the specified weight filtration.

Verify Pink 2.1 axioms from the boundary representation and the Hodge filtration comparison in 4.12. Use the corrected Lie filtration rather than the misprinted author-copy 2.1(v).

Identify the analytic boundary component through the pure quotient, retaining the finite homogeneous cover and incidence data.

This supplies [ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/partial-boundary-charts](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-partial-boundary-charts): Supplies the actual mixed boundary torsor and parabolic quotient.

This supplies `AutomorphicBundles:B3`: Uses the same boundary datum for canonical coefficient charts.

Expose the following API under the same hypotheses:

- `MixedBoundaryDatum.pureQuotient`: The quotient P1/W1 with its induced domain is the existing pure boundary datum.
- `MixedBoundaryDatum.weightFiltration`: The three steps are exactly Lie U1, Lie W1 and Lie P1; V1 identifies with gr_-1.
- `MixedBoundaryDatum.conjugation`: Rational conjugation transports the groups, domain cover, h1 and filtrations with the V2 boundary label.
- `MixedBoundaryDatum.h_finiteFibers`: The map h1 has finite fibers; the definition keeps X1 rather than setting X1=image h1.
- `MixedBoundaryDatum.ext`: With the ambient datum and homogeneous cover fixed, equality of the algebraic subgroup embeddings P1,W1,U1 and the boundary h1/domain data determines equality of boundary data; axiom proofs add no extra carrier.

**Checks.**

- `MixedBoundaryDatum.siegel2_rank1_test`: At a genus-two rank-one cusp, U1 has rank 1 and V1 dimension 2, so W1 has dimension 3.
- `MixedBoundaryDatum.pure_test`: When W1=1, U1=1 and the Lie algebra of P1 is entirely weight zero.
- `MixedBoundaryDatum.hodge_test`: The induced graded structures are the supplied HodgeStructure on the native weight-graded quotient.
- `MixedBoundaryDatum.radical_center_test`: Using all W1 as the torus character group gives rank 3 instead of 1 at the genus-two rank-one cusp and fails.
- For the rank-one Siegel cusp of genus 2, dim U1=1 while dim W1=3.
- For a pure datum the unipotent radical is zero and the entire adjoint Lie algebra remains in weight zero.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-boundary-mixed-hodge-structure"></a>
### C1.2 Boundary weight and Hodge filtrations

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C1/roadmap/boundary-mixed-hodge-structure -->

For a rational representation of the boundary group P1 and a chosen invariant integral lattice, construct the native mixed Hodge structure induced by h1, with its genuine rational/complex base changes. If the representation is the restriction of a rational representation of the original group P, Pink 4.12 compares corresponding points x and x1: the Hodge filtration is unchanged while the weight filtration changes. This comparison is not asserted for an arbitrary P1 representation that does not extend to P. For the boundary adjoint representation, the weight steps are Lie U1, Lie W1 and Lie P1, with graded types (-1,-1), {(-1,0),(0,-1)} and {(-1,1),(0,0),(1,-1)}. (Sources: [PINK-1990](#pink-1990) 4.7–4.12, especially 4.12, printed pp. 59–63.)

Use mixed-boundary-datum and the chosen rational/integral representation with genuine rational and complex base-change models. Do not postulate a canonical integral lattice in every rational representation.

For Hodge-filtration agreement, fix an ambient rational P representation, restrict it to P1, and use the associated points x and x1 of Pink 4.12. The independent boundary adjoint calculation does not assert that the P1 adjoint representation extends to P.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C1.boundary_mixed_hodge_structure` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C1`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Factor the boundary homomorphism through Pink's standard H0 construction and transport the induced filtrations on the representation.

Identify the induced pure Hodge structure on each weight quotient through the existing graded carrier. The adjoint weight statements follow from the unipotent groups.

Apply Pink 4.12 only to an ambient P representation restricted to P1 at corresponding points; import strictness and filtered tensor/morphism APIs from HodgeStructures L2 for boundary representations themselves.

This supplies [ShimuraCompactifications:C1/roadmap/boundary-torsor-tower](#shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-boundary-torsor-tower): Identifies torus and abelian pieces of the actual boundary neighbourhood.

- `BoundaryMHS.weight_adjoint`: Its weight steps identify with the three specified Lie subobjects.
- `BoundaryMHS.hodge_boundary_eq`: For a rational representation of P restricted to P1 and the corresponding x,x1 of Pink 4.12, the original and boundary Hodge filtrations agree on the same complexified representation.
- `BoundaryMHS.map`: Representation maps are morphisms of the native mixed Hodge structures and are strict by the owner API.
- `BoundaryMHS.native_filtrations`: The returned native mixed Hodge structure has exactly the h1-induced WQ and F on the specified base-change models; its graded Hodge filtration is the induced quotient filtration of the pinned carrier.

**Checks.**

- `BoundaryMHS.pure_test`: For a pure boundary group W1=1, W_-1=0 and W_0=Lie P1.
- `BoundaryMHS.siegel2_test`: At a genus-two rank-one cusp, gr_-2 has dimension 1 and gr_-1 dimension 2.
- `BoundaryMHS.graded_test`: The graded Hodge filtration is exactly the induced quotient filtration, not an unrelated pure structure.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-boundary-torsor-tower"></a>
### C1.3 Pure quotients and boundary torsors

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C1/roadmap/boundary-torsor-tower -->

At a neat boundary level, construct the mixed arithmetic boundary quotient as a torus torsor over an abelian-scheme torsor over a finite cover of the pure boundary Shimura variety. Its torus comes from the arithmetic lattice in U1, and its abelian directions from W1/U1. The commutator determines the Poincare/cubical torsor class. This is the boundary part of mixed Shimura theory; canonical models of the special mixed torsors required for descent are a separate supplier request. (Sources: [PINK-1990](#pink-1990) 3.12–3.22; 6.1–6.7; 7.2–7.5, printed pp. 47–53, 94–99 and 118–121.)

Use mixed-boundary-datum, its Hodge structure and the actual arithmetic lattices from K. Assume neatness for the asserted torsor form; at non-neat level retain finite stabilizer quotients.

Use the exact central lattice Gamma_U(-1) and the weight-minus-one lattice; they are not interchangeable.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C1.boundary_torsor_tower` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C1`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Pink 3.12–3.19 express the U quotient as an algebraic torus and the V/F0 quotient as a complex torus with the supplied polarization.

Apply the abelian-algebraization/polarization input to the compact weight-minus-one torus, and construct the commutator torsor through its line-bundle class.

Assemble the quotients over the pure arithmetic boundary quotient, compare transition maps and keep the finite level cover; pass to non-neat finite quotients separately.
