This supplies [ShimuraCompactifications:C2](#shimuracompactifications-c2): Supplies the mixed neighbourhood of each existing rational boundary.

Expose the following API under the same hypotheses:

- `BoundaryTorsor.torusCharacters`: The character lattice is dual to the actual integral U1 lattice.
- `BoundaryTorsor.abelianQuotient`: The quotient by the central torus is the constructed abelian torsor over the pure boundary base.
- `BoundaryTorsor.levelChange`: Specified level changes induce the lattice/torsor maps and commute with rational conjugation.

**Checks.**

- `BoundaryTorsor.siegel2_klingen_test`: The genus-two rank-one cusp has torus rank 1 and an elliptic abelian direction.
- `BoundaryTorsor.siegel2_siegel_test`: The maximal genus-two cusp has torus rank dim Sym²(Z²)=3 and abelian dimension zero.
- `BoundaryTorsor.nontrivial_test`: A nontrivial character line gives a nontrivial torus torsor, not a chosen product with G_m.
- At a genus-two rank-one Siegel cusp the abelian fibre has dimension 1 and the torus rank is 1.
- At a maximal genus-two cusp the torus rank is 3 and there is no positive-dimensional abelian fibre.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C1/roadmap/mixed-boundary-datum](#shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-mixed-boundary-datum); [ShimuraCompactifications:C1/roadmap/boundary-mixed-hodge-structure](#shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-boundary-mixed-hodge-structure); `AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli:A2`; `AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli:A5`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.1`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.3`; `ShimuraVarieties:V0/component-decomposition`; `tauceti:TauCeti.SplitTorus.groupScheme`.

Attribution: Central torus and abelian-torsor tower with finite homogeneous-cover and stabilizer qualifications.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-cusp-label"></a>
### C1.4 Cusp labels and their equivalence

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C1/roadmap/cusp-label -->

Define an adelic cusp label from the existing rational boundary component/parabolic, its boundary mixed datum and the finite-adelic level representative. Quotient by the actual rational conjugation, boundary arithmetic action and right-K action. A cone label adds a cone in that cusp's common lattice and uses the compatible induced equivalence. Neither a representative nor a connected/irreducible component of its boundary base is identified with the entire equivalence class. (Sources: [PINK-1990](#pink-1990) 6.10–6.12 and 7.2–7.5, printed pp. 100–102 and 118–121.)

Fix a pure Shimura datum (G,X), compact open K and its actual arithmetic component quotients from V0. Cones and lattices are the common toric carriers; the fans are compatible arithmetic-admissible systems from C0. No universal abelian scheme is assumed for this datum.

The mixed boundary datum and the finite adelic double-coset description are supplied; use Pink's finite cover X1.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C1.cusp_label` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C1`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Define the equivalence relation by Pink 6.10–6.12 elementary actions and boundary identifications; prove the displayed commutation relations give a consistent quotient.

Compute the stabilizing action on the central lattice and transport cone membership, preserving the actual cusp datum.

Use arithmetic double-coset finiteness and finite cone orbits for the finite set of stratum labels used in a complete compactification.

This supplies [ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/arithmetic-gluing](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-arithmetic-gluing): Indexes quotient charts and their overlaps.

This supplies [ShimuraCompactifications:C5/roadmap/formal-completion](#shimuracompactifications-c5-roadmap-formal-completion): Preserves the cusp/cone labels in formal charts.

Expose the following API under the same hypotheses:

- `CuspLabel.representative_invariant`: All elementary rational/level transformations give the same cusp class.
- `CuspLabel.cone_transport`: The label equivalence transports cones through the specified integral map.
- `CuspLabel.finite`: At finite level the cusp classes are finite; complete admissible cone systems have finitely many cone-label orbits.
- `CuspLabel.mk`: A genuine boundary/cusp representative with its finite-adelic level data defines its class in the equivalence quotient.
- `CuspLabel.eq_iff`: Two representatives have equal cusp labels exactly when they are related by the source's specified rational/level elementary equivalence; no chosen representative is part of a quotient label.

**Checks.**

- `CuspLabel.modular_test`: For GL2-type modular data cusp labels reduce to the standard rational-cusp double cosets with their level width.
- `CuspLabel.representative_test`: Right multiplication by K gives the identical cusp class.
- `CuspLabel.component_test`: A cusp base with two connected components has one admissible cusp class but two possible component strata; they are not collapsed.
- Changing a representative by an allowed action does not change the stratum label.
- Labels never force a disconnected boundary base to be irreducible.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C1/roadmap/mixed-boundary-datum](#shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-mixed-boundary-datum); `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3`; [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/arithmetic-admissible-fan](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-arithmetic-admissible-fan); `ShimuraVarieties:V2/rational-boundary`.

Attribution: Actual label equivalence and stratum quotients.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-arithmetic-stabilizer"></a>
### C1.5 Effective arithmetic stabilizers and finite overlap

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C1/roadmap/arithmetic-stabilizer -->

Construct the cusp normalizer quotient Delta1 and its effective action on U1/lattices. For polyhedral cones sigma,tau in the rational closure, the set of EFFECTIVE arithmetic images gamma for which gamma(sigma) intersects tau in the open positivity cone is finite. Identify the ineffective kernel up to the specified central/arithmetic subgroups. Cone stabilizers act through finite groups; neatness removes the relevant effective finite cone action. Do not assert that the full normalizer is finite or that a merely neat level removes every central kernel. (Sources: [PINK-1990](#pink-1990) 6.18–6.21, printed pp. 105–109.)

Use Pink 6.18 definitions and the positivity cone from the actual boundary datum. Both cones are in the appropriate rational closure.

The finite-overlap conclusion uses the arithmetic reduction input for the homogeneous positivity cone, requested from AA.3/V2.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C1.arithmetic_stabilizer` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C1`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Pass to the image rho(Q) of the cusp normalizer on U1; identify the kernel using Pink 6.20 rather than counting every ineffective element.

Apply Pink 6.19(a) arithmetic reduction to cone intersections and 6.19(b) a rational polyhedral reduction domain.

Derive finite cone stabilizer images; use neatness on the finite effective action and retain the ineffective quotient in the torsor/stack model.

**Checks.**

- An infinite central kernel does not contradict finite effective overlap.
- The quotient action must preserve the central lattice.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C1/roadmap/mixed-boundary-datum](#shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-mixed-boundary-datum); `ShimuraVarieties:V2/rational-boundary`; `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3`; [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/ReductiveGroups#layer-7-structure-theory](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/ReductiveGroups/README.md#layer-7-structure-theory).

Attribution: Effective finite-overlap argument; AMRT reduction proof remains a named source leaf.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-boundary-incidence"></a>
### C1.6 Positivity cones and boundary incidence

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C1/roadmap/boundary-incidence -->

Identify the positivity cone C(P1,X1) and its rational closure as the union of the cones of incident rational boundary data. For a pure initial datum the cone is open convex nondegenerate homogeneous self-adjoint in U1(R); general mixed reductions may carry lineality and require the quotient in Pink 4.15. Prove nested-boundary and rational-conjugation formulas on groups, central lattices, torsors and cones, preserving the V2 analytic incidence relation. (Sources: [PINK-1990](#pink-1990) 4.15 and 4.22–4.25, printed pp. 64 and 68–69.)

Fix a pure Shimura datum (G,X), compact open K and its actual arithmetic component quotients from V0. No universal abelian scheme is assumed for this datum.

Use the exact embedded central spaces and quotient maps of Pink 4.22–4.25; C* need be neither open nor closed.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C1.boundary_incidence` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C1`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Pink 4.15 identifies the pure-boundary cone using the Hodge representation and Cartan involution.

Pink 4.22–4.25 give the rational closure and intersection/quotient identities for incident components.

Transport through conjugation and the cusp-label equivalence, producing the face/stratum order for the toroidal charts.

**Checks.**

- For Siegel maximal cusps C is the positive-definite symmetric cone; C* includes positive-semidefinite forms with rational radical.
- Do not replace C* by the topological closure with irrational-radical boundary points.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C1/roadmap/mixed-boundary-datum](#shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-mixed-boundary-datum); [ShimuraCompactifications:C1/roadmap/boundary-mixed-hodge-structure](#shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-boundary-mixed-hodge-structure); `ShimuraVarieties:V2/rational-boundary`; [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/ReductiveGroups#layer-7-structure-theory](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/ReductiveGroups/README.md#layer-7-structure-theory); [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-0-the-toric-compatible-algebraic-supplier](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-0-the-toric-compatible-algebraic-supplier).

Attribution: Positivity and rational closure, with pure/mixed lineality distinction.

### Examples

- For a pure datum with W₁=1, the weight-zero step remains all of Lie P₁. A filtration with Lie W₁ as its top step fails exhaustivity.
- At a cusp distinguish U₁ from W₁ and V₁. Their weights and quotient maps must reproduce the central torus and abelian-torsor dimensions.
- An infinite ineffective subgroup of a stabilizer can act trivially on the lattice. Finite effective cone stabilizers never assert that the whole arithmetic stabilizer is finite.

### Dependencies

Alongside the local target chain, this layer consumes `AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli:A2`; `AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli:A5`; `AdelicAlgebraicGroups:AA.3`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.1`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.3`; `ShimuraData:D3`; `ShimuraData:D4`; `ShimuraVarieties:V0/component-decomposition`; `ShimuraVarieties:V2/rational-boundary`; `tauceti:TauCeti.Hodge.MixedHodgeStructure`; `tauceti:TauCeti.Hodge.MixedHodgeStructure.gradedHodgeStructure`; `tauceti:TauCeti.SplitTorus.groupScheme`; [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-0-the-toric-compatible-algebraic-supplier](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-0-the-toric-compatible-algebraic-supplier); [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/HodgeStructures#milestone-l2--mixed-hodge-structures-strictness-deligne](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/HodgeStructures/README.md#milestone-l2--mixed-hodge-structures-strictness-deligne); [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/ReductiveGroups#layer-7-structure-theory](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/ReductiveGroups/README.md#layer-7-structure-theory).

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c2"></a>
## Layer C2: Toroidal gluing in characteristic zero

Build partial boundary torsor embeddings, controlled arithmetic quotient neighbourhoods and their overlap relation before gluing. The finite regular complex charts and their overlap cocycle come from AnalyticToricGeometry. Singular and nonreduced analytic charts require the actual ComplexComparisonPartII carrier, with its scheme analytification and gluing interfaces.

Normality, dense interior, properness, local normal crossings, projective algebraization and canonical descent are separate milestones. Neatness and regular cones give local coordinate branches. Global simple normal crossings additionally requires the stated face no-self-identification condition. Canonical descent also requires the special mixed boundary models; uniqueness of a pure canonical model cannot construct them.

**Milestones.**

- [Partial toroidal compactification](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-partial-boundary-charts)
- [Toroidal compactification](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-arithmetic-gluing)
- [Toroidal properness](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-compactness-properness)
- [Normal crossings boundary](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-smooth-normal-crossings)
- [Projective toroidal algebraization](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-projective-algebraization)
- [Canonical toroidal model](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-canonical-toroidal-model)

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-partial-boundary-charts"></a>
### C2.1 Partial compactifications of boundary torsors

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/partial-boundary-charts -->

Construct first the ambient analytic torus embedding Y for the C1 boundary datum and its C0 cone system. Its ordinary finite regular split charts are analytifications of the supplied relative toric embeddings, preserving character functions, face opens and orbit strata. The chart used for arithmetic gluing is the controlled open subspace Ubar=Int_Y(closure_Y(U0)) of Pink 6.13, where U0 is the image of the original positive domain X+ in the boundary mixed-domain quotient at the chosen level. U0 is dense and open in Ubar. Retain the actual analytic monoid algebra for nonregular cones and nilpotents in the ambient analytic category; a regular ambient chart does not make Ubar the entire affine chart. (Sources: [PINK-1990](#pink-1990) 6.6–6.17, especially the construction in 6.13 and elementary identifications in 6.14–6.17, printed pp. 98–105.)

Fix a pure Shimura datum (G,X), compact open K and its actual arithmetic component quotients from V0. Cones and lattices are the common toric carriers; the fans are compatible arithmetic-admissible systems from C0. No universal abelian scheme is assumed for this datum.

Complex analytic spaces include nilpotents, structure sheaves, open gluing, fibre products and group actions. These are supplied by ComplexComparisonPartII:C0, with its still-open carrier gap exposed.

Retain the embedding X+ into the boundary domain and its positivity conditions from C1/boundary-incidence. U0 is the original-domain image, not the whole open torus torsor of Y.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C2.partial_boundary_charts` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C2`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Analytify the finite-type relative monoid charts through the owner functor and identify the regular coordinate specializations with L2.

Glue ordinary ambient face opens through torsor transition functions and L3 comparisons. Apply the positive-domain condition to form Int_Y(closure_Y(U0)); restrict every elementary identification to these controlled opens, as in Pink 6.13–6.17. Nonregular ambient charts use the recorded analytic monoid-algebra extension.

Retain the boundary lattice, cusp representative, controlled open and group action for arithmetic gluing. Use L4 to compare ambient strata, then restrict that comparison to Ubar.

This supplies [ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/arithmetic-gluing](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-arithmetic-gluing): Supplies the actual local chart, not an unspecified resolution.

Expose the following API under the same hypotheses:

- `PartialBoundaryChart.openTorsor`: The boundary torus torsor is an equivariant open subspace of the ambient embedding Y; the original positive-domain image U0 is an equivariant dense open subspace of the controlled chart Ubar.
- `PartialBoundaryChart.faceOpen`: A face gives the ordinary ambient open immersion obtained by analytifying the relative toric face map. The elementary identifications used in gluing restrict to the controlled positive-domain opens.
- `PartialBoundaryChart.anchor`: A finite regular trivialized ambient complex chart identifies with the anchor chart, preserving characters and strata. The controlled chart is the specified open subspace inside this identification.
- `PartialBoundaryChart.glue_hom`: Compatible analytic maps on the controlled opens, agreeing under the restricted elementary-identification cocycle, glue uniquely to the arithmetic partial boundary space. Ambient monoid charts retain the nilpotent-preserving analytic structure.

**Checks.**

- `PartialBoundaryChart.zero_test`: The ambient zero cone adds no boundary and returns its torus torsor; restricting to the controlled chart returns U0.
- `PartialBoundaryChart.rankOne_test`: At the standard rank-one cusp the ambient ray chart is A1_C with G_m open, but q=exp(2 pi i z), Im(z)>0 gives the controlled chart |q|<1 with punctured-disc interior. Using all of A1_C as the controlled chart fails.
- `PartialBoundaryChart.nilpotent_test`: For the ambient analytification functor, a nonreduced finite-type base keeps the epsilon class; passing to the reduced manifold fails. This tests the ambient carrier, not nilpotents in the original reduced Shimura domain.
- The ambient zero-cone chart is the original torsor. Its controlled open is U0, rather than necessarily the entire torsor.
- Nilpotent-preserving analytification is required for the ambient functor. At a standard rank-one cusp q=exp(2 pi i z), Im(z)>0 gives U0={0<|q|<1} inside the ambient C*; Ubar={|q|<1} inside the ambient C.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C1/roadmap/boundary-torsor-tower](#shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-boundary-torsor-tower); [ShimuraCompactifications:C1/roadmap/cusp-label](#shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-cusp-label); [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/relative-torus-embedding](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-relative-torus-embedding); [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/relative-face-open](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-relative-face-open); `ComplexComparisonPartII:C0/repair-analytification`; [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-0-the-toric-compatible-algebraic-supplier](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-0-the-toric-compatible-algebraic-supplier); [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-2-affine-analytic-charts-of-regular-cones](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-2-affine-analytic-charts-of-regular-cones); [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-3-finite-fan-analytic-gluing](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-3-finite-fan-analytic-gluing); [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-4-torus-actions-strata-and-the-boundary](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-4-torus-actions-strata-and-the-boundary); [ShimuraCompactifications:C1/roadmap/boundary-incidence](#shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-boundary-incidence).

Attribution: The ambient torus embedding precedes the interior-of-closure positive-domain restriction in 6.13; 6.14–6.17 give the elementary identifications used after restricting to that open.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-quotient-separation"></a>
### C2.2 Arithmetic quotient neighbourhoods and separation

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/quotient-separation -->

The elementary relation on partial boundary charts admits actual local quotient neighbourhoods by the cusp normalizer, has closed graph, and yields a Hausdorff complex analytic quotient with finite effective local stabilizers. Quotient invariant sheaves supply the local analytic structure. Neatness removes the effective finite stabilizer at smooth cone charts; arbitrary level retains the finite quotient, without asserting smoothness. (Sources: [PINK-1990](#pink-1990) 6.20–6.24, printed pp. 107–112.)

Fix a pure Shimura datum (G,X), compact open K and its actual arithmetic component quotients from V0. Cones and lattices are the common toric carriers; the fans are compatible arithmetic-admissible systems from C0. No universal abelian scheme is assumed for this datum.

Use Pink's controlled neighbourhoods V1→V2→V3 in the rational Satake space, with V2/satake-compactness and its local topology/reduction contract.

The ineffective center is removed through the specified quotient action; finiteness of the full cusp group is not assumed.

The elementary relation is on the controlled positive-domain opens Ubar of partial-boundary-charts, not on every point of the full ambient torus embedding.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C2.quotient_separation` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C2`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Pink 6.19 finite effective overlap gives discontinuity on the cone charts; use the controlled Satake neighbourhoods of 6.22 rather than all boundary completions.

Identify the chart equivalence relation on those neighbourhoods with the cusp-normalizer action; Pink 6.23 then proves its graph closed.

Construct the invariant analytic sheaf on the finite local quotient. Closed graph and the local separation results prove Hausdorffness; smoothness requires the neat/regular hypotheses.

**Checks.**

- A finite nontrivial stabilizer may give a normal singular quotient.
- Closed graph is a proved input, not an automatic consequence of finite cone orbits.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/partial-boundary-charts](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-partial-boundary-charts); [ShimuraCompactifications:C1/roadmap/arithmetic-stabilizer](#shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-arithmetic-stabilizer); `ShimuraVarieties:V2/satake-compactness`; `ComplexComparisonPartII:C0/repair-analytification`; [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-3-finite-fan-analytic-gluing](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-3-finite-fan-analytic-gluing); `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.1`.

Attribution: Controlled neighbourhood quotient and closed graph; AMRT neighbourhood proof is a recorded source leaf.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-arithmetic-gluing"></a>
### C2.3 Arithmetic toroidal gluing

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/arithmetic-gluing -->

Glue the local arithmetic quotient charts by Pink's rational conjugation, adelic-level and nested-boundary transitions to construct the actual analytic toroidal space Sh_K^tor(Sigma). It contains the original Sh_K(C), has the cone/cusp orbit stratification, and agrees with each controlled quotient neighbourhood. The gluing cocycle and effective quotient are part of the construction. (Sources: [PINK-1990](#pink-1990) 6.10–6.12 and 6.24, printed pp. 100–102 and 112.)

Fix a pure Shimura datum (G,X), compact open K and its actual arithmetic component quotients from V0. Cones and lattices are the common toric carriers; the fans are compatible arithmetic-admissible systems from C0. No universal abelian scheme is assumed for this datum.

Use the actual analytic-space category, quotient charts and their open transition maps. Completeness is not needed for the local construction; it is needed for compactness.

Glue the controlled opens Ubar=Int_Y(closure_Y(U0)) and their restricted elementary identifications from Pink 6.13; the ambient monoid charts alone are not the gluing domains.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C2.arithmetic_gluing` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C2`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Form the disjoint union of partial charts with their original interior and the elementary equivalence relation of Pink 6.10–6.12.

Use quotient-separation to descend structure sheaves and open charts through the controlled local equivalence relation.

Apply open analytic gluing and the transition cocycle to obtain the space and its atlas; identify the original interior and the cusp/cone orbit strata.

This supplies [ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/minimal-boundary-map](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-minimal-boundary-map): Constructs the source of the comparison to V2's minimal compactification.

This supplies [ShimuraCompactifications:C3](#shimuracompactifications-c3): Constructs sources and targets of refinement maps.

Expose the following API under the same hypotheses:

- `ToroidalSpace.chart`: Each controlled arithmetic quotient neighbourhood embeds as its stated open chart.
- `ToroidalSpace.interior`: The original analytic Shimura quotient is the given open subspace.
- `ToroidalSpace.strata`: The strata are the actual cone-label quotients with the nested-boundary incidence rule.
- `ToroidalSpace.chart_overlap`: Transition maps on a triple overlap satisfy the cocycle inherited from elementary label actions.
- `ToroidalSpace.descend_hom`: Compatible invariant maps on the controlled quotient charts, agreeing under all elementary boundary/level identifications, descend uniquely to the glued arithmetic toroidal quotient.

**Checks.**

- `ToroidalSpace.compact_interior_test`: If the pure Shimura variety has no rational boundary, the toroidal space is the interior.
- `ToroidalSpace.modular_q_test`: A modular cusp of width w gives the usual punctured q-disc plus its q=0 point.
- `ToroidalSpace.anchor_test`: A trivialized finite regular chart has the anchor's face-open overlap rather than an unrelated gluing.
- An interior point is not identified with an unrelated cusp representative.
- The construction depends on Sigma through the specified chart system.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/partial-boundary-charts](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-partial-boundary-charts); [ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/quotient-separation](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-quotient-separation); [ShimuraCompactifications:C1/roadmap/boundary-incidence](#shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-boundary-incidence); [ShimuraCompactifications:C1/roadmap/cusp-label](#shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-cusp-label); `ComplexComparisonPartII:C0/repair-analytification`; [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-3-finite-fan-analytic-gluing](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-3-finite-fan-analytic-gluing).

Attribution: Actual quotient/gluing construction.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-normal-open-dense"></a>
### C2.4 Normality and the dense open interior

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/normal-open-dense -->

The actual analytic toroidal space is normal and contains Sh_K(C) as an open dense analytic subspace. Nonregular rational saturated monoid charts remain allowed. Normality comes from normal toric chart algebras and finite invariant quotients, not from assuming a smooth compactification exists. (Sources: [PINK-1990](#pink-1990) 6.7 and 6.24, printed pp. 98–99 and 112.)

Fix a pure Shimura datum (G,X), compact open K and its actual arithmetic component quotients from V0. Cones and lattices are the common toric carriers; the fans are compatible arithmetic-admissible systems from C0. No universal abelian scheme is assumed for this datum.

Complex boundary bases are the actual smooth/normal mixed quotient bases; dual monoids are saturated in their character lattices.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C2.normal_open_dense` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C2`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Use integral saturated monoid normality on each complex chart and normality of the boundary base.

Finite stabilizer invariant rings/sheaves preserve normality. Descend this through the quotient neighbourhood atlas.

The ordinary torus is schematically dense in each monoid chart; transport the interior identification through the elementary chart relation.

**Checks.**

- A singular saturated cone gives a normal chart without being smooth.
- Normality is not claimed over every nonnormal coefficient ring of C0.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/arithmetic-gluing](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-arithmetic-gluing); [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/arbitrary-ring-toric-charts](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-arbitrary-ring-toric-charts); [ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/quotient-separation](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-quotient-separation); `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.0`; `ComplexComparisonPartII:C0/repair-analytification`.

Attribution: Normal finite quotient charts and their open dense interior.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-compactness-properness"></a>
### C2.5 Complete admissible fans give proper toroidal models

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/compactness-properness -->

If the cusp fan system is complete and admissible, the analytic toroidal space is compact. Once algebraized, the resulting algebraic model is proper over its characteristic-zero field. Finite arithmetic orbit control, complete cusp support and the compact minimal/Satake base are all retained. (Sources: [PINK-1990](#pink-1990) 6.27, printed pp. 114–115.)

Fix a pure Shimura datum (G,X), compact open K and its actual arithmetic component quotients from V0. Cones and lattices are the common toric carriers; the fans are compatible arithmetic-admissible systems from C0. No universal abelian scheme is assumed for this datum.

The system is complete with the required finite cone/cusp orbit conditions. Use the algebraization theorem when asserting algebraic properness.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C2.compactness_properness` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C2`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Map the constructed space to the compact rational Satake quotient.

Pink 6.27 uses controlled quotient charts and the torus valuation/support criterion to prove compactness above a finite cover of the Satake base.

Compactness over C supplies analytic properness to a point, and also properness of the comparison to the Hausdorff minimal space. After a separate algebraization, apply the owner's properness comparison; no integral properness follows from this step.

**Checks.**

- Deleting a needed boundary cone can destroy compactness.
- Properness of an integral C5 model requires its own valuative argument.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/arithmetic-gluing](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-arithmetic-gluing); [ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/minimal-boundary-map](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-minimal-boundary-map); [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/relative-fan-properness](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-relative-fan-properness); `ShimuraVarieties:V2/satake-compactness`; `ComplexComparisonPartII:C2`.

Attribution: Compactness proof for complete admissible arithmetic cone systems.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-smooth-normal-crossings"></a>
### C2.6 Neat smooth fans and normal crossings

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/smooth-normal-crossings -->

At neat level with a smooth admissible fan, the analytic toroidal space is smooth and its boundary is a normal-crossings divisor in the local sense. A global simple/no-self-intersection assertion requires the separate face no-self-identification condition. Compare its regular quotient coordinates with the ordinary coordinate hyperplanes supplied by L4. (Sources: [PINK-1990](#pink-1990) 6.26; 9.20–9.21, printed pp. 113–114 and 156–157.)

Fix a pure Shimura datum (G,X), compact open K and its actual arithmetic component quotients from V0. Cones and lattices are the common toric carriers; the fans are compatible arithmetic-admissible systems from C0. No universal abelian scheme is assumed for this datum.

K is neat; every cone is regular in the actual central lattice. For the stronger global branch separation impose Pink 7.12(*)/9.20 explicitly.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C2.smooth_normal_crossings` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C2`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Identify each regular torsor chart with polynomial/Laurent coordinates over the smooth boundary base.

Apply the effective stabilizer result at neat level to preserve smoothness and local coordinate branches under the quotient.

The boundary is locally a union of coordinate hyperplanes. Trace face identifications separately before asserting global simple normal crossings.

**Checks.**

- Simplicial nonregular cones do not satisfy this smoothness theorem.
- Two locally separate branches can still be globally identified without the extra fan condition.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/arithmetic-gluing](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-arithmetic-gluing); [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/relative-regular-coordinates](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-relative-regular-coordinates); [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/relative-boundary-coordinates](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-relative-boundary-coordinates); [ShimuraCompactifications:C1/roadmap/arithmetic-stabilizer](#shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-arithmetic-stabilizer); [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-4-torus-actions-strata-and-the-boundary](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-4-torus-actions-strata-and-the-boundary); `ComplexComparisonPartII:C0/repair-analytification`.

Attribution: Local smoothness and extra refinement for no self-identification.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-projective-algebraization"></a>
### C2.7 Algebraization for projective admissible fans

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/projective-algebraization -->

A smooth projective admissible fan at neat level gives a projective algebraization of the complete analytic toroidal space, whose ample line comes from the invariant piecewise-linear polarization. For arbitrary level construct the finite quotient algebraic model and its descended ample power. For a general admissible fan without the ample-cover condition construct the algebraic-space algebraization; do not call every proper toroidal algebraic space a projective scheme. (Sources: [PINK-1990](#pink-1990) 9.24; 12.4–12.5, printed pp. 158–159 and 197–198.)

Fix a pure Shimura datum (G,X), compact open K and its actual arithmetic component quotients from V0. Cones and lattices are the common toric carriers; the fans are compatible arithmetic-admissible systems from C0. No universal abelian scheme is assumed for this datum.

For the projective conclusion use complete projective admissible Sigma. The general algebraic-space conclusion uses the effective analytic/algebraic descent contract and Pink's local ample charts.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C2.projective_algebraization` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C2`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Pink 9.18–9.24 attach polarization line bundles to the arithmetic torus charts and prove their quotient compatibility.

An invariant ample power gives the neat projective realization through the complex comparison owner's Chow/GAGA algebraization theorem.

For a non-neat level descend from a normal neat subgroup through a finite quotient; for nonprojective systems use algebraic-space charts and Pink 12.5 instead of assuming an ample global line.

**Checks.**

- Projectivity records an actual ample line, not only compactness.
- A nonprojective complete fan is not forced into a projective scheme.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/arithmetic-gluing](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-arithmetic-gluing); [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/smooth-projective-refinement](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-smooth-projective-refinement); [ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/normal-open-dense](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-normal-open-dense); `ComplexComparisonPartII:C2`; `ComplexComparisonPartII:C4`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.1`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.3`; [ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/compactness-properness](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-compactness-properness).

Attribution: Scheme algebraization needs ample cover; algebraic spaces have the unconditional version.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-minimal-boundary-map"></a>
### C2.8 Comparison with the existing minimal compactification

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/minimal-boundary-map -->

Construct the continuous analytic comparison from the actual toroidal quotient to the already-owned minimal compactification. On each boundary cone stratum it is the map through the associated pure boundary quotient; its cone-stratum restriction is the specified torus/abelian-torsor quotient map. Preserve the cusp equivalence classes and incidence order; do not assume each entire boundary fibre or stratum is irreducible. Properness is a later consequence of compactness-properness for complete fans and a Hausdorff minimal target; its algebraic form is obtained after projective-algebraization. Neither conclusion is an input to constructing the continuous map. (Sources: [PINK-1990](#pink-1990) 6.21–6.24, printed pp. 109–112; 7.2–7.5, printed pp. 118–121.)

Fix a pure Shimura datum (G,X), compact open K and its actual arithmetic component quotients from V0. Cones and lattices are the common toric carriers; the fans are compatible arithmetic-admissible systems from C0. No universal abelian scheme is assumed for this datum.

Use V2's actual Baily–Borel/Satake algebraization and its boundary strata. The source toroidal model is the C2 construction, not a resolution chosen from an existence theorem.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C2.minimal_boundary_map` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C2`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

The ordinary partial embedding has the canonical map to its pure boundary quotient.

Pink 6.22–6.24 show these maps agree under elementary identifications and are continuous for the rational Satake topology; glue them on the constructed quotient.

Identify the cone-stratum restriction using Pink 7.2–7.5. The continuous comparison is constructed before compactness. Once compactness-properness and algebraization have been established, the same map is proper and the proper-morphism GAGA interface algebraizes it; these are subsequent consequences, not prerequisites of this construction.

**Checks.**

- A cone-label quotient maps to its existing pure boundary stratum.
- The map is independent of the chosen cusp representative.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/arithmetic-gluing](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-arithmetic-gluing); [ShimuraCompactifications:C1/roadmap/boundary-torsor-tower](#shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-boundary-torsor-tower); [ShimuraCompactifications:C1/roadmap/boundary-incidence](#shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-boundary-incidence); `ShimuraVarieties:V2/baily-borel`; `ComplexComparisonPartII:C4`.

Attribution: The toroidal-to-minimal map and actual quotient strata.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-canonical-toroidal-model"></a>
### C2.9 Canonical models of toroidal compactifications

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/canonical-toroidal-model -->

For a datum class with the actual pure canonical model and the special mixed-boundary canonical torsors supplied, descend the constructed toroidal algebraization to its reflex field E. The canonical model restricts to the supplied pure canonical model and has the specified completed boundary torsor charts. It is a scheme when Pink 12.4's ample-cover condition holds and otherwise an algebraic space as in 12.5. Canonical-model uniqueness on the dense interior determines morphisms after existence; it does not by itself construct boundary descent. (Sources: [PINK-1990](#pink-1990) 12.1–12.8, printed pp. 196–200.)

Fix a pure Shimura datum (G,X), compact open K and its actual arithmetic component quotients from V0. Cones and lattices are the common toric carriers; the fans are compatible arithmetic-admissible systems from C0. No universal abelian scheme is assumed for this datum.

Supply the canonical models of the actual boundary pure data and their special mixed torus/abelian torsors, reflex-field compatibility and dense special mixed points. V8 supplies pure functoriality; its general completion is instantiated only in C2.general.

The fan is rational and compatible with the descent actions. Scheme effectivity requires the explicit ample-line-cover hypothesis.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C2.canonical_toroidal_model` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C2`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Pink 12.1 identifies boundary reflex fields. Compare Galois transports of the special mixed canonical torsor charts and their formal neighbourhoods.

Pink 12.6–12.8 use special mixed-point density and normality to extend the descent isomorphisms from the interior; check the overlap cocycle on the actual charts.

Descend as an algebraic space by effective descent. Use the ample cover of 12.4 only for scheme effectivity and identify the restricted pure model by V8 uniqueness. The unread density proof 12.13–12.17 is exposed as a supplier/source gap.

This supplies [ShimuraCompactifications:C2.general](#shimuracompactifications-c2-general): Instantiates the same construction with general pure canonical models.

This supplies [ShimuraCompactifications:C3](#shimuracompactifications-c3): Provides algebraic sources and targets for descended refinement and Hecke maps.

Expose the following API under the same hypotheses:

- `ToroidalCanonicalModel.interior`: Restriction to Sh_K is the actual canonical model over E.
- `ToroidalCanonicalModel.boundaryCompletion`: Each labelled completed neighbourhood is the specified mixed canonical torsor embedding over E.
- `ToroidalCanonicalModel.descent_cocycle`: The Galois descent isomorphisms obey the cocycle and preserve the labelled boundary maps.
- `ToroidalCanonicalModel.unique`: Two effective descent models with the prescribed complex comparison and mixed boundary canonical identifications have the unique comparison isomorphism compatible with those data, by the specified descent/uniqueness theorem.

**Checks.**

- `ToroidalCanonicalModel.empty_boundary_test`: For a compact Shimura variety the construction is its original canonical model.
- `ToroidalCanonicalModel.baseChange_C_test`: Base change and analytification recover the C2 toroidal quotient with its original charts.
- `ToroidalCanonicalModel.modular_cusp_test`: For a modular cusp the descended completed ring is the cyclotomic cusp ring with its width-normalized q parameter.
- The dense-open pure canonical model agrees with the supplied one.
- An arbitrary formal chart isomorphism is not taken as an ordinary open overlap.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/projective-algebraization](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-projective-algebraization); [ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/minimal-boundary-map](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-minimal-boundary-map); `ShimuraVarieties:V8`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.1`; `ComplexComparisonPartII:C4`.

Attribution: Construction and effectivity conditions; density proof is explicitly not claimed read.

### Examples

- The zero cone recovers the original open torsor and its finite complex anchor chart.
- Two arithmetic translates that overlap must use the controlled quotient relation, rather than an arbitrary equivalence relation on chart points.
- A regular neat chart has a boundary locally cut out by x₁⋯xᵣ=0; identifying two branches globally prevents the stronger simple-boundary conclusion.

### Dependencies

Alongside the local target chain, this layer consumes `ComplexComparisonPartII:C0/repair-analytification`; `ComplexComparisonPartII:C2`; `ComplexComparisonPartII:C4`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.0`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.1`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.3`; `ShimuraVarieties:V2/baily-borel`; `ShimuraVarieties:V2/satake-compactness`; `ShimuraVarieties:V8`; [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-0-the-toric-compatible-algebraic-supplier](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-0-the-toric-compatible-algebraic-supplier); [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-2-affine-analytic-charts-of-regular-cones](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-2-affine-analytic-charts-of-regular-cones); [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-3-finite-fan-analytic-gluing](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-3-finite-fan-analytic-gluing); [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-4-torus-actions-strata-and-the-boundary](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-4-torus-actions-strata-and-the-boundary).

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c2-general"></a>
## Layer C2.general: General-data completion interface

Apply C2 to the general pure canonical tower and minimal models of V8.general. The construction uses the same rational boundary and cone labels, and requests canonical special mixed torsors for that datum class. Its output retains the distinction between an algebraic space and a scheme obtained through an ample cover. It supplies neither a universal abelian scheme nor an integral bad-prime model.

**Milestones.**

- [General toroidal canonical model](#shimuracompactifications-c2-general-roadmap-general-toroidal-descent)

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c2-general-roadmap-general-toroidal-descent"></a>
### C2.general.1 Toroidal canonical models for general pure data

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C2.general/roadmap/general-toroidal-descent -->

Instantiate the C2 descent construction for every pure Shimura datum using V8.general's actual general canonical tower/minimal models and the requested general boundary mixed canonical torsors. Preserve the existing rational boundary, cusp stabilizer and cone labels, together with the algebraic-space versus ample-cover scheme distinction. This adds no universal abelian scheme and no integral model at bad primes. (Sources: [PINK-1990](#pink-1990) 12.4–12.5, printed pp. 197–198.)

Fix a pure Shimura datum (G,X), compact open K and its actual arithmetic component quotients from V0. Cones and lattices are the common toric carriers; the fans are compatible arithmetic-admissible systems from C0. No universal abelian scheme is assumed for this datum.

Use ShimuraVarieties:V8.general/general-tower and general-minimal, plus the special mixed-boundary canonical supplier in exactly the datum class needed by Pink 12.4–12.5.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C2_general.general_toroidal_descent` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C2/general`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Specialize the pure canonical and minimal inputs of canonical-toroidal-model to V8.general.

Identify each boundary pure datum and its canonical special mixed torsors with the unchanged C1 labels; the remaining mixed-model source leaf is recorded, not absorbed into general pure uniqueness.

Apply the same effective descent and ample-cover criterion, comparing the finite complex charts through L0/L2/L3/L4.

**Checks.**

- The boundary pure datum is unchanged by choosing V7 auxiliary canonical-model data.
- No PEL moduli family is introduced for a general datum.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/canonical-toroidal-model](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-canonical-toroidal-model); `ShimuraVarieties:V8.general/general-tower`; `ShimuraVarieties:V8.general/general-minimal`; [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-0-the-toric-compatible-algebraic-supplier](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-0-the-toric-compatible-algebraic-supplier); [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-2-affine-analytic-charts-of-regular-cones](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-2-affine-analytic-charts-of-regular-cones); [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-3-finite-fan-analytic-gluing](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-3-finite-fan-analytic-gluing); [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-4-torus-actions-strata-and-the-boundary](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-4-torus-actions-strata-and-the-boundary).

### Examples

- The identity datum map agrees with C2 on the unchanged charts.

### Dependencies

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c3"></a>
## Layer C3: Refinements and Hecke maps

A compatible fan map gives the actual toroidal morphism; it is not automatically finite. Same-level subdivisions, changed levels, datum maps and Hecke spans have different degree and descent conditions. Common refinements identify choices through proper comparison maps.

The structure-sheaf equality, higher structure-sheaf vanishing and derived boundary-ideal comparison are three distinct results. Their proof uses the integral character-graded toric Čech complex. The boundary ideal is the ideal of the union of divisors, including over a nonreduced coefficient base. Canonical and subcanonical coefficient cohomology then follows with the exact bundle hypotheses. Ordinary Igusa formal charts and the compactified Klingen first projection retain their separate local-factorization and completion obligations.

**Milestones.**

- [Toroidal refinement map](#shimuracompactifications-c3-roadmap-refinement-map)
- [Toroidal Hecke correspondence](#shimuracompactifications-c3-roadmap-hecke-span)
- [Toric refinement vanishing](#shimuracompactifications-c3-roadmap-toric-structure-sheaf-vanishing)
- [Boundary ideal pushforward](#shimuracompactifications-c3-roadmap-refinement-boundary-ideal)
- [Cohomology independence of the fan](#shimuracompactifications-c3-roadmap-coherent-cohomology-invariance)

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c3-roadmap-refinement-map"></a>
### C3.1 Proper arithmetic refinement maps

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C3/roadmap/refinement-map -->

For a compatible refinement Sigma′ of Sigma at fixed datum and level, construct the canonical proper map Sh_K^tor(Sigma′)→Sh_K^tor(Sigma), restricting to the identity on the interior and to the shared ordinary toric refinement maps in each finite regular split chart. Identity and composition are canonical equalities of maps. The C5 integral version uses its actual degeneration-chart atlas and is a separate instantiation of this map contract. (Sources: [PINK-1990](#pink-1990) 6.25 and 9.22–9.23, printed pp. 112–113 and 157–158; [LAN-2021](#lan-2021) 6.4.2.2–6.4.2.3; 7.1.1.4, printed pp. 524–525 and 532.)

Fix a pure Shimura datum (G,X), compact open K and its actual arithmetic component quotients from V0. Cones and lattices are the common toric carriers; the fans are compatible arithmetic-admissible systems from C0. No universal abelian scheme is assumed for this datum.

Sigma′ refines Sigma with unchanged cusp support. Use the actual C2 algebraic models, or the C5 integral models, and compatible boundary torsor data.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C3.refinement_map` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C3`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Define the chart maps by the common character-monoid inclusion and the supplied finite-chart L5 map.

The elementary rational, boundary and level identifications commute with these chart maps; descend and glue them on the arithmetic models.

Support equality gives properness on local charts. Identity/composition hold there and hence globally, with canonical descent over the reflex field.

This supplies [ShimuraCompactifications:C3/roadmap/coherent-cohomology-invariance](#shimuracompactifications-c3-roadmap-coherent-cohomology-invariance): Supplies the actual map for derived sheaf comparison.

Expose the following API under the same hypotheses:

- `ToroidalRefinement.interior`: The open restriction is the identity of the original Shimura model.
- `ToroidalRefinement.comp`: Maps for successive refinements compose to the map for the composite refinement.
- `ToroidalRefinement.proper`: The map is proper under the stated support and model hypotheses.
- `ToroidalRefinement.id`: The map for the identity refinement is the identity of the actual toroidal model.

**Checks.**

- `ToroidalRefinement.identity_test`: Refinement by the identical cone system gives the identity map.
- `ToroidalRefinement.star_test`: The (1,1) star subdivision of the quadrant is the blow-up of A² at the origin.
- `ToroidalRefinement.anchor_test`: On a finite regular complex chart the map is exactly the supplied L5 monomial map.
- A nontrivial star subdivision gives a proper birational map, not an isomorphism.
- The zero refinement is the identity.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/canonical-toroidal-model](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-canonical-toroidal-model); [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/relative-fan-properness](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-relative-fan-properness); [ShimuraCompactifications:C1/roadmap/cusp-label](#shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-cusp-label); [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-0-the-toric-compatible-algebraic-supplier](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-0-the-toric-compatible-algebraic-supplier); [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-3-finite-fan-analytic-gluing](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-3-finite-fan-analytic-gluing); [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-4-torus-actions-strata-and-the-boundary](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-4-torus-actions-strata-and-the-boundary); [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-5-toric-maps-and-properness](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-5-toric-maps-and-properness).

Attribution: Functorial compatible fan maps and refinements.

Attribution: Integral instantiation and proper chart maps.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c3-roadmap-level-datum-functoriality"></a>
### C3.2 Compatible level and datum maps

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C3/roadmap/level-datum-functoriality -->

Extend a specified level map, rational datum map or Hecke translation to the toroidal models when its induced central lattice maps send every source cone into a target cone. Maps are over the stated reflex-field compositum, preserve labelled boundary strata, and satisfy the source's ordered composition laws. The corresponding normal-level finite quotient is constructed under its exact invariant-fan hypotheses; a level map is not declared etale at the boundary from interior etaleness. (Sources: [PINK-1990](#pink-1990) 6.25, printed pp. 112–113.)

Fix a pure Shimura datum (G,X), compact open K and its actual arithmetic component quotients from V0. Cones and lattices are the common toric carriers; the fans are compatible arithmetic-admissible systems from C0. No universal abelian scheme is assumed for this datum.

Provide compatible source and target fans for this particular map; use V8's pure finite-level/datum/translation maps and the actual C1 boundary lattice maps.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C3.level_datum_functoriality` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C3`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Use the induced monomial/torsor chart maps and Pink 6.25 compatibility with elementary identifications.

Descend and glue on the actual models, checking the same interior map and the boundary cone labels.

Prove composition and normal-level quotient by local chart comparison plus dense-open uniqueness. Record any boundary ramification in the lattice map.

**Checks.**

- The Tate cusp map q↦q^p has boundary ramification even though its generic torus map is etale in characteristic zero.
- A datum map carries the specified reflex-field base change.

### C3.3 Toroidal Hecke correspondences

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C3/roadmap/hecke-span -->

For a specified Hecke double coset choose compatible fans on its intermediate level and the two endpoints, and construct the ordered toroidal span extending V8's open Hecke span. A common refinement compares any two choices. The construction never claims one fixed fan admits every Hecke operator. (Sources: [PINK-1990](#pink-1990) 6.25 and 9.23, printed pp. 112–113 and 157–158.)

Fix a pure Shimura datum (G,X), compact open K and its actual arithmetic component quotients from V0. Cones and lattices are the common toric carriers; the fans are compatible arithmetic-admissible systems from C0. No universal abelian scheme is assumed for this datum.

Use the two actual maps at the intersection level K intersect gKg^-1 with the ordered translation convention of V8/hecke-span.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C3.hecke_span` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C3`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Apply the finite-family compatible-refinement theorem to the two boundary lattice maps.

Construct both toroidal arrows by level-datum-functoriality, preserving their open restrictions.

Compare different intermediate fans through refinement-map and identify the spans after refinement.

This supplies `AutomorphicBundles:B3`: Supplies the correspondence geometry for canonical/cuspidal coefficient maps.

This supplies `IntegralCoherentHeckeComplexes`: Imports the actual refined span before defining trace maps.

Expose the following API under the same hypotheses:

- `ToroidalHeckeSpan.openRestriction`: The interior span is V8's ordered Hecke span.
- `ToroidalHeckeSpan.commonRefinement`: Two compatible fan choices compare through a canonical refined span.
- `ToroidalHeckeSpan.boundaryMap`: Each arrow maps its cone stratum through the stated cusp/lattice map.

**Checks.**

- `ToroidalHeckeSpan.identity_test`: The identity double coset gives the identity span after the identity fan choice.
- `ToroidalHeckeSpan.q_power_test`: A rank-one p-isogeny cusp arrow has the prescribed q↦q^p or q′^p=q map, not an etale extension by assumption.
- `ToroidalHeckeSpan.refinement_test`: A further intermediate refinement leaves the open Hecke span unchanged.
- The order of the two arrows agrees with the open correspondence.
- An incompatible fan is first refined.

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C3/roadmap/choice-comparison -->

Any two admissible toroidal choices at fixed datum/level admit proper comparison maps from a common compatible refinement. Further common refinements give the same comparison diagrams. Choice independence means these maps and induced sheaf/cohomology identifications; it never means literal equality of all toroidal compactifications. (Sources: [PINK-1990](#pink-1990) 9.22–9.23, printed pp. 157–158.)

Fix a pure Shimura datum (G,X), compact open K and its actual arithmetic component quotients from V0. Cones and lattices are the common toric carriers; the fans are compatible arithmetic-admissible systems from C0. No universal abelian scheme is assumed for this datum.

Both fans satisfy the stated support and compatibility requirements.

Apply compatible-common-refinement and construct the two refinement maps.

Apply the identity/composition API to compare two common refinements through a third.

Use the resulting commutative diagrams for the sheaf comparison theorems, separating map existence from higher-direct-image vanishing.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c3-roadmap-toric-structure-sheaf-vanishing"></a>
### C3.5 Universal toric structure-sheaf acyclicity

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C3/roadmap/toric-structure-sheaf-vanishing -->

For a finite rational fan subdivision Sigma′→Sigma with equal support, the proper toric map over any commutative ring R satisfies O→pi_*O as an isomorphism and R^i pi_*O=0 for i>0. State the two assertions independently. The integral monomial Cech proof is universal in R; finite regular complex maps supplied by L5 alone do not contain this cohomological theorem. (Sources: [LAN-2021](#lan-2021) 7.1.1.4 and proof, printed pp. 532–533; [LAN-2017](#lan-2017) Proposition 7.5, proof (7.13)–(7.14), printed pp. 28–29; [PILLONI-2020](#pilloni-2020) 3.4, proof of Proposition 3.4.1, author-copy pp. 15–16.)

Use the arbitrary-ring C0 toric realization. Check finite subdivision and support equality; the source need not be asserted smooth for the universal monomial argument.

The required integral graded Cech exactness/contractibility lemma is a recorded proof leaf; the source quotation establishes the smooth-refinement applications, and the stronger universal argument must be verified before implementation.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C3.toric_structure_sheaf_vanishing` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C3`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Localize on an affine target cone and cover its subdivision inverse image by finitely many affine cone charts.

Grade the monomial Cech complex by the character lattice. Identify each weight complex with the integral relative polyhedral nerve complex and prove its augmentation exact with torsion-free/split terms.

Tensor that exact graded complex with arbitrary R, identify degree-zero functions and higher cohomology, then sheafify. The unread KKMS Ch. I §3 Corollaries on p44 remain an explicit source leaf.

**Checks.**

- For the blow-up of the quadrant, pi_*O=O and positive higher direct images vanish.
- Normality alone proves neither universal coefficient change nor higher vanishing.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/arbitrary-ring-toric-charts](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-arbitrary-ring-toric-charts); [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/relative-fan-properness](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-relative-fan-properness); `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.2`; [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-5-toric-maps-and-properness](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-5-toric-maps-and-properness).

### C3.8 Boundary ideal and its derived pushforward

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C3/roadmap/refinement-boundary-ideal -->

For same-level actual toroidal refinements, f_*I_D′=I_D and R^i f_*I_D′=0 for i>0. On a relative toric chart use the strict-positive monomial ideal of the UNION of boundary divisors, distinct from the face ideal of a closed stratum. Over a nonreduced coefficient base this is the base-changed boundary ideal, not its radical. On the good integral models it agrees with the stated reduced Cartier boundary ideal. In general f*I_D is only a subsheaf of I_D′. Changed-level degree-zero statements instead take the finite deck-group invariants under the normal-level hypothesis below. (Sources: [LAN-2017](#lan-2017) Proposition 7.5, (7.7) and (7.9), printed pp. 28–29; corrected changed-level invariant clause in E3; [PILLONI-2020](#pilloni-2020) 5.3; Theorem 6.1.5.1(2), author-copy pp. 24 and 29.)

Use the same map/chart/coefficient hypotheses as refinement-higher-structure-sheaf. Cartierty of the boundary twist is asserted only for the stated regular toroidal models.

The vanishing concerns the ideal on the source, not a falsely equal pullback ideal.

For changed levels in Lan 7.5, the boundary ideal on the coarser Hprime model is the Hprime/H-invariant source pushforward when H is normal in Hprime; use the corrected group direction of E3.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C3.refinement_boundary_ideal` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C3`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Use the strict-positive character grading in the toric Cech proof, retaining the relative boundary ideal in degree zero.

Lan 2017 Proposition 7.5(7.7),(7.9) descends both assertions through the actual completed toroidal charts.

On regular coordinates compare pullback vanishing orders of the reduced boundary with the new exceptional ray; the blow-up test rejects the printed pullback equality in Pilloni E40.

**Checks.**

- For the quadrant the boundary-union ideal is (xy), whereas its closed-stratum ideal is (x,y).
- Changed-level degree-zero identification takes Hprime/H-invariants; it does not identify the entire source boundary-ideal pushforward with the target ideal.

### Examples

- Blow up the origin in A² with D={xy=0}. The total pullback is D_strict+2E, while the new boundary is D_strict+E. Thus f*I_D is contained in I_D′ and need not equal it.
- A normal inclusion H⊂H′ gives deck group H′/H for the finer-to-coarser level map. The changed-level pushforward uses its invariants; a same-level refinement uses no such deck group.
- Properness alone does not establish acyclicity of the Klingen first projection. Its corrected boundary-chart factorization must satisfy the toric vanishing theorem.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c3-general"></a>
## Layer C3.general: General-data map and correspondence interface

Descend the compatible C3 maps and spans using the same general canonical tower and mixed-boundary supplier as C2.general. Specify the reflex-field map or compositum for each arrow. Fan compatibility remains a hypothesis, and a span is kept as a span. No independent analytic or canonical model theory is repeated here.

**Milestones.**

- [General toroidal functoriality](#shimuracompactifications-c3-general-roadmap-general-map-descent)

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c3-general-roadmap-general-map-descent"></a>
### C3.general.1 General-data toroidal maps and correspondences

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C3.general/roadmap/general-map-descent -->

Descend the C3 compatible refinement, level, datum and ordered Hecke maps to the C2.general toroidal canonical models, using V8.general functoriality over the prescribed reflex-field composita. Preserve boundary labels and the common-refinement comparisons, and prove identity/composition after descent. The coefficient vanishing theorems retain their own hypotheses and are not strengthened by this general-data instantiation. (Sources: [PINK-1990](#pink-1990) 6.25; 12.4–12.8, printed pp. 112–113 and 197–200.)

Fix a pure Shimura datum (G,X), compact open K and its actual arithmetic component quotients from V0. Cones and lattices are the common toric carriers; the fans are compatible arithmetic-admissible systems from C0. No universal abelian scheme is assumed for this datum.

Use the actual general canonical models and the special mixed-boundary descent input, with compatible source/target cone systems for each specified map.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C3_general.general_map_descent` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C3/general`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Apply the unchanged C3 chart maps to the C2.general models and identify their open restrictions with the V8.general maps.

Use canonical descent of the boundary torsors and dense-interior uniqueness to descend the maps.

Check identity/composition and common-refinement diagrams after complex base change, then reflect equality over the reflex field.

**Checks.**

- A general datum map uses the specified field compositum.
- No all-prime integral extension is added.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C2.general/roadmap/general-toroidal-descent](#shimuracompactifications-c2-general-roadmap-general-toroidal-descent); [ShimuraCompactifications:C3/roadmap/level-datum-functoriality](#shimuracompactifications-c3-roadmap-level-datum-functoriality); [ShimuraCompactifications:C3/roadmap/hecke-span](#shimuracompactifications-c3-roadmap-hecke-span); [ShimuraCompactifications:C3/roadmap/choice-comparison](#shimuracompactifications-c3-roadmap-choice-comparison); `ShimuraVarieties:V8.general/general-tower`; [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-0-the-toric-compatible-algebraic-supplier](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-0-the-toric-compatible-algebraic-supplier); [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-2-affine-analytic-charts-of-regular-cones](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-2-affine-analytic-charts-of-regular-cones); [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-3-finite-fan-analytic-gluing](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-3-finite-fan-analytic-gluing); [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-4-torus-actions-strata-and-the-boundary](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-4-torus-actions-strata-and-the-boundary); [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-5-toric-maps-and-properness](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-5-toric-maps-and-properness).

Attribution: Functorial fan maps and toroidal canonical-model descent.

### Examples

- An identity arrow descends to the identity over its reflex field.
- Composition compares the actual compatible fan maps and field embeddings.
- A two-arrow Hecke span is not replaced by a single everywhere-defined automorphism.
