Alongside the local target chain, this layer consumes `ShimuraVarieties:V8.general/general-tower`; [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-0-the-toric-compatible-algebraic-supplier](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-0-the-toric-compatible-algebraic-supplier); [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-2-affine-analytic-charts-of-regular-cones](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-2-affine-analytic-charts-of-regular-cones); [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-3-finite-fan-analytic-gluing](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-3-finite-fan-analytic-gluing); [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-4-torus-actions-strata-and-the-boundary](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-4-torus-actions-strata-and-the-boundary); [tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/AnalyticToricGeometry#layer-5-toric-maps-and-properness](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCetiRoadmap/blob/main/AnalyticToricGeometry/README.md#layer-5-toric-maps-and-properness).

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c4"></a>
## Layer C4: Degenerations of polarized abelian schemes

A degenerating semiabelian family is defined fibrewise. Its torus rank can jump, so a fixed global torus extension is an extra hypothesis. Import local polarized Raynaud uniformization from R11.3, and extend it to the complete normal-base setting through the relative Poincaré, lattice, trivialization and positivity data. The relative generic 1-motive [Y→G_η] is part of that degeneration datum; it is not a second owner of general motivic theory.

Mumford effectivity, universal cusp families, extension of homomorphisms/endomorphisms and boundary level maps lead to the integral compactifications. Extended isogeny kernels are only quasi-finite flat in the stated boundary situation. The Tate fibre coordinate u and base period q remain distinct. The layer also owns the semiabelian extension of the Hasse determinant, while the generic BT₁ invariant, LF and Hodge–Tate sequence remain R07.2 exports.

**Milestones.**

- [Semi-abelian scheme](#shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-semi-abelian-scheme)
- [Polarized degeneration datum](#shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-polarized-degeneration-data)
- [Mumford degeneration construction](#shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-mumford-quotient)
- [Polarized degeneration equivalence](#shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-degeneration-effectivity)
- [Universal boundary degeneration](#shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-formal-universal-degeneration)

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-semi-abelian-scheme"></a>
### C4.1 Semi-abelian schemes

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C4/roadmap/semi-abelian-scheme -->

A semi-abelian scheme G over S is a separated smooth commutative group scheme whose geometric fibres are extensions of abelian varieties by tori. Keep the relative dimension and constructible character sheaf of the maximal fibrewise torus. A single global exact sequence by a torus exists under the additional locally constant toric-rank hypothesis; it is not part of the definition for a degenerating family. Smoothness supplies local finite presentation. When an application needs global finite presentation or finite type, retain that additional hypothesis in the application rather than inserting it into Lan's fibrewise definition. (Sources: [LAN-2021](#lan-2021) 3.3.1.1–3.3.1.4, printed pp. 191–192.)

Work over the scheme or algebraic-space bases supplied by SF.1, using the existing group-scheme and abelian-scheme carriers. For Lan character/extension theorems retain his locally Noetherian base hypotheses.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C4.semi_abelian_scheme` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C4`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Use the existing smooth separated commutative group-scheme structure and impose the stated geometric-fibre condition.

The maximal tori and their characters are identified fibrewise; descent supplies the constructible character sheaf.

Relate the constant-rank case to an extension by a relative torus and abelian scheme.

This supplies [ShimuraCompactifications:C4/roadmap/polarized-degeneration-data](#shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-polarized-degeneration-data): Supplies the genuine group family reconstructed by degeneration data.

This supplies [ShimuraCompactifications:C5/roadmap/integral-toroidal-space](#shimuracompactifications-c5-roadmap-integral-toroidal-space): Supplies the universal boundary extension.

Expose the following API under the same hypotheses:

- `SemiAbelianScheme.baseChange`: Every base change preserves the geometric-fibre condition and its smooth group structure, with identity/composition comparisons.
- `SemiAbelianScheme.characterSheaf`: Returns the constructible character sheaf with its restriction to each geometric fibre.
- `SemiAbelianScheme.abelianEmbedding`: An existing abelian scheme gives a semi-abelian scheme with zero character sheaf.
- `SemiAbelianScheme.constantRankExtension`: Under the locally constant character-rank hypothesis the family is an extension of an abelian scheme by a torus.
- `SemiAbelianScheme.ofGroup`: A genuine separated smooth commutative group scheme with the stated geometric-fibre torus-extension property gives a semi-abelian scheme, without choosing a global torus extension for a rank-jumping family.
- `SemiAbelianScheme.ext`: On a fixed group-scheme carrier and structure maps, two semi-abelian structures agree because the smooth/separated and geometric-fibre requirements are properties; equality does not require equality of chosen local torus splittings.

**Checks.**

- `SemiAbelianScheme.abelian_test`: An abelian scheme has toric rank zero.
- `SemiAbelianScheme.splitTorus_test`: The pinned rank-r SplitTorus is semi-abelian with character sheaf Z^r and zero abelian quotient.
- `SemiAbelianScheme.tate_rank_test`: The Tate family has generic toric rank zero and boundary toric rank one.
- `SemiAbelianScheme.additive_test`: G_a is excluded: its geometric fibre is not an extension of an abelian variety by a torus.
- A Tate degeneration can have toric rank zero generically and one at the boundary.
- A constant-rank extension has the expected relative torus but a rank-jumping family need not.

*Needs:* `AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli:A2`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.0`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.1`; `tauceti:TauCeti.SplitTorus.groupScheme`.

Attribution: Definition and constructible character data.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-constructible-character-sheaf"></a>
### C4.2 Character sheaves and homomorphisms from tori

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C4/roadmap/constructible-character-sheaf -->

For a semi-abelian scheme G over a locally Noetherian base, construct its etale constructible character sheaf X(G), restricting to the free character lattice of the maximal torus in every geometric fibre. Identify morphisms from a torus T to G with the dual character-sheaf maps in Lan 3.3.1.9, with the exact sheaf direction and specializations retained. On the constant-rank locus this recovers the usual anti-equivalence for tori. (Sources: [LAN-2021](#lan-2021) 3.3.1.8–3.3.1.10, printed pp. 192–193; [FALTINGS-CHAI-1990](#faltings-chai-1990) Chapter I, Proposition 2.9, Theorem 2.10 and Corollary 2.11, printed pp. 11–13 / PDF pp. 23–25.)

Use Lan's constructible etale sheaf setting; no locally constant character lattice is assumed across a rank jump.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C4.constructible_character_sheaf` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C4`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Faltings–Chai I.2.9 extends a generic subtorus over a normal base. On the DVR test bases its characters specialize by the quotient map from special to generic characters.

Use the sheaf construction of I.2.10: sections are fibrewise Galois-invariant characters satisfying the DVR specialization condition. The proof uses reduction to a reduced finite-type base, conductor gluing and Noetherian induction; its auxiliary descent and torus-rigidity inputs must be supplied by the owners.

Compare this sheaf with Lan 3.3.1.9 and check the contravariant torus-to-semi-abelian Hom identification and arbitrary base change. A constructible sheaf may have jumping rank; it is not silently replaced by a local system.

**Checks.**

- For G=T, the comparison is the native contravariant character-lattice Hom.
- For an abelian scheme G, every torus-to-G group homomorphism is zero.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C4/roadmap/semi-abelian-scheme](#shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-semi-abelian-scheme); `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.1`.

Attribution: Character sheaf and torus-to-semi-abelian Hom comparison.

Attribution: Directly inspected character-sheaf construction, specialization and constant-rank global-extension consequence.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-poincare-extension-classification"></a>
### C4.3 Relative torus extensions and Poincare classes

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C4/roadmap/poincare-extension-classification -->

For an abelian scheme A and an isotrivial torus H with finite-free character sheaf X(H), classify commutative group scheme extensions 0→H→G→A→0 by homomorphisms c:X(H)→A^dual. Lan 3.1.5.1 gives an anti-equivalence of the source categories (and, for fixed A,H, the corresponding classification of extension classes). In Lan's convention the rigidified invertible sheaf for chi corresponds to pushout of the torsor by -chi. Retain this negative-character/inverse-Poincare sign in reconstruction, multiplication, base change and duality; dual abelian schemes and biextensions remain A3/A5 inputs. (Sources: [LAN-2021](#lan-2021) Proposition 3.1.5.1, printed p. 183; 4.2.1.7 and 4.2.1.13, printed pp. 210 and 213.)

Use the locally Noetherian base and isotrivial finite-free character data of Lan 4.2.1; do not assert a globally split torus without an etale trivialization.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C4.poincare_extension_classification` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C4`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

For each character chi, take the rigidified invertible sheaf associated to the G_m-torsor obtained by pushout by -chi, as in Lan 3.1.5.1; the group-extension law puts its class in Pic^0=A^dual.

Reconstruct the graded character-line algebra using the Poincare biextension and its coherent multiplication.

Use the rigidifications to check the inverse constructions and contravariance of the character-sheaf description; retain the negative-character sign before defining degeneration trivializations.

**Checks.**

- A trivial c gives the split extension A×T.
- A nonzero c gives a genuinely nontrivial character line, retained by C0 torsor charts.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C4/roadmap/semi-abelian-scheme](#shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-semi-abelian-scheme); [ShimuraCompactifications:C4/roadmap/constructible-character-sheaf](#shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-constructible-character-sheaf); `AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli:A3`; `AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli:A5`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.1`.

Attribution: Anti-equivalence and negative-character pushout; inverse-biextension convention for degeneration data.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-polarized-degeneration-data"></a>
### C4.4 Polarized relative degeneration data

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C4/roadmap/polarized-degeneration-data -->

In the relative setting R,I,S,eta, define the polarized degeneration datum DD_pol of Lan Definition 4.4.6 (using the entries and positivity from 4.2.1.13): an abelian scheme A with polarization lambda_A, isotrivial finite-free sheaves X,Y, an injective phi:Y→X with finite cokernel, maps c:X→A^dual and c^dual:Y→A satisfying lambda_A c^dual=c phi, and a bilinear rigidified trivialization tau of the pullback of the INVERSE Poincare biextension on the generic fibre. Require its cubical/symmetry relations and the I-adic positivity condition: diagonal trivializations yield sections vanishing along I for nonzero y. The period lattice lives on the generic fibre; it is not silently an everywhere-defined map Y→G over S. Its associated generic polarized 1-motive is the complex [Y to G_eta], where G is the torus extension determined by c and the lift of c^dual is the period map determined by tau. This names the degeneration datum already being constructed, not a second generic motivic theory. This polarized tuple omits the additional ample-line and Y-action fields L^sharp and psi of DD_ample; when Mumford reconstruction uses them, derive the auxiliary data as in Remark 4.4.7, keeping track that its displayed choice induces 2 lambda_A. (Sources: [LAN-2021](#lan-2021) Definition 4.4.6 and Remark 4.4.7, printed p. 251; 4.2.1.7 and 4.2.1.13–4.2.1.14, printed pp. 210 and 213–214.)

Let R be a Noetherian normal domain, complete for a radical ideal I, S=Spec R and eta=Spec Frac R. Use the isotrivial torus hypothesis and the cubical rigidifications of Lan 4.1 and 4.2.1.1; this relative setting is stronger and more general in its base than the complete-DVR local R11.3 specialization.

Retain Lan's ampleness/polarization, positivity and base-change conditions. Use the same local lattice and Raynaud carriers as R11.3 when R is a complete DVR.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C4.polarized_degeneration_data` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C4`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Assemble the extension of A by T through c and the supplied Poincare biextension.

Express the period map through tau, fixing the inverse-biextension and phi compatibility convention.

State positivity on the ideal of degeneration and compare the complete-DVR restriction to the imported polarized Raynaud datum.

This supplies [ShimuraCompactifications:C4/roadmap/mumford-quotient](#shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-mumford-quotient): Supplies periods and ample gluing data.

This supplies [ShimuraCompactifications:C4/roadmap/formal-universal-degeneration](#shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-formal-universal-degeneration): Instantiates the universal cusp family.

Expose the following API under the same hypotheses:

- `PolarizedDegenerationData.extension`: Returns the actual torus extension of A determined by c.
- `PolarizedDegenerationData.baseChange`: Admissible complete base changes preserving the ideal and positivity transport all data and rigidifications.
- `PolarizedDegenerationData.localRaynaud`: For a complete DVR, identifies the datum with the R11.3 polarized Raynaud/lattice input.
- `PolarizedDegenerationData.period_pairing`: The period trivialization is bilinear and symmetric with the prescribed phi and inverse-Poincare convention.
- `PolarizedDegenerationData.genericOneMotive`: Returns [Y to G_eta] with the actual tau-defined period map and polarization comparison lambda_A c^dual=c phi; it is compatible with admissible base change.
- `PolarizedDegenerationData.iso_iff`: Structure-preserving isomorphisms of the abelian scheme and the character/period sheaves identify two DD_pol objects precisely when lambda_A,phi,c,c^dual and the rigidified tau commute with those isomorphisms and retain the same positivity data.
- `PolarizedDegenerationData.toAmple`: Remark 4.4.7 derives auxiliary DD_ample data from DD_pol: M=(Id,lambda_A)^*P_A, L^sharp=pi^*M and psi=(Id_Y,phi)^*tau, with the displayed 2phi and induced 2lambda_A retained. This auxiliary construction is not an equality of the two categories.

**Checks.**

- `PolarizedDegenerationData.rankZero_test`: X=Y=0 gives A with its existing polarization.
- `PolarizedDegenerationData.tate_test`: For X=Y=Z and period q, positivity is v(q)>0 and reconstruction gives the Tate degeneration.
- `PolarizedDegenerationData.local_test`: The complete-DVR specialization retains the same character and period lattices as R11.3.
- `PolarizedDegenerationData.negative_test`: Period q^-1 for v(q)>0 fails the diagonal positivity condition.
- The no-torus case X=Y=0 recovers the polarized abelian scheme A.
- A negative Tate parameter valuation violates positivity and cannot define the stated proper degeneration.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C4/roadmap/poincare-extension-classification](#shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-poincare-extension-classification); `NeronModelsAndSemistableAbelianVarieties:R11.3`; `AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli:A3`; `AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli:A5`.

Attribution: The DD_pol tuple, its positivity, and its relation to the larger DD_ample tuple.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-mumford-quotient"></a>
### C4.5 Relative Mumford quotient and algebraization

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C4/roadmap/mumford-quotient -->

From positive polarized degeneration data and a compatible rational polyhedral decomposition, construct the relatively complete torus model, its formal period-lattice quotient and ample descent line. Algebraize the projective formal quotient over the complete base to obtain the semi-abelian degeneration and its generic polarized abelian scheme. The finite-index subgroup Y0 used to make an intermediate ample quotient is comparison data; the final construction descends the full lattice action. (Sources: [LAN-2021](#lan-2021) Proposition 4.5.2.15, displayed isomorphism (4.5.2.16), Construction 4.5.2.17 and Definition 4.5.2.18, printed pp. 273–275.)

Let R be a Noetherian normal domain, complete for a radical ideal I, S=Spec R and eta=Spec Frac R. Use the isotrivial torus hypothesis and the cubical rigidifications of Lan 4.1 and 4.2.1.1; this relative setting is stronger and more general in its base than the complete-DVR local R11.3 specialization.

Choose the compatible polarization and fan, preserving the cubical identities and I-adic positivity. Formal schemes and effectivity are imported from their owners, not reconstructed by ad hoc inverse limits.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C4.mumford_quotient` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C4`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Build the relatively complete torus model from C0 relative torsor charts, with line-bundle multiplication induced by tau.

Use positivity to prove period translations are locally discrete on the formal model, and descend the ample line after a finite-index lattice restriction.

Apply Lan 4.5.2.15–4.5.2.18 to the projective formal quotient and algebraize; compare the intermediate quotient with the full-lattice output. Relatively complete model/theta lemmas not read in full are explicit gaps.

This supplies [ShimuraCompactifications:C4/roadmap/degeneration-effectivity](#shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-degeneration-effectivity): Constructs the quasi-inverse to extracting degeneration data.

This supplies [ShimuraCompactifications:C5/roadmap/good-algebraic-model](#shimuracompactifications-c5-roadmap-good-algebraic-model): Supplies the universal formal degeneration charts.

Expose the following API under the same hypotheses:

- `MumfordDegeneration.genericFibre`: The generic fibre is the polarized abelian scheme uniformized by the specified Raynaud extension and period lattice.
- `MumfordDegeneration.formalCompletion`: The completion is the constructed formal period quotient with its descended ample line.
- `MumfordDegeneration.fanComparison`: Compatible subdivisions induce the canonical comparison maps, agreeing on the generic fibre.

**Checks.**

- `MumfordDegeneration.rankZero_test`: The zero-period-lattice datum returns the original abelian scheme.
- `MumfordDegeneration.tate_test`: The rank-one period q gives G_m/q^Z on the generic analytic fibre.
- `MumfordDegeneration.refinement_test`: Changing to a compatible subdivision changes the model by its toroidal comparison, preserving the generic uniformization.
- Torus rank zero returns A.
- A positive rank-one datum gives the Tate family and its proper formal quotient.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C4/roadmap/polarized-degeneration-data](#shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-polarized-degeneration-data); [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/relative-torus-embedding](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-relative-torus-embedding); [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/smooth-projective-refinement](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-smooth-projective-refinement); `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.1`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.3`; `NeronModelsAndSemistableAbelianVarieties:R11.3`.

Attribution: Projective formal quotient and ample effectivity.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-degeneration-effectivity"></a>
### C4.6 Polarized degeneration equivalence

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C4/roadmap/degeneration-effectivity -->

Under Lan's complete normal-base and positivity hypotheses, the functor extracting polarized degeneration data from semi-abelian degenerations with polarized abelian generic fibre is an equivalence with the groupoid DD_pol(R,I) of Definition 4.4.6. Prove full faithfulness and essential surjectivity separately, retaining the auxiliary cubical line construction and the structure-preserving isomorphisms used as morphisms in the polarized categories. The complete-DVR local specialization is imported from R11.3; this target is its relative effectivity extension. (Sources: [LAN-2021](#lan-2021) 4.4.1–4.4.16, printed pp. 250–255.)

Let R be a Noetherian normal domain, complete for a radical ideal I, S=Spec R and eta=Spec Frac R. Use the isotrivial torus hypothesis and the cubical rigidifications of Lan 4.1 and 4.2.1.1; this relative setting is stronger and more general in its base than the complete-DVR local R11.3 specialization.

Use exactly the degeneration categories and polarization requirements of Lan 4.4.1–4.4.16. An arbitrary singular complete ring outside this normal-domain setting is not covered.

For the polarized equivalence the morphisms are structure-preserving isomorphisms in DEG_pol and DD_pol. This is not an equivalence of categories with all generic homomorphisms as arrows.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C4.degeneration_effectivity` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C4`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Extend a structure-preserving generic isomorphism and its inverse by homomorphism-extension; uniqueness makes their composites identities. Identify the compatible polarization, character, period and biextension maps. Full faithfulness concerns precisely the source's polarized isomorphism groupoids.

Essential surjectivity uses mumford-quotient and the relatively complete model/theta inputs in Lan 4.5.

Compare extraction and reconstruction on the actual objects and morphisms, including polarizations. The chapter's uninspected construction lemmas are named proof gaps, not an inferred equivalence from the statement alone.

**Checks.**

- The functor is fully faithful on morphisms, not just a bijection of isomorphism classes.
- The complete-DVR comparison uses the imported local theory.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C4/roadmap/polarized-degeneration-data](#shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-polarized-degeneration-data); [ShimuraCompactifications:C4/roadmap/mumford-quotient](#shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-mumford-quotient); [ShimuraCompactifications:C4/roadmap/homomorphism-extension](#shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-homomorphism-extension); `NeronModelsAndSemistableAbelianVarieties:R11.3`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.3`.

Attribution: Categories and equivalence theorem, with relative construction proof obligations.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-formal-universal-degeneration"></a>
### C4.7 Universal degeneration on a cusp chart

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C4/roadmap/formal-universal-degeneration -->

For the PEL cusp label, construct the universal positive degeneration datum over the formal completion of its relative torus embedding and apply the relative effectivity equivalence. Obtain the formal semi-abelian family with its polarization, torus characters, abelian quotient and generic PEL family, compatible with admissible faces and changes of cusp representative. (Sources: [LAN-2021](#lan-2021) 6.2.5.25–6.2.5.28; 6.3.2.5, printed pp. 484–487 and 503.)

Fix the PEL datum, its order, lattice, pairing and reflex field F0 from M1. Let B=Spec(O_F0,(S)) for a set S of good primes in Lan 1.4.1.1: no prime divides the level integer n, Ibad or Disc[L# : L]. Retain the order/unramifiedness, polarization-defect and quaternionic p=2 restrictions of M2; H has the specified prime-to-S level. No bad-prime smoothness is inferred.

Complete along the specified positive monomial degeneration ideal; use the exact cusp base and formal chart rather than the completion of the entire boundary without isolating its stratum.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C4.formal_universal_degeneration` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C4`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-semiabelian-hasse-boundary-comparison"></a>
### C4.15 Semiabelian Verschiebung on the Hodge line

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C4/roadmap/semiabelian-hasse-boundary-comparison -->

For a smooth semiabelian group scheme G/S in characteristic p of constant relative dimension g, relative Frobenius F:G→G^(p) admits the natural Verschiebung V:G^(p)→G with V∘F=[p]. Its pullback on the invariant differential bundle E=e*Ω¹_(G/S) is E→Frob_S*E. The determinant is a section of λ^(p−1), λ=det(E), compatible with base change, isomorphisms and gluing of degeneration charts. On an abelian chart it agrees with the R07.2/H2 Hasse determinant. On a split torus it is a unit; on a stratum 0→T→G→A→0 of constant torus rank its determinant is the toric unit times the abelian determinant under the determinant identification. This gives the semiabelian boundary extension consumed by C6 and T0. (Sources: [BHW-2023](#bhw-2023) Lemma 5.12 proof, printed pp. 1747–1748; Section 7.1, printed pp. 1759–1760.)

S is an F_p-scheme and G is smooth, separated, commutative and of finite presentation, with semiabelian geometric fibres and constant relative dimension. E is locally free of rank g.

The torus/abelian factorization is made on a stratum where the actual extension 0→T→G→A→0 exists. No fixed-rank global torus extension across a torus-rank jump is assumed.

Use relative Frobenius and its actual fppf quotient, not a fixed-height abelian BT₁ at a toric boundary. The generic Ha, LF and BT₁ Hodge–Tate sequence retain owner R07.2.

Use target name `semiabelian_hasse_boundary_comparison` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Compactification/C4`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Use the characteristic-p smooth commutative-group Frobenius input: F is finite locally free and its kernel is killed by p. Fppf descent of [p] along F constructs V uniquely; uniqueness proves naturality and base change.

Identify invariant differentials on the Frobenius twist with Frob_S*E, and take determinants. For an invertible sheaf, Frobenius pullback is its pth tensor power, giving λ^(p−1).

On G_m the Frobenius is z↦z^p=[p], so V is the identity. On a constant torus-rank extension, use the exact invariant-differential sequence and naturality of V to compute its determinant as the toric and abelian factors.

Import the abelian determinant identification from R07.2, then glue by the naturality already proved. C6 checks the O-action, polarization and ramified H2 model separately.

**Checks.**

- For G=G_m^g the determinant is 1 in the dlog basis, including p=2.
- For G=T×A the total Hasse determinant equals the toric unit times Ha(A), so a supersingular abelian factor still forces vanishing.
- At a totally toric cusp the p-kernel has toric height g; treating it as an abelian BT₁ of height 2g fails the fibre test.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C4/roadmap/semi-abelian-scheme](#shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-semi-abelian-scheme); `AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli:A0`; `FiniteFlatGroupsAndIntegralPadicHodgeTheory:R07.2`; `AutomorphicBundles:B3`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.0`.

Attribution: The source uses boundary ordinarity and distinguishes natural from modified integral differentials. The general semiabelian determinant argument is supplied here by Frobenius descent, not attributed to Lemma 5.12 as its statement.

### Examples

- A Tate degeneration changes from an elliptic fibre to a toric boundary. Requiring one constant-rank global torus extension excludes this intended example.
- On a Tate family, du/u is the invariant fibre differential and dq/q is a logarithmic base differential. A Kodaira–Spencer theorem must state their comparison, not identify the two carriers.
- For G_m in characteristic p, relative Frobenius equals [p], so Verschiebung is the identity and the Hodge determinant is a unit.
- A boundary isogeny can lose finite-kernel rank at the toric fibre. Its quasi-finite flat kernel must not be fed into finite-flat Fargues machinery without an extra theorem.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c5"></a>
## Layer C5: Integral PEL compactifications

The ordinary smooth construction starts over Lan’s good arithmetic base, with the order, pairing, polarization-defect, level and quaternionic p=2 restrictions. Formal cusp models acquire étale algebraic charts and a quotient relation. At neat level this gives an algebraic space; projective choices and ample descent give a scheme. Non-neat levels first give the specified quotient stack, and smoothness of the stack does not imply smoothness of its coarse space.

Hodge generation, finite generation of the section algebra, its Proj/Stein contraction and the descended ample line give the integral minimal compactification. Five separate neat boundary-closure results prove geometric component detection. Higher-level finite normalizations retain their exact model and coefficient hypotheses. Lan’s more general projective normalized blow-up uses a different ramified interior/minimal supplier and is stated separately. Ordinary/formal Koecher, normalized coefficient extension and good-boundary geometry export keep their own hypotheses; downstream étale cohomology is not used as a supplier of C5 geometry.

**Milestones.**

- [Integral PEL toroidal compactification](#shimuracompactifications-c5-roadmap-integral-toroidal-space)
- [Boundary component detection](#shimuracompactifications-c5-roadmap-neat-strata-detect-geometric-components)
- [Hodge line semiampleness](#shimuracompactifications-c5-roadmap-hodge-semiampleness)
- [Integral minimal compactification](#shimuracompactifications-c5-roadmap-integral-minimal-space)
- [Quasi-projectivity of PEL moduli](#shimuracompactifications-c5-roadmap-open-quasiprojectivity)
- [Higher-level toroidal normalization](#shimuracompactifications-c5-roadmap-higher-level-toroidal-normalization)

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c5-roadmap-good-algebraic-model"></a>

### C5.6 Smooth relative boundary intersections

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C5/roadmap/neat-boundary-intersection-smooth -->

Every D_J is smooth over B. Empty intersections are allowed; no claim is made that a nonempty intersection is connected. (Sources: [LAN-2021](#lan-2021) 6.3.2.5(2), 6.3.3.14-6.3.3.16; 6.4.1.1(3), pp. 503, 517-520; [STACKS-NC](#stacks-nc) 41.21.2; unpaginated Stacks web section.)

Work on the actual good-prime PEL toroidal algebraic space X=M_H,Sigma^tor of Lan 6.3.3.15, over the indicated regular Noetherian arithmetic base B=Spec R. H is neat; Sigma satisfies 6.3.3.4, including 6.2.5.25. Retain 1.4.3.10 and the good-prime/level hypotheses of 1.4.1.2 and M2. The source construction and its ordinary etale chart/stratification theorem are required inputs, not implemented objects or arbitrary smooth resolutions.

D_i are the finitely many irreducible components of the reduced boundary. For a subset J of their index set, D_J is their scheme-theoretic intersection (D_empty=X), and D_J^o=D_J minus the union of D_i with i outside J. These are subspaces of the actual X, not new boundary carriers.

Use target name `ShimuraCompactification.neat_boundary_intersection_smooth` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Compactification/BoundaryStrata`, namespace `ShimuraCompactification`.

Use the actual ordinary etale presentation from 6.3.2.5-6.3.3.16. At neat level the cone stabilizers act trivially as in 6.2.5.27. Do not replace this presentation by formal completions alone.

Apply the new relative-regular-coordinates and relative-boundary-coordinates nodes on the ordinary chart. Their integral monoid-algebra calculation is over the smooth abelian-torsor base and retains the actual torsor multiplication. The source model still needs its ordinary chart identification.

The source fan condition and neat no-self-intersection clause identify distinct global branches through a point with distinct coordinate hyperplanes. Scheme-theoretic intersection quotients by the corresponding coordinate variables, leaving another smooth polynomial/Laurent chart.

Smoothness composes with the smooth cusp base and descends through the actual etale cover by SF.0/SF.1. Work with algebraic spaces throughout; minimal compactification and projectivity are not premises.

**Checks.**

- For a two-ray affine chart the three cases J=empty, one ray, both rays yield affine plane, affine line and the base.
- Disconnected intersections remain smooth and are not renamed a single stratum.
- An irreducible nodal boundary divisor cannot be treated as one smooth global branch: the no-self-intersection premise is essential. This is a generic warning, not a PEL counterexample.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c5-roadmap-neat-boundary-open-fiberwise-dense"></a>
### C5.7 Density of exact boundary opens in every geometric fiber

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C5/roadmap/neat-boundary-open-fiberwise-dense -->

For every geometric point b of B and every J, the open (D_J^o)_b is dense in (D_J)_b. In particular, it meets each nonempty geometric-fiber component of each open-and-closed subspace of D_J. (Sources: [LAN-2021](#lan-2021) 6.3.2.5 and 6.4.1.1(2)-(3), printed pp. 519–523.)

Work on the actual good-prime PEL toroidal algebraic space X=M_H,Sigma^tor of Lan 6.3.3.15, over the indicated regular Noetherian arithmetic base B=Spec R. H is neat; Sigma satisfies 6.3.3.4, including 6.2.5.25. Retain 1.4.3.10 and the good-prime/level hypotheses of 1.4.1.2 and M2. The source construction and its ordinary etale chart/stratification theorem are required inputs, not implemented objects or arbitrary smooth resolutions.

D_i are the finitely many irreducible components of the reduced boundary. For a subset J of their index set, D_J is their scheme-theoretic intersection (D_empty=X), and D_J^o=D_J minus the union of D_i with i outside J. These are subspaces of the actual X, not new boundary carriers.

Use target name `ShimuraCompactification.neat_boundary_open_fiberwise_dense` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Compactification/BoundaryStrata`, namespace `ShimuraCompactification`.

Base-change the ordinary etale chart and the scheme-theoretic boundary intersections. Smoothness and the coordinate descriptions survive this base change.

Apply relative-boundary-coordinates: the exact open in the coordinate quotient inverts the remaining boundary monomial. Multiplication by that monomial is injective on its exponent basis over every base ring. Thus the local open is schematically dense after every base change; in particular it is topologically dense on the geometric fiber. This strengthens the preceding reduced-base coordinate argument without changing the target.

For clarity, use the generic open-map proof rather than counting rational points: the inverse image of a dense open under an open map is dense, because any nonempty open has nonempty open image. Etale maps are open.

Apply this to the etale chart maps and descend density using their jointly surjective cover. Restriction to an open-and-closed component preserves it. Density after extension to an algebraic closure also implies density in an ordinary residue fiber.

**Checks.**

- In A1 over a field, G_m is dense even when that field has finitely many rational points; no rational-point cardinality proof is admissible.
- If J contains every boundary coordinate, the remaining product is 1 and the exact open equals the entire coordinate intersection.
- Over a DVR, deleting the closed fiber from a boundary section gives a total-space dense open that is NOT fiberwise dense. The arbitrary dense-open statement is false.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c5-roadmap-neat-stratum-closure-component"></a>
### C5.8 A labelled stratum component is an exact boundary open

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C5/roadmap/neat-stratum-closure-component -->

For every nonempty irreducible component Z of a labelled stratum, let J consist of the global boundary components containing Z. There is a unique connected component W of D_J with Z = W intersect D_J^o. Consequently the reduced closure of Z in X is W. (Sources: [LAN-2021](#lan-2021) 6.3.2.16; 6.3.3.16; 6.4.1.1(2)-(3), printed pp. 507–508 and 518–520.)

Work on the actual good-prime PEL toroidal algebraic space X=M_H,Sigma^tor of Lan 6.3.3.15, over the indicated regular Noetherian arithmetic base B=Spec R. H is neat; Sigma satisfies 6.3.3.4, including 6.2.5.25. Retain 1.4.3.10 and the good-prime/level hypotheses of 1.4.1.2 and M2. The source construction and its ordinary etale chart/stratification theorem are required inputs, not implemented objects or arbitrary smooth resolutions.

D_i are the finitely many irreducible components of the reduced boundary. For a subset J of their index set, D_J is their scheme-theoretic intersection (D_empty=X), and D_J^o=D_J minus the union of D_i with i outside J. These are subspaces of the actual X, not new boundary carriers.

Use target name `ShimuraCompactification.neat_stratum_closure_component` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Compactification/BoundaryStrata`, namespace `ShimuraCompactification`.

The source closure/incidence theorem makes each boundary component a union of stratum components. Thus Z lies wholly inside or wholly outside each D_i, so it lies in the indicated exact-pattern open D_J^o.

On the ordinary relative toric chart, an exact coordinate pattern is a face-orbit stratum, now described by relative-face-open, relative-stratum-quotient and relative-boundary-coordinates. Good algebraic models preserve these labels (6.3.2.5 and 6.3.2.16), and the two projections of the quotient groupoid preserve their equivalence classes (6.3.3.16). Thus, on D_J^o, every labelled stratum is open: around each point it is the image of the appropriate exact-pattern etale chart.

All label subsets in D_J^o are open, so their complements are open as well. A stratum is smooth over the regular base; its irreducible components are open and closed. Hence the partition of D_J^o into stratum components is a partition by open-and-closed subsets. No assertion about arbitrary refinements of a stratification is used.

By neat-boundary-intersection-smooth, D_J is regular and Noetherian. Its connected components are irreducible and open and closed. By neat-boundary-open-fiberwise-dense, each nonempty component W has a nonempty dense open W intersect D_J^o, which is irreducible.

An irreducible space admits no partition into two nonempty open-and-closed subsets. Therefore W intersect D_J^o is exactly the stratum component it meets. Conversely the connected Z lies in one W. Its closure is W, which is already reduced. This proves existence and uniqueness without assuming that all of D_J is one stratum closure.

**Checks.**

- A smooth quadrant has one exact zero/nonzero pattern for each subset of its two rays, with the usual face-incidence order.
- Over a base where 2 is invertible, on P1_z times P1_w the divisors w=1 and w=z^2 meet in two disjoint sections z=1 and z=-1. One must choose a connected component of their intersection, not identify the whole intersection with one stratum closure.
- For J empty, this includes a connected component of the open moduli stratum and its closure in the corresponding component of X. It does not manufacture a boundary cusp when the boundary is empty.

Attribution: Refines the source open-dense intersection assertion using its ordinary stratum-preserving charts. The exact-pattern and connected-component argument is this blueprint deduction; it needs the stated no-self-intersection and label-descent inputs.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c5-roadmap-neat-stratum-closure-proper"></a>
### C5.9 Properness of the neat stratum closure

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C5/roadmap/neat-stratum-closure-proper -->

The reduced closure W of each nonempty stratum component Z is proper over B. Together with the preceding nodes it is smooth over B, and Z_b is dense in W_b for every geometric point b. (Sources: [LAN-2021](#lan-2021) 6.3.3.17 and 6.4.1.1, combined with the preceding stratum identification, printed pp. 518–523.)

The source construction and its ordinary etale chart/stratification theorem are required inputs, not implemented objects or arbitrary smooth resolutions.

D_i are the finitely many irreducible components of the reduced boundary. For a subset J of their index set, D_J is their scheme-theoretic intersection (D_empty=X), and D_J^o=D_J minus the union of D_i with i outside J. These are subspaces of the actual X, not new boundary carriers.

Since D_J is Noetherian and regular, W is open and closed in D_J; D_J is a closed subspace of X. Thus W to X is a closed immersion.

Compose with the actual proper structural morphism X to B from the early toroidal construction. Smoothness follows from the first node, and fiberwise density from the second after restricting to W.

Do not use the integral minimal compactification, an ample Hodge power, scheme representability or any projectivity theorem. Those are separate C5 targets.

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C5/roadmap/neat-strata-detect-geometric-components -->

Apply the preceding closure and density nodes to obtain smooth proper W_a and opens Z_a dense in every geometric fiber of W_a. This is the specific PEL instance missing from a mere total-space density assertion.

Import the generic smooth-closure component detector from SF.2, after its SF.1 algebraic-space and proper coherent cohomology inputs. Its proof uses the arithmetic-base Stein factor X to E to B with E finite etale. Each W_a to E is proper and smooth, so its image is clopen; the total-component hypothesis makes these images cover E.

Over a geometric b, the fibers of X over the points of the discrete E_b are the connected regular, hence irreducible components of X_b. The corresponding nonempty W_a fibers are open and closed in W_a,b, so the dense Z_a,b meets them. This describes the exact imported detector, not a second C5 definition or independent copy of Stein theory.

Export the conclusion directly to AutomorphicBundles B5. The owner order is early spaces/descent, then SF.2 Stein/detection, then early C5, then B5. Do not insert a B5-to-C5 edge or use the C5 minimal-compactification endpoint.

### C5.28 Canonical bundle of a Siegel threefold

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C5/roadmap/siegel-canonical-bundle -->

For a neat smooth genus-two Siegel toroidal threefold X/B with boundary D and invariant Hodge bundle E of rank two, det Omega^1_X/B(log D)=(det E)^3 and the relative dualizing line is (det E)^3 tensor O_X(-D). Derive this from the logarithmic Kodaira–Spencer isomorphism and the local normal-crossings coordinate formula; D is the actual boundary divisor. These are smooth-model bundle identities, not automatic dualizing formulas for singular p-level normalizations. (Sources: [CG-2020](#cg-2020) 5.3, published pp. 830–831.)

Fix the PEL datum, its order, lattice, pairing and reflex field F0 from M1. Let B=Spec(O_F0,(S)) for a set S of good primes in Lan 1.4.1.1: no prime divides the level integer n, Ibad or Disc[L# : L]. Retain the order/unramifiedness, polarization-defect and quaternionic p=2 restrictions of M2; H has the specified prime-to-S level. No bad-prime smoothness is inferred.

Use relative dimension three, principal Siegel polarization, smooth cone system and the specified boundary normal-crossings hypotheses.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C5.siegel_canonical_bundle` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C5`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Take determinants in Sym² E≅Omega^1(log D); compute det Sym² E=(det E)^3 by a rank-two basis/transition calculation.

Use the local logarithmic coordinate wedge to identify det Omega^1(log D)=det Omega^1 tensor O(D).

Identify det Omega^1 with the relative dualizing line in the smooth setting and descend the local comparison.

**Checks.**

- For a diagonal change of basis, Sym² has determinant (t1 t2)^3.
- Removing the logarithmic poles contributes O(-D).

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C5/roadmap/log-kodaira-spencer](#shimuracompactifications-c5-roadmap-log-kodaira-spencer); `AutomorphicBundles:B4`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.0`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.2`.

Attribution: Canonical/subcanonical duality bundle identity.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c5-roadmap-good-boundary-cohomology-export"></a>
### C5.29 Good-reduction boundary geometry for cohomology

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C5/roadmap/good-boundary-cohomology-export -->

Export the exact good-prime toroidal family, smooth proper model, open Siegel moduli locus and normal-crossings boundary with the hyperspecial/tame level conditions used in BCGP 2025 Theorem 1.8.29. The cohomology owner must supply Lan–Stroh nearby-cycle/open comparison and duality to obtain the stated unramified cohomology conclusion. Smoothness of the nonproper open alone is not a proof of unramifiedness. (Sources: [BCGP-2025](#bcgp-2025) Theorem 1.8.29 and beginning of proof, arXiv v1 p. 17.)

At ell distinct from p use the theorem's hyperspecial ell-level, prime-to-ell auxiliary level and exact automorphic coefficient system; export only the model satisfying those hypotheses.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C5.good_boundary_cohomology_export` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C5`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Choose the compatible good-prime smooth projective toroidal model and its universal semi-abelian extension.

Identify its interior, boundary and coefficient extension with the theorem's Siegel variety and local system inputs.

Pass the geometry to the etale/cohomology supplier, naming the nearby-cycle/open comparison and duality separately. The cited Lan–Stroh Corollary 5.20 proof is not read and is not a new theorem in C5.

**Checks.**

- The geometry retains hyperspecial good-reduction conditions.
- No cohomological conclusion is inferred from smoothness of the open alone.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C5/roadmap/integral-toroidal-space](#shimuracompactifications-c5-roadmap-integral-toroidal-space); [ShimuraCompactifications:C5/roadmap/valuative-properness](#shimuracompactifications-c5-roadmap-valuative-properness); [ShimuraCompactifications:C5/roadmap/log-kodaira-spencer](#shimuracompactifications-c5-roadmap-log-kodaira-spencer); `AutomorphicBundles:B3`.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c5-roadmap-projective-normalized-blowup"></a>
### C5.30 Projective normalized-blowup compactification

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C5/roadmap/projective-normalized-blowup -->

For Lan 2017's integral PEL/lattice-collection setting at a prime p, construct the projective toroidal model for a compatible PROJECTIVE cone system Sigma, without imposing smoothness or good reduction at p. Choose its compatible polarization function pol and a smooth refinement over the characteristic-zero reflex field. Push the pol-weighted boundary ideal to the minimal model, take its schematic closure J_tilde_(H,dpol) in the supplied p-integral minimal model, and form the normalization of its blow-up. For sufficiently divisible d the resulting models stabilize to the normal projective flat scheme M_tilde_(H,Sigma)^tor of Theorem 6.1, with the stated formal torus-torsor charts and tautological degenerating families indexed by the auxiliary lattice collection. This is a normalized blow-up of a minimal model; it is not defined as a finite cover of a fixed good-level toroidal model. (Sources: [LAN-2017](#lan-2017) Construction 3.1, Definition 3.5 and Construction 3.12, printed pp. 7–9; Theorem 6.1, printed pp. 22–24.)

Use the integral PEL datum (O,*,L,pairing,h0) and Condition 1.4.3.10 as in Lan 2017 Section 2. Let H be open compact in G(Zhat) with NEAT image H^p away from p, and retain the collection (g_j,L_j,pairing_j) indexed by J as in the source's reference [18], Section 2.

Work over S_tilde_0=Spec(O_(F0,(p))). Sigma is complete admissible and compatible as in Definition 2.1/Condition 6.2.5.25, and projective as in Definitions 2.5 and 2.7. A compatible invariant polarization function is supplied; individual cones need not be smooth.

The ramified normalized interior, minimal model and auxiliary lattice families of [18], Propositions 6.1 and 6.4 are requested from M4. The currently inspected M4 nodes cover only the good-prime normalization case, so this wider supplier interface is an explicit gap.

Use target name `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications.C5.projective_normalized_blowup` in `TauCeti/ShimuraCompactifications/C5`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Use C0 projective refinements and Lan Construction 3.1 to form the boundary ideal whose ray multiplicities are dpol; Definition 3.5 pushes it to the characteristic-zero minimal model. Keep the invariant piecewise-linear function and its lattice-valued ray multiplicities.

Construction 3.12 takes the schematic closure of that ideal in the supplied p-integral minimal model and the normalization of its blow-up, yielding normality, projectivity and flatness. Generic normalization and blow-ups are foundations inputs, rather than new private carriers.

Lan Sections 4–5 identify the completed ideals and torus-torsor charts and establish stabilization for sufficiently divisible d; Theorem 6.1(1)–(6) supplies the resulting families, strata and uniqueness. The complete Sections 4–5 proof and [18] supplier constructions were not read, so their precise proof leaves remain a gap. When using Lemma 2.12 allow its nonvertex lattice generators as required by Remark 2.15.

This supplies [ShimuraCompactifications:C5/roadmap/normalized-chart-finiteness](#shimuracompactifications-c5-roadmap-normalized-chart-finiteness): Supplies the projective source model whose formal chart theorem must be distinguished from a finite same-fan normalization.

Expose the following API under the same hypotheses:

- `ProjectiveToroidalModel.toMinimal`: Returns the canonical proper map to the supplied normalized minimal model, with the source's boundary stratum images.
- `ProjectiveToroidalModel.completion`: The completed neighbourhood of the labelled stratum is the formal torus-torsor chart of Theorem 6.1(3)–(4), with its actual normal flat torsor base and all indexed families.
- `ProjectiveToroidalModel.auxiliaryIndependent`: For sufficiently divisible d, the comparison identifies models for different d and polarization functions inducing the same Sigma; retain the fixed PEL lattice-collection data.
- `ProjectiveToroidalModel.refinement`: Compatible projective refinements induce canonical proper maps with identity/composition, as in Theorem 6.1(2) and Proposition 7.1.
- `ProjectiveToroidalModel.valuativeExtension`: For an irreducible Noetherian normal S over the local reflex base carrying the specified indexed degenerating families and generic level map, the map extends uniquely when, at each geometric point, ALL dominating complete-DVR valuation pairings lie in one corresponding cone, exactly as in Theorem 6.1(6).

**Checks.**

- `ProjectiveToroidalModel.rankZero_test`: When there is no boundary and the minimal model is already the proper interior, the ideal is the unit ideal and its normalized blow-up returns that normal model.
- `ProjectiveToroidalModel.blowup_test`: The normalized blow-up of (x,y) in a regular two-dimensional affine chart has exceptional P1 and a positive-dimensional fibre; declaring every normalized blow-up a finite normalization fails this local construction test.
- `ProjectiveToroidalModel.stabilization_test`: Two sufficiently divisible d giving the same source Sigma yield the canonical comparison preserving the labelled completed chart and each auxiliary family.
- Every lattice index j has its own tautological semi-abelian family; higher-level structures are specified only where the source defines them.
- The output is projective over the local reflex base and maps properly to the supplied minimal model; no general finiteness to a different fan is claimed.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/smooth-projective-refinement](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-smooth-projective-refinement); [ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/projective-algebraization](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-projective-algebraization); [ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/minimal-boundary-map](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-minimal-boundary-map); [ShimuraCompactifications:C4/roadmap/polarized-degeneration-data](#shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-polarized-degeneration-data); `PELModuli:M4`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.2`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.3`.

Attribution: Normalized-blowup construction, local reflex base, projective compatible fan, and stabilized model theorem.

### Examples

- A discriminant prime outside the good-base hypotheses does not acquire smooth PEL reduction from the ordinary construction.
- An auxiliary-level Hodge line may descend only after taking a positive tensor power; on the coarse model retain the resulting Hodge Q-line interpretation.
- The normalization of a same-fan finite generic level cover is finite over the lower-level model. A normalized blow-up of a minimal model is a projective birational construction and may be nonfinite.
- Generic characteristic-zero Hartogs does not prove extension of torsion coefficients on an ordinary special fibre. The formal S2/normality, codimension and finite-thickening comparisons are required.

### Dependencies

Alongside the local target chain, this layer consumes `AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli:A5`; `AdicSpacesPartII:F0/formal-direct-image-comparison`; `AdicSpacesPartII:R2`; `AdicSpacesPartII:R3`; `AutomorphicBundles:B3`; `AutomorphicBundles:B4`; `AutomorphicBundles:B5`; `NeronModelsAndSemistableAbelianVarieties:R11.3`; `PELModuli:M2`; `PELModuli:M2/representability`; `PELModuli:M2/universal-family`; `PELModuli:M4`; `PELModuli:M4/higher-level-normalization`; `PELModuli:M4/normalization-finite-normal-flat`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.0`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.1`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.2`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.3`; `ShimuraVarieties:V2/baily-borel`; `ShimuraVarieties:V2/rational-boundary`.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c6"></a>
## Layer C6: Hilbert and modular specializations

Use the fine Hilbert tame-level model over B=Z[1/N(n)], including discriminant primes through the precise H2 Deligne–Pappas/local-model inputs. This is a direct C6 construction from C0 charts, C4 families and formal gluing; it is not an instance of C5 good-prime smoothness. The level-image cusp lattice is X=cbb′, the trace dual includes the different, and the full stabilizer differs from its finite cyclotomic image.

For degree g>1, unit transport forces a bounded-support nonzero exponent to be totally positive. Constant terms survive unless their covariance has a regular nonzero multiplier. Toroidal/minimal models, integral boundary functions and ideals, weight descent and q-expansion detection retain separate hypotheses. Arbitrary module detection uses directed unions and coefficientwise injection, never commutation of a whole formal-series module with filtered colimits.

At arbitrary residue primes, use H2’s actual ordinary and Hasse neighbourhood models. Wild-level compactifications remain conditional on their integral local-model/normalization interfaces. At F=Q, import the dimension-one modular/Tate geometry: Hilbert Koecher’s degree condition does not apply. The prime diamond comparison with PR81 is restricted to prime N≥5 and the stated diamond group.

**Milestones.**

- [Arithmetic Koecher principle](#shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-arithmetic-koecher)
- [Hilbert boundary-ideal criterion](#shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-hilbert-boundary-constant)
- [Hilbert toroidal compactification](#shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-hilbert-toroidal-model)
- [Universal semiabelian extension](#shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-hilbert-semiabelian-extension)
- [Hilbert minimal compactification](#shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-hilbert-minimal-contraction)
- [Ordinary Hilbert boundary](#shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-hilbert-boundary-ordinary)

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-finite-index-cusp-unit-contraction"></a>
### C6.1 A contracting unit in a cusp subgroup

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/finite-index-cusp-unit-contraction -->

If U has finite index and F has more than one infinite place, then for each w₀ there is u∈U with |τ_w₀(u)|>1 and |τ_w(u)|<1 for every w≠w₀. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Theorem 8.3 proof, author copy printed p. 547.)

F is a totally real number field; τ_w denotes its signed real embedding at infinite place w. The native InfinitePlace value w(x) is |τ_w(x)|. U is a subgroup of the integer units, not a subgroup of F× with an assumed finite index.

The cardinality hypothesis is equivalent to [F:Q]>1 in this totally real setting.

Use target name `finiteIndex_unit_contracts_away` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Hilbert/Koecher`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

Apply the pinned Dirichlet exists_unit theorem at w₀ to obtain v whose other absolute values have negative logarithms.

Use positivity of unit absolute values and the logarithmic product formula, with all multiplicities equal to one. There is at least one other place, so the logarithm at w₀ is strictly positive.

Apply the finite-index positive-power theorem to v. Raising all absolute values to this positive power preserves the strict inequalities and produces an element of U.

**Checks.**

- Over Q every integer unit has absolute value one, so the degree assumption cannot be removed.
- For a principal cusp-unit subgroup, use its actual finite index from H3; no logarithmic density of a discrete lattice is asserted.

*Needs:* `mathlib:NumberField.Units.dirichletUnitTheorem.exists_unit`; `mathlib:NumberField.Units.sum_mult_mul_log`; `mathlib:NumberField.Units.pos_at_place`; `mathlib:NumberField.IsTotallyReal.mult_eq`; `mathlib:Subgroup.exists_pow_mem_of_index_ne_zero`.

Attribution: Declaration-level expansion of the coefficient argument, with its necessary hypotheses made explicit.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-negative-cusp-exponent"></a>
### C6.2 A negative embedding of a nonpositive exponent

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/negative-cusp-exponent -->

If ξ∈F is nonzero and is not totally positive, then τ_w(ξ)<0 for some infinite place w. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Theorem 8.3 proof, author copy printed p. 547.)

F is a totally real number field; τ_w denotes its signed real embedding at infinite place w. The native InfinitePlace value w(x) is |τ_w(x)|. U is a subgroup of the integer units, not a subgroup of F× with an assumed finite index.

Use target name `exists_negative_embedding` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Hilbert/Koecher`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

Negate the existing universal strict-positivity predicate to obtain τ_w(ξ)≤0.

A field embedding is injective, so ξ≠0 implies τ_w(ξ)≠0; hence the inequality is strict.

**Checks.**

- ξ=0 has no negative embedding and must be excluded.
- A nonzero element cannot have a zero real conjugate.

*Needs:* `tauceti:NumberField.isTotallyPositive_iff`.

Attribution: Corrects the omitted exclusion of exponent zero in the printed contradiction argument; see E1.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-negative-trace-orbit"></a>
### C6.3 Unbounded negative trace along cusp units

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/negative-trace-orbit -->

Let U have finite index, [F:Q]>1, ξ≠0 not totally positive, and y_w>0 for every w. For every real B there is u∈U with Σ_w τ_w(u²ξ)y_w<B. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Theorem 8.3 proof, author copy printed p. 547.)

F is a totally real number field; τ_w denotes its signed real embedding at infinite place w. The native InfinitePlace value w(x) is |τ_w(x)|. U is a subgroup of the integer units, not a subgroup of F× with an assumed finite index.

The y_w form a vector of the open positive dual cone. It need not itself be a field element.

Use target name `negative_trace_orbit_unbounded` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Hilbert/Koecher`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

Choose a negative embedding w₀ by negative-cusp-exponent and a unit v∈U contracting at all other places by finite-index-cusp-unit-contraction.

For u=v^n, the w₀ summand is τ_w₀(ξ)y_w₀|τ_w₀(v)|^(2n), tending to negative infinity.

At every other place |τ_w(v)|^(2n) tends to zero. The finite sum of these terms tends to zero. Therefore the whole sum is less than any prescribed B for sufficiently large n.

**Checks.**

- For Q the orbit of ξ=−1 under integer-unit squares is the singleton {−1}.
- ξ=0 has identically zero pairing; no unboundedness conclusion applies.
- For Q(√2), ξ=−√2, v=1+√2 and y=(1,1), the first positive n already gives a negative trace; positive powers continue to negative infinity.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/negative-cusp-exponent](#shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-negative-cusp-exponent); [ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/finite-index-cusp-unit-contraction](#shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-finite-index-cusp-unit-contraction); `mathlib:tendsto_pow_atTop_atTop_of_one_lt`; `mathlib:tendsto_pow_atTop_nhds_zero_of_lt_one`.

Attribution: Declaration-level expansion of the coefficient argument, with its necessary hypotheses made explicit.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-coefficient-unit-orbit"></a>
### C6.4 Nonzero coefficients survive unit transport

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/coefficient-unit-orbit -->

For a commutative ring R, a:F→R, multipliers c:U×F→R× and covariance a(u²ξ)=c(u,ξ)a(ξ), one has a(u²ξ)≠0 if and only if a(ξ)≠0. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Equation (5), author copy printed p. 546.)

F is a totally real number field; τ_w denotes its signed real embedding at infinite place w. The native InfinitePlace value w(x) is |τ_w(x)|. U is a subgroup of the integer units, not a subgroup of F× with an assumed finite index.

No reducedness, domain, characteristic-zero, or nontriviality condition on R.

Use target name `coefficient_ne_zero_on_unit_orbit` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Hilbert/Koecher`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

Rewrite using the stated covariance.

Apply Units.mul_right_eq_zero and negate the equivalence. No division by a nonunit or by the coefficient is used.

**Checks.**

- In Z/4, multiplication by the nonunit 2 kills the nonzero coefficient 2; the unit condition is essential.
- A root of unity and a unit-valued weight character remain units after any coefficient-ring map, even if their reductions equal one.

*Needs:* `mathlib:Units.mul_right_eq_zero`.

Attribution: The scalar unit consequence of the actual Fourier transformation law; the lattice and line trivialization are supplier obligations.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-bounded-cusp-support"></a>
### C6.5 Koecher support for coefficient families

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/bounded-cusp-support -->

Let [F:Q]>1, U have finite index, and a:F→R satisfy unit-valued covariance as in coefficient-unit-orbit. Suppose some y_w>0 and B∈ℝ satisfy B≤Σ_w τ_w(ξ)y_w whenever a(ξ)≠0. Then every nonzero coefficient is indexed by ξ=0 or by a totally positive ξ. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Theorem 8.3 proof, author copy printed p. 547.)

F is a totally real number field; τ_w denotes its signed real embedding at infinite place w. The native InfinitePlace value w(x) is |τ_w(x)|. U is a subgroup of the integer units, not a subgroup of F× with an assumed finite index.

A coefficient family alone is not a convergent or completed formal series.

Use target name `bounded_cusp_support_is_positive` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Hilbert/Koecher`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

Suppose a nonzero coefficient has ξ≠0 not totally positive.

Apply negative-trace-orbit with the given y and B, obtaining u∈U with pairing strictly below B.

The coefficient at u²ξ is nonzero by coefficient-unit-orbit, contradicting the support lower bound.

**Checks.**

- The constant family supported only at zero is permitted. Koecher does not imply cuspidality.
- The conclusion allows torsion and nilpotents in the coefficient ring because only multiplication by units was cancelled.
- A geometric application must supply the support bound; it is not assumed from the notation for a formal series.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/negative-trace-orbit](#shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-negative-trace-orbit); [ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/coefficient-unit-orbit](#shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-coefficient-unit-orbit).

Attribution: Declaration-level expansion of the coefficient argument, with its necessary hypotheses made explicit.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-positive-exponents-on-charts"></a>
### C6.6 Positive exponents vanish on every boundary ray

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/positive-exponents-on-charts -->

If ξ is totally positive and v=(v_w) is nonzero with all v_w≥0, then Σ_w τ_w(ξ)v_w>0. In an integral Hilbert cusp chart, its pairing with each primitive nonzero boundary ray is therefore a strictly positive integer. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Section 2 boundary coordinates, author copy printed pp. 529–530; Theorem 8.3, printed p. 547.)

F is a totally real number field; τ_w denotes its signed real embedding at infinite place w. The native InfinitePlace value w(x) is |τ_w(x)|. U is a subgroup of the integer units, not a subgroup of F× with an assumed finite index.

For the final interpretation ξ belongs to the character lattice X and the ray belongs to its integral dual; lattice integrality is imported from C0/H1.

Use target name `positive_exponent_pairs_pos` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Hilbert/Koecher`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

All summands are nonnegative. Since v is nonzero, at least one coordinate is strictly positive.

Its product with the corresponding strictly positive conjugate of ξ is positive, so the finite sum is positive.

Use the character/cocharacter integral pairing to interpret the result as a positive coordinate exponent.

**Checks.**

- The zero dual vector gives zero and must be excluded.
- For ξ=1 and every y_w>0 the pairing is positive.
- On a regular chart, positivity at each of its r rays implies divisibility of the monomial by the product x₁⋯x_r.

*Needs:* `tauceti:NumberField.isTotallyPositive_iff`; `mathlib:Finset.one_lt_prod_iff_of_one_le`; [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/relative-regular-coordinates](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-relative-regular-coordinates); `HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:H1`.

Attribution: Elementary positivity consequence used to identify the Hilbert boundary ideal; generic toric coordinate construction remains in C0.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-constant-term-covariance"></a>
### C6.7 The annihilator of a constant coefficient

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/constant-term-covariance -->

Under a(u²ξ)=c(u,ξ)a(ξ) with c(u,ξ)∈R×, every u satisfies (c(u,0)−1)a(0)=0. For Dimitrov’s actual weight law the root-of-unity phase at zero is one. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Proposition 8.5(iii), author copy printed p. 548.)

F is a totally real number field; τ_w denotes its signed real embedding at infinite place w. The native InfinitePlace value w(x) is |τ_w(x)|. U is a subgroup of the integer units, not a subgroup of F× with an assumed finite index.

R is any commutative ring; the scalar formula is written after a local trivialization of the invertible coefficient line.

Use target name `constant_coefficient_annihilated` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Hilbert/Koecher`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

Set ξ=0 in the transformation law, since u²·0=0.

Subtract a(0); distributivity yields the annihilation relation.

**Checks.**

- This does not conclude a(0)=0 over a ring with zero divisors.
- For the trivial character, every a(0) satisfies the relation.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/coefficient-unit-orbit](#shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-coefficient-unit-orbit).

Attribution: The explicit ring-valued equality underlying the source’s zero-divisor formulation.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-constant-term-vanishing"></a>
### C6.8 Constant-term vanishing with a regular multiplier

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/constant-term-vanishing -->

If, for some u, multiplication by c(u,0)−1 on R has zero kernel, then the covariance relation forces a(0)=0. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Proposition 8.5(iii), author copy printed p. 548.)

F is a totally real number field; τ_w denotes its signed real embedding at infinite place w. The native InfinitePlace value w(x) is |τ_w(x)|. U is a subgroup of the integer units, not a subgroup of F× with an assumed finite index.

The zero-kernel hypothesis is required; c(u,0)≠1 alone is insufficient for a general coefficient ring.

Use target name `constant_coefficient_eq_zero` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Hilbert/Koecher`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

Apply constant-term-covariance at the selected u.

Apply the zero-kernel hypothesis to a(0).

**Checks.**

- In Z/4, the unit 3 differs from 1 and fixes the nonzero coefficient 2. Thus the nontrivial-character argument valid over a field does not apply to every R.
- Over a field, any multiplier different from one supplies the zero-kernel hypothesis.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/constant-term-covariance](#shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-constant-term-covariance).

Attribution: Coefficient-sensitive consequence; no assertion that every nonparallel character remains nontrivial modulo a prime.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-meromorphic-cusp-support-bound"></a>
### C6.9 A pole bound on a Hilbert cusp chart

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/meromorphic-cusp-support-bound -->

Under the stated Hilbert geometric hypotheses, a section of ω^κ over the open part of a completed regular cusp chart, which has finite pole order along its boundary, has Fourier support bounded below under every y in that cone. More explicitly, after a character-compatible local trivialization, write it as t^(−m)g, where t=x₁⋯x_r, m≥0, and g lies in the t-adic completed chart ring. Then a(ξ)≠0 implies ⟨ξ,y⟩≥−mΣ_i⟨m_i,y⟩, with m_i the r polynomial coordinate characters. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Section 2 completed toric coordinates, author copy printed pp. 529–530; Theorem 8.3 proof, printed p. 547.)

Use the actual Hilbert moduli and toroidal model supplied by H1–H4, C4 and the C6 formal-gluing targets, not an arbitrary scheme record with the conclusions as fields. F is totally real of degree greater than one; n is prime to the discriminant and divides neither (2) nor (3); c is prime to n. Use a regular admissible cusp fan, finite modulo its unit group, and a Noetherian algebra R over o′[1/Δ], Δ=N(d n), containing the weight values and the required square roots for polarization descent. Adjoin the finite cyclotomic cusp coefficients by an étale cover when needed. ω^κ is the actual descended invariant-differential line; no general automorphic-bundle construction is duplicated here.

The completion and localization are formed over R; an unproved interchange of completion with arbitrary tensor products is not used. The coefficient description of this completion and the meromorphic representation are requested generic inputs.

Use target name `meromorphic_cusp_support_bound` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Hilbert/Koecher`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

Import the C0 regular coordinate identification and the boundary-union ideal (t). Complete that R-algebra and use its proven admissible coefficient expansion.

Every exponent of g has nonnegative pairing with y in the cone; the Laurent coordinates pair to zero.

Multiplication by t^(−m) translates the support by −mΣ_i m_i. Coefficient injectivity yields the stated lower bound.

- The union of coordinate boundary divisors is cut out by their product, whereas the sum ideal defines their intersection.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-hilbert-cusp-positive-support"></a>
### C6.10 Positive support of meromorphic Hilbert expansions

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/hilbert-cusp-positive-support -->

Under the Hilbert geometric hypotheses, every unit-equivariant meromorphic cusp expansion of an open Hilbert modular form has support contained in X_+∪{0}, where X=cbb′ is the actual character lattice at that cusp. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Theorem 8.3 proof, author copy printed p. 547.)

Use the actual Hilbert moduli and toroidal model supplied by H1–H4, C4 and the C6 formal-gluing targets, not an arbitrary scheme record with the conclusions as fields. F is totally real of degree greater than one; n is prime to the discriminant and divides neither (2) nor (3); c is prime to n. Use a regular admissible cusp fan, finite modulo its unit group, and a Noetherian algebra R over o′[1/Δ], Δ=N(d n), containing the weight values and the required square roots for polarization descent. Adjoin the finite cyclotomic cusp coefficients by an étale cover when needed. ω^κ is the actual descended invariant-differential line; no general automorphic-bundle construction is duplicated here.

The finite-index subgroup U consists of the stabilizer pairs (u,1); its action preserves X. Its weight and cyclotomic multipliers are units. The coefficient family on X is extended by zero to F.

Use target name `hilbert_cusp_support_positive` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Hilbert/Koecher`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

Use H3’s exact stabilizer congruences and finite-index assertion; use H1’s lattice and trace dictionary, including the distinction between b and b′.

Choose an open positive dual vector y. Completeness of the admissible fan puts y in a cone, without asserting that the whole fan is finite.

Apply meromorphic-cusp-support-bound on that cone.

In a local trivialization of the coefficient line, apply bounded-cusp-support. The result is independent of the trivialization because its transition scalars are units.

**Checks.**

- Both unramified level cusps (b′=b) and ramified level cusps (b′≠b) retain their actual exponent lattice.
- Ramification of a level cusp does not mean a base prime dividing the field discriminant.
- No finite flatness of a full p-adic tower is inferred from the finite cusp cyclotomic cover.

### C6.11 Arithmetic Koecher extension

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/arithmetic-koecher -->

For g=[F:Q]>1, every coefficient algebra R over the discriminant-inverted weight base O′[1/Δ], and the actual Hilbert toroidal model, restriction Γ(Mbar_R,ωκ)→Γ(M_R,ωκ) is an isomorphism. No cusp vanishing is included. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Theorem 8.3, author copy printed p. 547.)

Dimitrov’s torsion-free tame level and admissible fan; Δ=N(d n). The weight κ is integral, the coefficient base contains its values and the square roots needed for polarization descent.

The Noetherian case uses coherent completion detection. The general R case uses finite presentation and the requested qcqs filtered-colimit theorem; completion is not asserted to commute with arbitrary tensor products.

Use target name `arithmetic_koecher` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Hilbert/Koecher`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

Use schematic density of the open immersion and invertibility of the line to obtain injectivity.

For an open section, use the requested finite-pole and formal-detection theorem to examine it on every completed cusp chart.

Apply hilbert-cusp-positive-support. Its exponents lie in every positive dual monoid, so the existing meromorphic expansion has no negative coordinate exponents and belongs to the completed regular ring.

Use the actual equivariant chart relation to glue these regular formal sections, then coherent formal detection to extend across the boundary. Uniqueness follows from injectivity.

Descend the finite-presentation model, line and section data to finitely generated subalgebras of R over O′[1/Δ]. Apply the Noetherian result there and use the requested qcqs H0/filtered-colimit comparison for both open and compactified schemes. This is the explicit supplier input for arbitrary coefficients.

**Checks.**

- For degree one the weakly holomorphic q^(−1) behavior is not eliminated by units; the theorem is not claimed for F=Q.
- The constant coefficient need not vanish.
- No ramified-base Hasse-ideal conclusion follows from a theorem over o′[1/Δ].
- Changing an admissible fan uses common-refinement comparison maps from C3, not equality of the chosen toroidal schemes.
