<a id="shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-hilbert-boundary-constant"></a>
### C6.12 Hilbert cusp forms and the boundary ideal

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/hilbert-boundary-constant -->

Under the Hilbert geometric hypotheses, let D be the scheme-theoretic relative toroidal boundary and I_D its ideal, locally (x₁⋯x_r). For s∈Γ(M̄_Σ,R,ω^κ), membership in the image of Γ(M̄_Σ,R,ω^κ⊗I_D) is equivalent to vanishing of its constant Fourier coefficient at every cusp component, after the stated étale coefficient and line-trivializing covers. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Section 2 boundary coordinates, author copy printed pp. 529–530; equation (5) and Theorem 8.3, printed pp. 546–547.)

Use the actual Hilbert moduli and toroidal model supplied by H1–H4, C4 and the C6 formal-gluing targets, not an arbitrary scheme record with the conclusions as fields. F is totally real of degree greater than one; n is prime to the discriminant and divides neither (2) nor (3); c is prime to n. Use a regular admissible cusp fan, finite modulo its unit group, and a Noetherian algebra R over o′[1/Δ], Δ=N(d n), containing the weight values and the required square roots for polarization descent. Adjoin the finite cyclotomic cusp coefficients by an étale cover when needed. ω^κ is the actual descended invariant-differential line; no general automorphic-bundle construction is duplicated here.

This is the scalar Hilbert coefficient comparison on its zero-dimensional cusp bases. It is not the generic B5 theorem for higher-dimensional boundary coefficients, and it does not import B3 or B5 back into their C6 supplier.

The Noetherian case is followed by the same filtered-colimit argument as arithmetic-koecher; it is not a pointwise or reduced-base test.

Use target name `hilbert_cuspidal_iff_constant_zero` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Hilbert/Koecher`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

Use arithmetic-koecher to identify open forms with regular extended sections, and hilbert-cusp-positive-support for their expansions.

For ξ∈X_+, positive-exponents-on-charts makes every boundary coordinate exponent a positive integer. Hence each nonconstant monomial is divisible by t=x₁⋯x_r. Division by t preserves the requested completed-ring support condition.

The constant coefficient survives modulo (t). Thus an expansion is in (t) exactly when its constant coefficient is zero. This is a scheme-theoretic coefficient computation, valid with nilpotents.

Use the invertible-line boundary exact sequence, étale descent of its ideal and coherent formal detection at every cusp to obtain the asserted global equivalence.

**Checks.**

- A section with nonzero constant term may satisfy Koecher extension but is not cuspidal.
- On a rank-one formal boundary chart the condition reduces to divisibility by q.
- Use every cusp component; a single scalar at one cusp does not replace all boundary restrictions.
- For a general Shimura variety a boundary coefficient can itself be a nonconstant form; that case remains owned by AutomorphicBundles:B5.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-cusp-lattice-comparison"></a>
### C6.13 Hilbert cusp lattice and trace pairing

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/cusp-lattice-comparison -->

For an H1 (R,n)-cusp with exact sequence 0→a*→L→b→0 and c=ab⁻¹, the level-image ideal b′⊃b gives character lattice X=cbb′=ab′. Its cocharacter lattice is X*, and its real pairing is Tr_F/Q. The unipotent stabilizer is X*, and the effective diagonal action is multiplication by u²ε. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) §3, Definition 3.2 and Proposition 3.3(iv), printed pp. 531–535.)

F is totally real of degree g; O its integers, d its different; f*=f⁻¹d⁻¹. The tame ideal n is coprime to Δ_F and does not divide 2 or 3, as in Dimitrov’s introduction; c is prime to n. B=Z[1/N(n)]. Use the actual torsion-free moduli input H1, not an unrestricted level.

Keep the chosen uniformization and finite cyclotomic action H_C separately from translations and diagonal units.

Use target name `hilbert_cusp_lattice_compare` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Hilbert/Compactification`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

Apply the cusp classification to the primitive level vector; its projection determines b′.

Compute translations preserving that vector as (cbb′)* by Proposition 3.3(iv); identify toric monomials through the trace pairing.

Transport, rather than redefine, the H1 lattice and H3 stabilizer.

**Checks.**

- For b′=b one obtains X=cb²; for b′≠b this lattice generally changes.
- The phase quotient uses (ab)*/(ab′)*, not the inverse quotient.

### C6.14 Admissible Hilbert cusp fans

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/admissible-fan-specialization -->

C0’s arithmetic fan at a cusp identifies with a complete rational polyhedral decomposition of the open positive cone in X*⊗R, together with zero, locally finite away from zero, stable under the effective cusp-unit action, with finitely many cone orbits and compatibility with cusp isomorphisms. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Definition 7.1 and proof of Theorem 7.2, printed pp. 541–542.)

F is totally real of degree g; O its integers, d its different; f*=f⁻¹d⁻¹. The tame ideal n is coprime to Δ_F and does not divide 2 or 3, as in Dimitrov’s introduction; c is prime to n. B=Z[1/N(n)]. Use the actual torsion-free moduli input H1, not an unrestricted level.

Use target name `hilbert_admissible_fan_compare` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Hilbert/Compactification`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

Use the lattice comparison and transport the signed positive cone.

Match the C0 arithmetic admissibility conditions with Definition 7.1; retain finite orbit data, not a finite fan in the whole cone.

**Checks.**

- In degree >1 finitely many unit orbits need not mean finitely many cones.
- A fan regular for (cb²)* need not be regular for (cbb′)* at a level-ramified cusp (Remark 7.3).

### C6.15 Integral uniformization exponents

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/trace-exponent-integral -->

For Z-submodules A,B of a number field K, nB⊆A, ξ∈B and x∈traceDual_Z/Q(A), there is an integer m with m=n Tr_K/Q(ξx). (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Proposition 4.1(ii), printed p. 537.)

n is a natural number; these are native Mathlib submodules, not newly defined cusp carriers.

Use target name `UniformizationPrototype.trace_exponent_integral` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Hilbert/Compactification`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

Pair x with nξ∈A using the existing trace-dual membership criterion.

Use Q-linearity of trace and commutativity to obtain the integer witness.

**Checks.**

- A=Z, B=(1/3)Z, n=3, ξ=1/3, x=1 gives m=1.
- Using n=2 for the same B fails to clear the denominator.

### C6.16 Congruence under a uniformization lift

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/trace-exponents-congruent -->

If ξ∈B and x′−x∈traceDual(B), integer witnesses m=nTr(ξx), m′=nTr(ξx′) differ by n times an integer. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Proposition 4.1(ii), printed p. 537.)

Subtract the two exponent identities in Q and use injectivity of Z→Q.

With the previous exponent witnesses and ζ∈R× satisfying ζ^n=1, ζ^m′=ζ^m. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Proposition 4.1(ii), printed p. 537.)

### C6.18 Additivity of uniformization phases

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/phase-additive-in-character -->

For integer witnesses mξ=nTr(ξx), mη=nTr(ηx), msum=nTr((ξ+η)x), ζ^msum=ζ^mξ ζ^mη. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Proposition 4.1(ii), printed p. 537.)

ζ is a unit of a commutative ring.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-uniformization-phase-character"></a>
### C6.19 Uniformization phase character

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/uniformization-phase-character -->

Given native Z-submodules A,B of K, nB⊆A, x∈traceDual(A) and ζ∈R× with ζ^n=1, construct the additive character B→Additive(R×) sending ξ to ζ^m where m=nTr(ξx). (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Proposition 4.1(ii) and equation (5), printed pp. 537, 546.)

K is a number field; R any commutative ring. All exponent witnesses are integral; no choice of a generator of the fractional ideal B.

For the geometric specialization use A=ab, B=ab′, n the exponent of b′/b.

Use target name `UniformizationPrototype.tracePhase` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Hilbert/Compactification`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

Obtain integral exponent witnesses by trace-exponent-integral.

Independence of the integer witness follows from Z→Q injectivity; phase-additive-in-character supplies the homomorphism laws.

The phase depends only on x modulo traceDual(B) by phase-independent-of-lift.

This supplies `Dimitrov Proposition 4.1(ii)`: Describes how changing the uniformization acts on toric monomials.

This supplies `Dimitrov equation (5) and C6/hilbert-cusp-positive-support`: Ensures cyclotomic coefficient multipliers are units, with phase one at the zero exponent.

This supplies `HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:H5 and OverconvergentAutomorphicForms:O6`: Keeps the q-expansion law compatible with coefficient specialization and ramified level cusps.

Expose the following API under the same hypotheses:

- `UniformizationPrototype.tracePhase_apply`: For ξ∈B and an integer witness m=nTr(ξx), evaluation equals ζ^m.
- `UniformizationPrototype.tracePhase_zero`: Evaluation at the zero character is one in R×.
- `UniformizationPrototype.tracePhase_add`: Evaluation at ξ+η equals the product of the evaluations at ξ and η.
- `UniformizationPrototype.tracePhase_lift`: Changing x by traceDual(B) leaves the character unchanged.
- `UniformizationPrototype.tracePhase_trivial_root`: For ζ=1 the character has every value one.
- `UniformizationPrototype.tracePhase_map`: A coefficient ring map transports the character by its induced map on units, without a primitivity hypothesis.

**Checks.**

- `UniformizationPrototype.phase_third_denominator`: For K=Q, A=Z, B=(1/3)Z, n=3 and x=1, evaluation at 1/3 is ζ (for any ζ³=1).
- `UniformizationPrototype.phase_zero_character`: For every datum the zero character evaluates to one, including the zero coefficient ring.
- `UniformizationPrototype.phase_dual_shift`: For K=Q, A=Z, B=(1/3)Z, x=1 and x′=4, the characters are equal when ζ³=1, since 3 belongs to traceDual(B).
- `UniformizationPrototype.phase_wrong_dual`: For A=Z, B=(1/4)Z, n=4, ζ=2 in (Z/5)×, x=0 and x′=1, the evaluations at 1/4 are 1 and 2; quotienting by traceDual(A) would be wrong.
- `UniformizationPrototype.phase_nonprimitive`: For any datum with n=4 over Z/8 and ζ=3, evaluation equals 3^m, although this root has order 2.
- Use A=ab and B=ab′ in the actual H1 chart; this character alone does not construct a cusp action.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/trace-exponent-integral](#shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-trace-exponent-integral); [ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/phase-independent-of-lift](#shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-phase-independent-of-lift); [ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/phase-additive-in-character](#shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-phase-additive-in-character).

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-uniformized-level-chart"></a>
### C6.20 The universal family on a Hilbert cusp chart

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/uniformized-level-chart -->

Over B[ζ_e], where e is the exponent of b′/b, the C4 Mumford family on the C0 toric chart carries the H1 c-polarization and µ_n level structure; on the open abelian locus it is the pullback of the universal fine HBAV. Changing uniformization x∈(ab)*/(ab′)* acts by q^ξ↦ζ_e^{eTr(ξx)}q^ξ. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Proposition 4.1(i)–(iii), printed pp. 536–537.)

F is totally real of degree g; O its integers, d its different; f*=f⁻¹d⁻¹. The tame ideal n is coprime to Δ_F and does not divide 2 or 3, as in Dimitrov’s introduction; c is prime to n. B=Z[1/N(n)]. Use the actual torsion-free moduli input H1, not an unrestricted level.

Use target name `hilbert_uniformized_level_chart` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Hilbert/Compactification`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

Import C4’s family and its level exact sequence; use the H1 chosen lift of the projected level data.

Identify the phase with tracePhase; verify faces and cusp-unit/cyclotomic changes by Proposition 4.1(iii).

**Checks.**

- At b′=b, e=1 and the cyclotomic phase is trivial.
- A ramified level cusp is a property of b′/b; it does not mean that the residue prime ramifies in F.

### C6.21 Fine Hilbert toroidal compactification

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/hilbert-toroidal-model -->

For an admissible cusp fan system Σ, there is a scheme Mbar¹_Σ over B, an open immersion M¹→Mbar¹_Σ and the prescribed completed cusp-chart identifications compatible with the universal HBAV. This pair is unique up to a unique isomorphism fixing the open and formal data. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Theorem 7.2(i), §§5–6, printed pp. 538–543.)

Apply the SF.3 arithmetic formal-gluing/effectivity input in the form of Dimitrov Proposition 5.11: the interior is schematically dense, boundary charts have the specified permitted generic points, and the relation gives exactly the intended identifications.

Verify the DVR and Artinian-thickening lifting conditions via R11.3 Raynaud uniformization and C4 Mumford effectivity. Use H2 at discriminant primes; no good-prime smoothness is used.

The same formal-gluing uniqueness theorem fixes the open/formal pair. The source leaves uniqueness implicit; its exact foundational proof is a recorded supplier obligation.

For Dimitrov’s torsion-free tame situation Mbar¹_Σ→Mbar_Σ remains finite étale. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Theorem 7.2(ii), author copy printed pp. 542–543; [BHW-2023](#bhw-2023) Proposition 8.4, printed p. 1766.)

### C6.30 Hilbert minimal contraction

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/hilbert-minimal-contraction -->

The determinant section ring Rλ=⊕_{k≥0}Γ(Mbar¹_Σ,λ^k) defines a surjective contraction π:Mbar¹_Σ→M^{1,min}=Proj_B Rλ. The minimal scheme is independent of Σ. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Theorem 8.6(ii) and proof, printed pp. 548–549.)

F is totally real of degree g; O its integers, d its different; f*=f⁻¹d⁻¹. The tame ideal n is coprime to Δ_F and does not divide 2 or 3, as in Dimitrov’s introduction; c is prime to n. B=Z[1/N(n)]. Use the actual torsion-free moduli input H1, not an unrestricted level.

For g>1 use arithmetic Koecher to identify the section ring independently of the fan; degree one is treated by the modular comparison.

Use target name `hilbert_minimal_contraction` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Hilbert/Compactification`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

Use the C6 determinant semiampleness and SF.3 proper coherent section-module/Proj construction on the actual normal Hilbert model over B. Define Rλ as the direct sum of its determinant-power global sections.

Identify a sufficiently divisible Veronese Proj with Proj Rλ, using the exact graded-algebra comparison; the finite-generation obligation is hilbert-minimal-finite-generation.

For two regular fan systems choose a common regular refinement. Its proper birational maps to the normal C6 models have pushforward of O equal to O by SF.0; C4 uniqueness identifies the pulled-back semiabelian families and determinant lines. The projection formula identifies every section-ring graded piece over B, proving independence on these fan systems.

Keep the Δ-inverted Koecher weight comparison separate from this integral determinant-ring argument. The normalization/Stein construction gives the contraction with its stated dense-open behavior.

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/hilbert-minimal-finite-generation -->

Rλ is a finitely generated B-algebra. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Theorem 8.6(iii) and proof, printed pp. 548–549.)

For each 0≤j<m, the proper coherent direct image of λ^j on Y gives a finitely generated section module for O_Y(1); use the SF.3 section-module finite-generation theorem over the Noetherian base.

Combine the m residue classes to make the whole graded section algebra finite over a finitely generated Veronese algebra. This proves finite type without relying on the invalid implication integral therefore finite.

The normal toroidal model and its schematically dense open give the birational normal contraction; SF.0 supplies the structure-sheaf pushforward and geometric connectedness with the exact Stein hypotheses.

### C6.33 Polarization quotients of minimal models

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/minimal-polarization-quotient -->

The H3 finite tame polarization action on the section ring induces an action on M^{1,min}; the quotient M^{min} is normal projective finite type over B, and the contraction descends. The action can stabilize minimal cusps, so the fine-to-coarse minimal map is not generally étale. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Theorem 8.6(iii) and proof, printed pp. 548–549.)

Transport the linearized determinant section ring under the actual finite H3 polarization action.

Apply the SF.1 finite quotient and SF.3 projective normalization theorems to the C6 model and contraction. The minimal action may have stabilizers; freeness of the toroidal action is not transferred to the minimal model.

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/hilbert-minimal-cusps -->

The contraction identifies M with a dense open of M^{min}. Its reduced boundary is the finite étale B-scheme ⨿_C Spec(B[ζ_e]^{H_C}), with e the exponent of b′/b; it has relative codimension g, hence at least two when g>1. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Theorem 8.6(iv), printed p. 548.)

F is totally real of degree g; O its integers, d its different; f*=f⁻¹d⁻¹. The tame ideal n is coprime to Δ_F and does not divide 2 or 3, as in Dimitrov’s introduction; c is prime to n. B=Z[1/N(n)]. Use the actual torsion-free moduli input H1, not an unrestricted level.

The cusp subscheme here has the source’s finite étale structure. Boundary ideals under coefficient base change use this scheme, not only its geometric points.

Use target name `hilbert_minimal_cusps` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Hilbert/Compactification`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

On the actual completed Hilbert toric boundary charts, positive exponents vanish along the product-boundary union and weight-zero unit-invariant coefficients reduce to constants.

Use the C6 normal contraction and proper formal functions to identify the finite contracted cusp subscheme over B.

Apply the H3 finite cyclotomic quotient to obtain the cusp coefficient rings; the cusp exponent is prime to the tame-inverted integer N(n), so these cyclotomic covers are etale over B. Verify the actual exponent-divisibility hypothesis rather than replacing it with an unrestricted level.

- A cusp component is not in general a B-rational geometric point.

### C6.35 Connected boundary fibres of Hilbert contraction

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/hilbert-minimal-boundary-fibres -->

Each minimal cusp has inverse image one connected toroidal boundary component. Every other geometric fibre of π is one point of the open Hilbert variety. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Theorem 8.6(v) and proof using [7] V.2.2, printed pp. 548–549.)

F is totally real of degree g; O its integers, d its different; f*=f⁻¹d⁻¹. The tame ideal n is coprime to Δ_F and does not divide 2 or 3, as in Dimitrov’s introduction; c is prime to n. B=Z[1/N(n)]. Use the actual torsion-free moduli input H1, not an unrestricted level.

Use target name `hilbert_minimal_boundary_fibres` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Hilbert/Compactification`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

Use SF.0 Stein connectedness for the C6 contraction; after geometric base change verify the source normality and the actual Stein comparison instead of assuming connectedness is universally automatic.

The determinant Hodge line has degree zero on curves in a contraction fibre. Apply A5’s Faltings–Chai V.2.2 isotriviality criterion to show constancy of the abelian part, with its proper-curve and semistable-family hypotheses.

At a Hilbert cusp the abelian part is zero. The admissible connected boundary fan and its effective unit quotient then form the connected toroidal fibre over the computed cyclotomic cusp. This is the geometry used in hilbert-boundary-global-functions.

**Checks.**

- Do not replace connected boundary components by separate fan cones.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-hilbert-minimal-weight-extension"></a>
### C6.37 Weights extending to the Hilbert minimal model

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/hilbert-minimal-weight-extension -->

For g>1 and integral weight κ over O′[1/Δ], the canonical weight line on M extends to an invertible sheaf on M^{min} if and only if κ is parallel. The pushforward π_*ωκ is coherent for every κ. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Theorem 8.6(vi) and proof, printed pp. 548–549.)

F is totally real of degree g; O its integers, d its different; f*=f⁻¹d⁻¹. The tame ideal n is coprime to Δ_F and does not divide 2 or 3, as in Dimitrov’s introduction; c is prime to n. B=Z[1/N(n)]. Use the actual torsion-free moduli input H1, not an unrestricted level.

This is the arithmetic weight-base statement of Theorem 8.6(vi); no assertion of the converse over an arbitrary special-fibre quotient of O′ is made.

Use target name `hilbert_minimal_weight_extension` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Hilbert/Compactification`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

Use SF.0 coherent proper pushforward and the normal/codimension-two extension comparison on the specified Δ-inverted weight base.

Use the H3 cusp-unit action on the trivialized line. For a nonparallel weight choose a maximal weight place and a finite-index contracting unit there; the weighted logarithmic sum is positive because another weight component is smaller. Thus the character is nontrivial.

For a parallel weight, use the H3 norm/polarization law and the descended C6 determinant Hodge line. At minimal stabilizers retain the exact power or Q-line descent condition.

**Checks.**

- Nonparallel weights can have reduced characters become trivial in a special coefficient ring; that does not prove invertible extension over the arithmetic weight base.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-hilbert-q-expansion-module-injective"></a>
### C6.39 Hilbert q-expansion detection with coefficient modules

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/hilbert-q-expansion-module-injective -->

On the fixed smooth geometrically connected component over the Δ-inverted weight base A₀ (a localized number ring containing the character and cyclotomic values), completion at the chosen uniformized cusp detects sections of ωκ⊗_{A₀}N for every A₀-module N. The target consists of the actual completed coefficient-line expansions, not a tensor product identified with formal series without proof. (Sources: [DIMITROV-TILOUINE-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-tilouine-author) §7, Definition 7.2 and Proposition 7.3 proof, printed pp. 585–586; [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Proposition 8.5(i), printed p. 547 (proof delegated to [6], §7).)

F is totally real of degree g; O its integers, d its different; f*=f⁻¹d⁻¹. The tame ideal n is coprime to Δ_F and does not divide 2 or 3, as in Dimitrov’s introduction; c is prime to n. B=Z[1/N(n)]. Use the actual torsion-free moduli input H1, not an unrestricted level.

No claim of injectivity at one cusp for a disjoint union of untested components.

Use the smooth Δ-inverted model, not a ramified-base singular local model. The line is invertible and the underlying model is flat over A₀.

Use target name `hilbert_qExpansion_module_injective` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Hilbert/Compactification`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

For finitely generated coefficients over the Dedekind base, reduce to a finite projective module and quotients by prime powers. Use geometric irreducibility of the smooth fibres and schematic density on each thickening to detect a section by the boundary completion. These generic density/completion facts are precise SF.0/F0 requests.

Write N as the directed union of its finitely generated submodules. The section descends to a submodule N₀ by the qcqs H0 colimit theorem. The invertible coefficient line a(κ) is flat over A₀, so N₀⊂N induces injective coefficient maps. Thus zero expansion over N already means zero expansion over N₀, where finite-module detection applies. This needs no interchange of a full power-series module with filtered colimits.

At a ramified cusp retain the cyclotomic cover and the unit-valued trace phase in the coefficient line; faithful cover descent reduces to the same detection statement.

**Checks.**

- Detection is for a fixed weight; the direct sum of all weight spaces is not injective after reduction (a Hasse form and 1 can have the same expansion).
- Torsion coefficient modules and infinitesimal thickenings are retained. Testing only reduced geometric points is insufficient.
- No assertion that an infinite product of coefficients commutes with filtered colimits is used.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/hilbert-q-expansion-comparison](#shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-hilbert-q-expansion-comparison); `HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:H1`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.0`; `AdicSpacesPartII:F0`.

No claim of injectivity at one cusp for a disjoint union of untested components.

Apply module-coefficient detection to N=R, with its A₀-module structure.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-hilbert-q-expansion-coefficient-descent"></a>
### C6.41 Coefficient descent from Hilbert q-expansions

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/hilbert-q-expansion-coefficient-descent -->

For an inclusion R⊂R′ of coefficient algebras and a fixed-weight section f′ over R′, if its full q-expansion at the chosen component cusp has coefficients in the image of the R-valued coefficient line, then f′ descends uniquely to an R-valued form. Flatness of R′ over R is not required. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Proposition 8.5(ii), printed p. 547 (proof delegated to [6], §7); [DIMITROV-TILOUINE-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-tilouine-author) §7, Definition 7.2 and Proposition 7.3 proof, printed pp. 585–586.)

F is totally real of degree g; O its integers, d its different; f*=f⁻¹d⁻¹. The tame ideal n is coprime to Δ_F and does not divide 2 or 3, as in Dimitrov’s introduction; c is prime to n. B=Z[1/N(n)]. Use the actual torsion-free moduli input H1, not an unrestricted level.

The coefficient map is injective. Use the smooth flat model and invertible line over the fixed weight base; a noninjective map requires a separate image formulation.

Use target name `hilbert_qExpansion_coefficient_descent` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Hilbert/Compactification`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

Apply the coefficient exact sequence 0→R→R′→R′/R→0 to the invertible line on the flat model. Global sections are left exact.

The image of f′ in the quotient-coefficient section module has zero expansion. Module-coefficient detection makes this image zero. Exactness gives existence over R; injectivity gives uniqueness.

**Checks.**

- An injective nonflat map is permitted: Z/4→(Z/4)[t]/(2t) supplies a coefficient test for the stronger statement.
- For a noninjective map R→0, unique recovery is false for nonzero R.
- The zero coefficient submodule recovers injectivity; the zero unital coefficient ring alone gives a vacuous specialization.

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/hilbert-boundary-ideal-pushforward -->

For g>1, on the stated normal Hilbert models π_*I_D=I_cusp, where D is the toroidal boundary and I_cusp is the ideal of the finite étale minimal cusp subscheme. For a parallel weight line Lmin, π_*(π*Lmin⊗I_D)=Lmin⊗I_cusp. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Theorem 8.6(iv),(v) and its proof, author copy printed pp. 548–549; Corollary 7.4, printed p. 544.)

F is totally real of degree g; O its integers, d its different; f*=f⁻¹d⁻¹. The tame ideal n is coprime to Δ_F and does not divide 2 or 3, as in Dimitrov’s introduction; c is prime to n. B=Z[1/N(n)]. Use the actual torsion-free moduli input H1, not an unrestricted level.

First over the source arithmetic base; any coefficient extension requires the requested coherent pushforward/base-change comparison, not an automatic equality of pulled-back ideals.

Use the C6 normal proper contraction theorem to obtain π_*O_Mbar=O_Mmin over B=Z[1/N(n)]. Let C be the finite étale minimal cusp subscheme and f:D→C the induced boundary map.

Use hilbert-boundary-etale-charts and C0: the scheme-theoretic toric boundary has a monomial quotient basis over its cusp coefficient ring, so it is flat and has geometrically reduced fibres. Descend along the actual cusp étale covers. Hilbert-minimal-boundary-fibres identifies the geometrically connected fibres over C; properness follows from the contraction.

Apply hilbert-boundary-global-functions to obtain O_C≅f_*O_D with its universal coherent base-change statement. This is an integral global-function argument.

Any coefficient extension uses the explicitly permitted coherent base changes; do not interchange a completed series ring with arbitrary tensor product.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-hilbert-ordinary-model-comparison"></a>
### C6.43 Hilbert models at arbitrary residue primes

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/hilbert-ordinary-model-comparison -->

For any prime p not dividing the tame level, including p=2 and p ramified in F, the completions of the H2 Deligne–Pappas model and its minimal/toroidal compactifications identify with the C6 models on their common moduli open and cusp charts. The ordinary locus and Hasse neighbourhood models are those of H2’s actual local model. (Sources: [BHW-2023](#bhw-2023) Sections 5.1.2–5.2, printed pp. 1744–1748; [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Theorems 7.2 and 8.6, author copy printed pp. 541–543, 548–549.)

F is totally real of degree g; O its integers, d its different; f*=f⁻¹d⁻¹. The tame ideal n is coprime to Δ_F and does not divide 2 or 3, as in Dimitrov’s introduction; c is prime to n. B=Z[1/N(n)]. Use the actual torsion-free moduli input H1, not an unrestricted level.

Use the H2 moduli condition at ramified primes and its ordinary/Rapoport locus, rather than C5’s good-prime smoothness. BHW uses N≥4, (N,p)=1.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-hilbert-hasse-boundary-comparison"></a>
### C6.44 Hilbert Hasse ideal on the boundary

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/hilbert-hasse-boundary-comparison -->

The H2 total Hasse ideal on the ordinary Hilbert model is the ideal obtained from the R07.2 determinant of Verschiebung on invariant differentials on its abelian locus, and from C4’s semiabelian extension on the C4 boundary charts. These agree on overlaps and are compatible with the O-action and changes of polarization. (Sources: [BHW-2023](#bhw-2023) BHW §5.2, Lemma 5.12 proof, pp. 1746–1748; §7.1, pp. 1759–1760.)

F is totally real of degree g; O its integers, d its different; f*=f⁻¹d⁻¹. The tame ideal n is coprime to Δ_F and does not divide 2 or 3, as in Dimitrov’s introduction; c is prime to n. B=Z[1/N(n)]. Use the actual torsion-free moduli input H1, not an unrestricted level.

The generic BT₁ determinant invariant, LF and BT₁ Hodge–Tate sequence have owner R07.2. C6 does not define them.

At the toric boundary use the semiabelian Verschiebung/differential construction, not an abelian BT₁ of fixed height 2g.

Use target name `hilbert_hasse_boundary_compare` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Hilbert/Compactification`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

Compare the abelian H2 determinant with imported R07.2 Ha.

Use C4’s semiabelian Verschiebung comparison along the C4 charts and the conormal identification; determinant functoriality glues the ideal.

Use the actual H2 ordinary local model for p=2 and ramified primes.

**Checks.**

- The determinant line is Z-rank g; partial Hasse factors after splitting are not the definition of a new invariant.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-hilbert-boundary-ordinary"></a>
### C6.45 The Hilbert boundary is ordinary

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/hilbert-boundary-ordinary -->

The Hasse ideal is the unit ideal on the formal toric boundary. Consequently every cusp lies in the ε=0 ordinary neighbourhood, for every permitted prime including p=2 and discriminant primes. (Sources: [BHW-2023](#bhw-2023) BHW Lemma 5.12 proof, printed p. 1748.)

F is totally real of degree g; O its integers, d its different; f*=f⁻¹d⁻¹. The tame ideal n is coprime to Δ_F and does not divide 2 or 3, as in Dimitrov’s introduction; c is prime to n. B=Z[1/N(n)]. Use the actual torsion-free moduli input H1, not an unrestricted level.

Use target name `hilbert_boundary_ordinary` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Hilbert/Compactification`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

On a split torus, Verschiebung is the identity after the Frobenius factorization of [p]; its differential determinant is a unit.

Descend this unit-ideal statement from the splitting/cyclotomic chart cover to the actual boundary.

**Checks.**

- The boundary lies in the Rapoport locus even though the whole special fibre need not be smooth.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/hilbert-hasse-boundary-comparison](#shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-hilbert-hasse-boundary-comparison); [ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/hilbert-semiabelian-extension](#shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-hilbert-semiabelian-extension); [ShimuraCompactifications:C4/roadmap/semiabelian-hasse-boundary-comparison](#shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-semiabelian-hasse-boundary-comparison); `HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:H2`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.1`.

### C6.46 Hasse neighbourhood model comparison

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/hilbert-near-ordinary-model -->

For 0≤ε<1 with |p|^ε in |L×|, the H2 admissible normalized blowup model of |Ha|≥|p|^ε on the compactified Hilbert model has generic fibre exactly BHW’s X*(ε). Locally its blowup chart before normalization is the p-torsion-free quotient of R⟨T⟩/(T Ha_lift−p^ε). It contains the entire boundary. (Sources: [BHW-2023](#bhw-2023) BHW §5.2, pp. 1746–1748; elliptic convention §2.1, p. 1721.)

F is totally real of degree g; O its integers, d its different; f*=f⁻¹d⁻¹. The tame ideal n is coprime to Δ_F and does not divide 2 or 3, as in Dimitrov’s introduction; c is prime to n. B=Z[1/N(n)]. Use the actual torsion-free moduli input H1, not an unrestricted level.

Choose an integral element of valuation ε in a coefficient extension, as in H2; normalization and p-torsion removal are part of the supplied model.

Use target name `hilbert_nearOrdinary_model_compare` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Hilbert/Compactification`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

Compare the H2 ideal construction with the local lift equation.

If Ha′−Ha∈pR and ε<1, the ultrametric inequality makes the two valuation conditions equivalent.

Use boundary-ordinary and formal/adic generic-fibre comparison.

**Checks.**

- At ε=1, Ha=0 and Ha′=p need not define the same condition.
- This node does not prove any canonical-subgroup radius; T3 owns those estimates.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-hilbert-ordinary-polarization-quotient"></a>
### C6.47 Polarization quotients of ordinary neighbourhoods

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/hilbert-ordinary-polarization-quotient -->

For tame Δ(N), the Hasse inequality is invariant under changing the chosen polarization, and the G ordinary-neighbourhood model is the effective quotient of the G* model. For Γ0(p^n) level, the diagram over the corresponding unlevelled opens is Cartesian and is a finite étale Δ(N)-torsor on the adic moduli locus. (Sources: [BHW-2023](#bhw-2023) BHW Proposition 8.4 and Lemma 8.5, pp. 1766–1767.)

F is totally real of degree g; O its integers, d its different; f*=f⁻¹d⁻¹. The tame ideal n is coprime to Δ_F and does not divide 2 or 3, as in Dimitrov’s introduction; c is prime to n. B=Z[1/N(n)]. Use the actual torsion-free moduli input H1, not an unrestricted level.

For compactified formal charts use the actual toroidal/minimal action above; no étale torsor assertion is added at minimal cusps.

Use target name `hilbert_ordinary_polarization_quotient` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Hilbert/Compactification`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

The underlying O-abelian scheme, hence Hasse ideal, is unchanged by the polarization action.

Use H4’s relative Γ0 moduli problem and H3’s tame quotient.

The natural subgroup is O-stable, giving the Cartesian property of Lemma 8.5; compare formal charts by equivariance.

**Checks.**

- For full G* p-level, the positive-unit action need not preserve the Weil-pairing constraint; the mixed full-level model is needed.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/toroidal-polarization-quotient](#shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-toroidal-polarization-quotient); [ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/minimal-polarization-quotient](#shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-minimal-polarization-quotient); [ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/hilbert-near-ordinary-model](#shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-hilbert-near-ordinary-model); `HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:H3`; `HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:H4`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.1`.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-hilbert-p-level-boundary-comparison"></a>
### C6.48 Finite-level Hilbert boundary maps

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/hilbert-p-level-boundary-comparison -->

The forgetful and finite effective H4 level/polarization maps extend between the canonical Hilbert generic-fibre minimal compactifications (or the specified finite normalizations). For toroidal compactifications they extend after choosing fans compatible with the induced cusp lattice maps; a finite toroidal extension uses the pullback fan/finite normalization, while further refinements may give proper nonfinite maps. These maps agree on completed cusp data, including lattice inclusions, root actions and determinant pairing. The full-level comparison passes through H4’s mixed G-level problem; it is not the quotient of X_{Γ*(p^n)} by all positive units. (Sources: [BHW-2023](#bhw-2023) BHW §5.1.1–5.1.2 and §8.2–8.4, pp. 1742–1745, 1768–1779.)

F is totally real of degree g; O its integers, d its different; f*=f⁻¹d⁻¹. The tame ideal n is coprime to Δ_F and does not divide 2 or 3, as in Dimitrov’s introduction; c is prime to n. B=Z[1/N(n)]. Use the actual torsion-free moduli input H1, not an unrestricted level.

Integral models at wild p-level are only those explicitly supplied by H2/H4 with the stated SF.3 normalization and formal-gluing comparison; the BHW generic-fibre tower alone supplies no all-level smooth integral model.

For every toroidal map choose source and target admissible fans compatible under the actual cusp lattice map, using C0/C3 equivariant refinements. A finiteness assertion requires the source to be the finite normalization of the target, equivalently the pullback fan in the finite toric lattice case; compatibility alone does not imply finiteness.

Use target name `hilbert_pLevel_boundary_compare` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Hilbert/Compactification`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

Use H4’s actual finite-level action (precomposition by γ∨=det(γ)γ⁻¹) and effective centers.

Use the specified H2/H4 integral model and SF.3 finite-normalization/minimal-extension comparison for finite open maps, verifying normality, schematic density and permitted base change. The BHW generic tower does not itself identify these integral models.

For toroidal maps use C0’s lattice-map criterion and C3’s compatible equivariant refinements, with C4’s period data. Identify the finite normalization with the pullback fan when claiming finiteness; additional subdivisions only supply proper comparison maps.

Use H3’s phase compatibility to identify the formal boundary maps, and schematic density to compare them with the extended open maps.

**Checks.**

- The map from full G* to full G level is not generally surjective (BHW §8.2).
- Finite effective groups at fixed n are not the profinite group Δ(p∞N).
- Unrelated fixed fans need not admit the level map. A proper subdivision of a compatible fan can give a nonfinite toroidal map, even though the open level map is finite.

### C6.49 Natural integral Hilbert differential lattice

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/hilbert-integral-differential-interface -->

The natural integral conormal lattice on the C6 ordinary toroidal model pulls back on H4 finite levels to the ω⁺ used in BHW §7.1. This comparison supplies the boundary family and natural lattice to T3–T5; the modified inverse-image lattice ω^int, its local freeness and estimates remain T5’s own constructions. (Sources: [BHW-2023](#bhw-2023) BHW §7.1, Definition 7.3 and Proposition 7.4, pp. 1759–1760.)

F is totally real of degree g; O its integers, d its different; f*=f⁻¹d⁻¹. The tame ideal n is coprime to Δ_F and does not divide 2 or 3, as in Dimitrov’s introduction; c is prime to n. B=Z[1/N(n)]. Use the actual torsion-free moduli input H1, not an unrestricted level.

Use O⊗O⁺-rank one only on the stated Rapoport locus; p may ramify in F.

Use target name `hilbert_integral_differential_compare` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Hilbert/Compactification`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

Take the conormal of the actual formal semiabelian family and apply the requested formal/generic-fibre sheaf comparison.

Use the moduli compatibility of finite level pullback and the ordinary-model comparison.

**Checks.**

- At ramified primes the natural lattice can differ from T5’s ω^int.
- No canonical subgroup or Igusa torsor is constructed here.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-modular-toroidal-minimal-comparison"></a>
### C6.50 Degree-one compactification comparison

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/modular-toroidal-minimal-comparison -->

For F=Q, after identifying principal polarizations and the same tame and finite p-level moduli data and bases, the generic-fibre normal Hilbert toroidal and minimal curves are canonically isomorphic to the compactified coarse modular curves of R13.4a. The isomorphism restricts to the moduli identification on the open and identifies the cusp subschemes. (Sources: [MODULAR-CURVES-II](#modular-curves-ii) R13.4a; unpaginated roadmap layers and reviewed PR81 link contract; [BHW-2023](#bhw-2023) Section 2.1, printed pp. 1720–1721.)

Work in characteristic zero, component by component, with the precise full/fixed-pairing/Γ1/Γ0 level and coefficient field supplied by R13.4a.

For full level retain the Weil-pairing/cyclotomic component field; do not collapse it to the tame Γ1 curve.

Use target name `modular_toroidal_minimal_compare` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/ModularComparison`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

The H1 HBAV moduli problem at F=Q is the elliptic one with the specified level.

Use the R13.4a normal proper coarse construction. Normal proper curve completions of the common dense open are uniquely isomorphic.

A proper birational contraction of normal curves is an isomorphism, so toroidal and minimal generic curves coincide.

**Checks.**

- g=1 does not satisfy arithmetic Koecher; a pole at a cusp can exist.
- Composite and full levels use R13.4a.

*Needs:* `HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:H1`; `HilbertModularVarietiesAndShimuraCurves:H4`; [ShimuraCompactifications:C4/roadmap/tate-degeneration-comparison](#shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-tate-degeneration-comparison); `ModularCurvesPartII:R13.4a`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.0`; `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.3`.

Attribution: The elliptic compactification and Hasse-neighbourhood convention. The algebraic/formal comparison with the Hilbert specialization still uses the separately requested modular-curve contracts.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-modular-formal-cusp-comparison"></a>
### C6.51 Modular cusp parameters and analytification

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/modular-formal-cusp-comparison -->

Under the degree-one comparison, the C4 Tate/Mumford family, cusp fields and the formal q parameter agree with R13.4a/R13.4b. For a cusp of width w, j=q_Tate⁻¹+744+… and q_Tate=t^w in its level cusp parameter t. Analytification and the permitted level/degeneracy correspondences commute with this identification. (Sources: [MODULAR-CURVES-II](#modular-curves-ii) R13.4a and R13.4b; unpaginated roadmap layers and reviewed PR81 link contract; [BHW-2023](#bhw-2023) Section 2.1, printed pp. 1720–1721.)

Use R13.4b’s component and correspondence descent hypotheses; at stack level keep inertia, and at coarse level keep cusp width.

Generic fibre and completed cusp comparisons use the formal/adic comparison F0/R2/R3.

Use target name `modular_formal_cusp_compare` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/ModularComparison`, namespace `TauCeti.HilbertCusp`.

Identify the universal Tate object on the common punctured cusp using C4’s uniqueness.

Transport the character inclusion at the cusp; its index is the width, giving q_Tate=t^w.

Use R13.4b’s analytic and correspondence comparison rather than proving analytic uniformization here.

**Checks.**

- At width w>1 the coarse parameter t is not the Tate q itself.
- A coarse-space map is not a substitute for a stack correspondence.

<a id="shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-hilbert-boundary-global-functions"></a>
### C6.53 Integral global functions on a Hilbert boundary fibre

<!-- Contract: ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/hilbert-boundary-global-functions -->

For the fine Hilbert toroidal contraction over B=Z[1/N(n)], let C be its finite etale minimal cusp scheme and D its scheme-theoretic toroidal boundary. The induced f:D→C is proper, flat and finitely presented with geometrically reduced and geometrically connected fibres, and O_C→f_*O_D is an isomorphism compatible with arbitrary base change on C. The assertion is integral and includes coefficient bases with nilpotents. (Sources: [DIMITROV-AUTHOR](#dimitrov-author) Corollary 7.4, printed p. 544; Theorem 8.6(iv)–(v), printed pp. 548–549.)

Use the tame-level hypotheses of the fine Hilbert model, degree g>1, its admissible regular boundary fans and the actual etale cusp-chart presentation.

The scheme-theoretic boundary is the product monomial ideal in its regular toric charts. Flatness is verified over the finite etale cusp coefficient base, not inferred from connectedness alone.

Use the actual geometrically connected boundary fibre description over each geometric cusp from the Hilbert contraction theorem; a generic-fibre connectedness statement alone is insufficient.

Use target name `hilbert_boundary_global_functions` in `TauCeti/Geometry/Shimura/Compactification/C6`, namespace `TauCeti.ShimuraCompactifications`.

Use the completed cusp chart and its etale algebraic realization. C0 gives the monomial quotient basis, hence flatness and geometric reducedness; faithfully flat completion detects these properties in the Noetherian local charts.

The minimal-cusp and boundary-fibre theorems identify C and the geometrically connected fibres. Properness and finite presentation follow from the toroidal contraction and its closed boundary.

Apply the requested SF.0 proper-flat global-function/cohomology-and-base-change theorem: a proper flat finitely presented family with geometrically reduced connected fibres has H⁰ equal to its base, universally.

Use this isomorphism with the contraction Stein equality in the boundary ideal pushforward theorem.

**Checks.**

- Over a field a connected reduced proper boundary fibre has only scalar global functions.
- A coefficient base Z/4 retains its nilpotent scalar 2 in f_*O_D.
- Geometric connectedness without reducedness is insufficient: a proper infinitesimal thickening of a point can have extra global functions.

*Needs:* [ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/hilbert-boundary-etale-charts](#shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-hilbert-boundary-etale-charts); [ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/hilbert-minimal-cusps](#shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-hilbert-minimal-cusps); [ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/hilbert-minimal-boundary-fibres](#shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-hilbert-minimal-boundary-fibres); [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/regular-boundary-union](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-regular-boundary-union); `SchemeAndStackFoundations:SF.0`; `AdicSpacesPartII:F0`.

Attribution: The source provides the etale chart, cusp and connected-fibre geometry. The universal integral global-function conclusion additionally uses the requested foundational theorem; it is a deduction, not a separately numbered claim in the source.

### Examples

- For F=Q every unit square is 1, so a negative exponent can have a bounded unit orbit. This rejects the degree-one version of Hilbert Koecher.
- For coefficients Z/4 and unit character value 3, the nonzero coefficient 2 satisfies 3·2=2. Nontrivial character alone does not force its constant term to vanish.
- At ξ=0 no real embedding is negative. The negative-trace contradiction explicitly assumes ξ≠0.
- Let N_j=Z^j and N=⊕ᵢZ. A series whose ith coefficient is the ith basis vector belongs to N[[q]] but to no N_j[[q]]. Detection uses injective coefficient maps, not surjectivity of colim N_j[[q]]→N[[q]].
- For g>1 the regular toroidal boundary over its finite étale cusp base has universally constant global functions. Together with the contraction Stein equality, this identifies the pushforward cusp ideal over B, retaining nilpotents.

The examples retain a killed positive monomial, a surviving nilpotent coefficient, a surviving negative Laurent character, the degree-one unit-orbit obstruction, the zero-exponent exclusion and a torsion constant term fixed by a nontrivial character. They distinguish several plausible incorrect formulations. The many intermediate proof steps in this reader are not a proposed Lean auxiliary-lemma development.

Each request states the missing interface and its consumers. A stage citation is not a claim that the supplier already contains the requested theorem. Generic constructions stay with their owners; the arithmetic or boundary instance stays in this roadmap.

### S4. AbelianSchemesAndArithmeticModuli:A5

Poincare/cubical biextensions, polarized complex-torus algebraization and relative line-bundle identities, plus the theta-generation input for the Hodge semiampleness theorem. These generic abelian constructions are imported rather than duplicated. The Hilbert instance needs determinant Hodge semiampleness for a polarized semiabelian family over a normal excellent Noetherian base without discriminant inversion, and the proper-curve zero-Hodge-degree isotriviality criterion of Faltings–Chai V.2.2. Retain the exact auxiliary-level and descent hypotheses. The general generation theorem must retain the boundary family hypotheses that make it valid (normal excellent Noetherian base, generically abelian polarized semiabelian family), with the exact compatible auxiliary level/finite descent; no theorem for an arbitrary line on an arbitrary family is assumed.

Consumed by [ShimuraCompactifications:C1/roadmap/boundary-torsor-tower](#shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-boundary-torsor-tower); [ShimuraCompactifications:C4/roadmap/poincare-extension-classification](#shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-poincare-extension-classification); [ShimuraCompactifications:C4/roadmap/polarized-degeneration-data](#shimuracompactifications-c4-roadmap-polarized-degeneration-data); [ShimuraCompactifications:C5/roadmap/hodge-semiampleness](#shimuracompactifications-c5-roadmap-hodge-semiampleness); [ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/hilbert-hodge-semiampleness](#shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-hilbert-hodge-semiampleness); [ShimuraCompactifications:C6/roadmap/hilbert-minimal-boundary-fibres](#shimuracompactifications-c6-roadmap-hilbert-minimal-boundary-fibres).

## Additional proof and interface requirements

The following proof and interface requirements retain their own hypotheses and source limitations. They are needed before the geometric constructions and comparison theorems can be implemented.

### G1. Analytic carrier and nonreduced chart extension

Use ComplexComparisonPartII:C0/repair-analytification, which is a planned repair, not an existing analytic-space implementation. Require the nilpotent-preserving local analytic-space carrier, morphisms, gluing/products and actual scheme analytification. Its current infrastructure/PR196 integration remains unfinished. Extend regular finite anchor charts to singular/nonreduced charts through that same carrier.

Affects [ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/partial-boundary-charts](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-partial-boundary-charts); [ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/arithmetic-gluing](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-arithmetic-gluing); [ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/projective-algebraization](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-projective-algebraization).

### G2. Arithmetic reduction and controlled quotient neighbourhoods

Pink 6.19 and 6.22 use AMRT reduction/controlled Satake-neighbourhood input. Those proof passages were not read; AA.3/V2 must supply finite cusp indexing, effective stabilizer control and the stated separated quotient-neighbourhood theorem. Finite cone orbits alone do not prove separatedness or finiteness of stabilizers.

Affects [ShimuraCompactifications:C1/roadmap/arithmetic-stabilizer](#shimuracompactifications-c1-roadmap-arithmetic-stabilizer); [ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/quotient-separation](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-quotient-separation); [ShimuraCompactifications:C0/roadmap/smooth-projective-refinement](#shimuracompactifications-c0-roadmap-smooth-projective-refinement).

### G3. Special mixed canonical boundary models

Pink 12.13–12.17 constructing special mixed boundary canonical models and the special-point descent input were not read. The pure V8 model does not supply these models by itself. Request the torus/abelian-torsor boundary subclass as the explicit descent supplier; the C2/C2.general and C3.general outputs remain conditional on it.

Affects [ShimuraCompactifications:C2/roadmap/canonical-toroidal-model](#shimuracompactifications-c2-roadmap-canonical-toroidal-model); [ShimuraCompactifications:C2.general/roadmap/general-toroidal-descent](#shimuracompactifications-c2-general-roadmap-general-toroidal-descent); [ShimuraCompactifications:C3.general/roadmap/general-map-descent](#shimuracompactifications-c3-general-roadmap-general-map-descent).

### G4. Universal toric subdivision cohomology

Supply the integral monomial Cech/contractibility proof for structure sheaves and strictly boundary-positive degree ideals, including arbitrary coefficient base change and the derived boundary-ideal version. Lan 2017 Proposition 7.5 reduces to KKMS Corollary 2 p. 44, which was not read. A real-cone contractibility observation without the coefficient complex is insufficient.

Affects [ShimuraCompactifications:C3/roadmap/toric-structure-sheaf-vanishing](#shimuracompactifications-c3-roadmap-toric-structure-sheaf-vanishing); [ShimuraCompactifications:C3/roadmap/refinement-higher-structure-sheaf](#shimuracompactifications-c3-roadmap-refinement-higher-structure-sheaf); [ShimuraCompactifications:C3/roadmap/refinement-boundary-ideal](#shimuracompactifications-c3-roadmap-refinement-boundary-ideal).

### G5. Relative effectivity construction proof leaves

## Bibliography and reading provenance

Sources are cited at the target level; no source is reproduced or summarized section by section. The bibliography identifies the editions and reading scopes behind the target locators. Public-copy hashes identify the inspected binary; they do not assert a reading of every page. Printed pagination is used unless explicitly identified as physical PDF pagination; web tags and roadmap layers are unpaginated.
